
# The Stardust Engine - Master Discography & Lore
Compiled on: 10-26-2026 09:01:12 PM

# Electric Color (1987)
* **Narrative Era:** 1987
* **Real-World DSP Release:** 2026-03-29

## Electric Color
### Lore
The solid **#2 hit**. It sat at the runner-up spot for six weeks, famously blocked from #1 by a certain "novelty song" that swept the summer of '87.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
Woke up this morning, the world was gray
Didn't know the colors had all faded away
Been living in a room with the curtains drawn tight
Then you flipped a switch and turned on the light

(Chorus)
Oh, I'm seeing in electric color
Finding a rhythm I never knew
Dancing in a brand new summer
My whole world feels bright and new
It's an electric-colored feeling
And it all started with you!

(Verse 2)
The city sounds were just a buzz in my ear
A static station I could never quite hear
But now the noise is turning into a song
And I finally feel like I belong

(Chorus)
Oh, I'm seeing in electric color
Finding a rhythm I never knew
Dancing in a brand new summer
My whole world feels bright and new
It's an electric-colored feeling
And it all started with you!

(Bridge)
The beat is getting stronger
I can't hide it any longer
My heart is playing catch-up
To the music turning way up!

(Guitar Solo)

(Chorus)
Oh, I'm seeing in electric color
Finding a rhythm I never knew
Dancing in a brand new summer
My whole world feels bright and new
It's an electric-colored feeling
And it all started with you!

(Outro)
Electric color
Feeling so new
It's all because of you
## Plastic Sky
### Lore
Ryan's first act of "malicious compliance." He hid angsty, anti-corporate lyrics about "living under a plastic sky" inside a polished, studio-approved pop-rock wrapper. It is a direct protest against the "thin" sound the studio demanded—Ryan sings explicitly about his guitar being "clean" and the drums being "thin" while the executives nodded along, missing the insult completely.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
The camera's on, the smile is bright
Another take in the burning light
They painted the set with a perfect blue
But it doesn't feel real, it doesn't feel true

(Chorus)
We're living under a plastic sky (Oh-oh-oh)
They tell me "jump," I ask "how high?"
(Oh-oh-oh)
It's just a set, it's all a lie
Living under a plastic sky

(Verse 2)
My guitar is clean, the drums are thin
They polished away all the grit and sin
I want to scream, I want to roar
But they just ask me to sing some more

(Chorus)
We're living under a plastic sky (Oh-oh-oh)
They tell me "jump," I ask "how high?"
(Oh-oh-oh)
It's just a set, it's all a lie
Living under a plastic sky

(Bridge)
Is this the dream we were chasing?
A different part, a different face
I'm losing me, I'm losing you
In all this shiny, plastic blue

(Guitar Solo)

(Chorus)
We're living under a plastic sky (Oh-oh-oh)
They tell me "jump," I ask "how high?"
(Oh-oh-oh)
It's just a set, it's all a lie
Living under a plastic sky

(Outro)
Plastic sky...
Feeling so new...
(fades out)
## My Anchor Holds
### Lore
A power ballad the studio marketed as a romance, but which the band wrote about their family bond. This "sibling-love-disguised-as-romance" became a staple of their early sound.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
The lights are bright, but the room is cold
Another story that's bought and sold
I'm trying to find a reason why
Beneath this plastic, empty sky

(Chorus)
But when the world is spinning out of my control
You're the one true thing that I can hold
Through the static and the driving rain
You're the one who keeps me sane
You are the shelter, you are the truth
My anchor in this crazy youth

(Verse 2)
They pull us under, they push us high
They build us up just to watch us die
It's hard to know who you can trust
When all the promises turn to dust

(Chorus)
But when the world is spinning out of my control
You're the one true thing that I can hold
Through the static and the driving rain
You're the one who keeps me sane
You are the shelter, you are the truth
My anchor in this crazy youth

(Guitar Solo)

(Bridge)
So let them talk, let them stare
As long as you are standing there
I'm not afraid of what's to come
I know where I belong...

(Chorus)
'Cause when the world is spinning out of my control
You're the one true thing that I can hold
Through the static and the driving rain
You're the one who keeps me sane
You are the shelter, you are the truth
My anchor in this crazy youth

(Outro)
My anchor...
My anchor holds...
(Fades out with piano and synth)
## Dancing in the Neon
### Lore
"I wanna see you dancing to this one, Chicago!" A crowd-pleaser that fit the label's "pop" image perfectly. This was one of the "safe" synth-pop singles Apex Records pushed for, prioritizing high energy and polished production over the band's rock roots.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
The city's quiet, the sun is down
We're in the glow of the other side of town
The beat is calling from a place I know
Just follow the sound, and let it go

(Chorus)
We're dancing in the neon (Oh-oh, oh-oh)
We're moving 'til the night is gone (Oh-oh, oh-oh)
Forget about tomorrow
We're shining like a light
Dancing in the neon tonight!

(Verse 2)
The rhythm's heavy, a flashing bright
Reflected colors in your eyes
I don't care what the real world wants
This moment's all that I could ever want

(Chorus)
We're dancing in the neon (Oh-oh, oh-oh)
We're moving 'til the night is gone (Oh-oh, oh-oh)
Forget about tomorrow
We're shining like a light
Dancing in the neon tonight!

(Bridge)
Don't stop, don't stop the feeling
This music's got me reeling
Don't stop, don't stop the beat
(Synth-pop breakdown / keyboard solo)

(Chorus)
We're dancing in the neon (Oh-oh, oh-oh)
We're moving 'til the night is gone (Oh-oh, oh-oh)
Forget about tomorrow
We're shining like a light
Dancing in the neon tonight!

(Outro)
Dancing in the neon... (Oh-oh, oh-oh)
'Til the night is gone...
(fades)
## Running on the Edge
### Lore
Another protest song. Ryan sings about being a "shadow in the light" and having his guitar suppressed ("This keyboard hook is all a bore / I know this guitar's made for more"). It features angsty, anti-corporate lyrics hidden inside a "safe" pop-rock wrapper.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
Another day, another show
They tell me which way the wind should blow
I'm smiling 'til my face is numb
Waiting for the day to come

A rumble starting underground
This pretty world is gonna break
How much more can I take?

(Chorus)
I'm running on the edge, about to lose control (Oh-oh-oh)
Trying to save my rock and roll soul
I'm running on the edge, a shadow in the light (Oh-oh-oh)
Gonna break out into the night!

(Verse 2)
This keyboard hook is all a bore
I know this guitar's made for more
I want to feel the fire and grit

I can hear a different sound
A rumble starting underground
This pretty world is gonna break
How much more can I take?

(Chorus)
I'm running on the edge, about to lose control (Oh-oh-oh)
Trying to save my rock and roll soul
I'm running on the edge, a shadow in the light (Oh-oh-oh)
Gonna break out into the night!

(Guitar Solo)

(Chorus)
I'm running on the edge, about to lose control (Oh-oh-oh)
Trying to save my rock and roll soul
I'm running on the edge, a shadow in the light (Oh-oh-oh)
Gonna break out into the night!

(Outro)
Running on the edge...
Running on the edge...
(fades)

## Just a Girl
### Lore
Cassidy's quiet rebellion. Apex executives dismissed this moody, introspective track as "filler," but it became a grassroots anthem for female fans. The honest lyrics about her treatment in the industry ("They tell me 'sing the high note' / They tell me 'wear the red dress'") resonated deeply with young women who felt similarly boxed in by expectations.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
They tell me "sing the high note"
They tell me "wear the red dress"
They never ask me what I wrote
They only want to see the press

(Verse 2)
I bring my chords, my melody
They pat my head and disagree
They turn to him, "now *that's* a sound"
And I'm just standing in the background

(Chorus)
Am I ever gonna break through?
Am I ever gonna get my due?
Or am I just a girl in the band?
A pretty picture, holding your hand

(Verse 3)
They say "be 'Stardust', be the grace"
"Know your role and keep your place"
But I am more than what you see
I'm fighting just to be... me

(Chorus)
Am I ever gonna break through?
Am I ever gonna get my due?
Or am I just a girl in the band?
A pretty picture, holding your hand

(Bridge)
But the music's in my blood
The synth, the key, the flood
You can't erase the fire
My voice is getting higher

(Chorus)
Am I ever gonna break through?
Am I ever gonna get my due?
Or am I just a girl in the band?
A pretty picture, holding your hand

(Outro)
Just a girl...
Just a girl...
(fades out on a shimmering synth pad)
## Light Up the Night
### Lore
The song Apex Records poured *all* of their money into. They were convinced this polished synth-pop track would be "The Big One." It wasn't. Despite the massive marketing campaign, the "safe" sound failed to connect with audiences in the way the band's authentic rock tracks did.

We're gonna light up the night!

Chasing the stars 'til the morning light

(Keyboard Solo)

(Chorus)
We're gonna light up the night! (Oh-oh, oh-oh)
Chasing the stars 'til the morning light

## Break the Walls
### Lore
The one "pure rock" anthem Apex allowed on the album. It opened Side B and became an immediate fan-favorite for its raw energy. While the studio pushed "Light Up the Night," the fans embraced this track as the true spirit of the band.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
Been counting the days in this neon cage
Smiling for the cameras on a plastic stage
They turned the synths up, drowned the sound
But you can't keep a good thing down

(Chorus)
We're gonna break the walls tonight! (Yeah!)
Turn up the volume, hit the light!
No more whispers, time to shout
The Engine's roaring, let it out!

(Verse 2)
I'm tired of the glitter, tired of the gloss
Tired of the suits who think they're the boss
This guitar is loaded, it's ready to scream
Waking up from their pretty dream

(Chorus)
We're gonna break the walls tonight! (Yeah!)
Turn up the volume, hit the light!
No more whispers, time to shout
The Engine's roaring, let it out!

(Guitar Solo)
(A blistering, technical, shredding solo that Ryan fought to keep on the record)

(Bridge)
For every kid who feels the same
Trapped in a world, playing their game
This one's for you, this one's for me
Time to be free!

(Chorus)
We're gonna break the walls tonight! (Yeah!)
Turn up the volume, hit the light!
No more whispers, time to shout
The Engine's roaring, let it out!

(Outro)
Let it out!
Break the walls!
C'mon!
(fades on a hanging guitar chord)
## Worlds Apart
### Lore
An epic mid-tempo rock ballad that defied the studio's attempt to separate the band's "Stardust" and "Engine" personas. It features a massive, soaring duet between Ryan and Cassidy, proving their voices worked best when fighting together rather than apart. The lyrics in the third verse ("They want the 'Stardust,' they want the 'Engine'") are a direct meta-commentary on their struggle with Apex Records.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
Another mile on a lonely road
Just trying to get back to you
I'm carrying this heavy load
They're telling me what I have to do

(Verse 2)
The lights are bright, the stage is cold
They're trying to fit me in their mold
I see your face across the black
I know that we can find our way back

(Chorus)
We're worlds apart, but our hearts beat the same (Oh-oh)
I can hear you call my name
Through the distance, through the static and the dark
We're finding a way back to the start (Oh-oh)
Even when we're worlds apart

(Verse 3)
They want the "Stardust," they want the "Engine"
A different version for a different show
But we're the ones who'll be here in the end
We've got a fire they'll never know

(Chorus)
We're worlds apart, but our hearts beat the same (Oh-oh)
I can hear you call my name
Through the distance, through the static and the dark
We're finding a way back to the start (Oh-oh)
Even when we're worlds apart

(Guitar Solo)

(Bridge)
And when the mountains fall
And when the oceans rise
I'll find you (I'll find you!)

(Chorus)
We're worlds apart, but our hearts beat the same (Oh-oh)
I can hear you call my name
Through the distance, through the static and the dark
We're finding a way back to the start (Oh-oh)
Even when we're worlds apart

(Outro)
Worlds apart...
(fades)
## Another Night
### Lore
A "safe" filler track demanded by Apex to pad out the album with radio-friendly pop. Ryan was explicitly banned from playing on this track ("No guitar allowed" was written on the session sheet), leaving Cassidy isolated with just a synthesizer backing track. Her vocal performance is genuinely upset, but not because of a breakup—she was miserable recording without her brother in the studio.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
The clock on the wall is ticking slow
I'm watching the rain fall on the window
I pick up the phone, but I let it go
Just waiting for a voice I know

(Chorus)
It's just another night without you (Oh-oh)
Another night I've got to get through
The city's sleeping, but I'm awake
Another night, for goodness sake

(Verse 2)
The TV's on, but the sound is low
I've got no other place to go
The silence is a heavy sound
I wish that you were here right now

(Chorus)
It's just another night without you (Oh-oh)
Another night I've got to get through
The city's sleeping, but I'm awake
Another night, for goodness sake

(Bridge)
(A very simple, minimal, clean keyboard solo. No guitar allowed.)

(Chorus)
It's just another night without you (Oh-oh)
Another night I've got to get through
The city's sleeping, but I'm awake
Another night, for goodness sake

(Outro)
Another night...
Another night...
(fades out)
## Out of Time
### Lore
Ryan's punk-inspired vent. The 13-track album was too long for a standard vinyl LP, so Apex executives "gleefully" cut this short, 1:35 track from the original vinyl release to save space. They viewed it as "noise," but to Ryan, cutting his rawest song was a direct attempt to silence the "Engine."

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
Tick-tock goes the plastic clock
Another meeting on another block
They're selling me a future that I don't want
It's just the same old tired font

(Chorus)
I'm out of patience!
I'm out of time!
This ain't your product, this life is mine!
Out of patience!
Out of time!
Gonna break free, gonna cross the line!

(Verse 2)
You want another ballad, soft and slow
Another song for the radio
But I've got a fire burning in my hands
You'll never, ever understand

(Chorus)
I'm out of patience!
I'm out of time!
This ain't your product, this life is mine!
Out of patience!
Out of time!
Gonna break free, gonna cross the line!

(Guitar Solo)
(Short, fast, and chaotic. Pure punk-rock energy)

(Chorus)
I'm out of patience!
I'm out of time!
This ain't your product, this life is mine!
Out of patience!
Out of time!
Gonna break free, gonna cross the line!

(Outro)
Out of time!
(Song ends abruptly on a final, crashing chord)
## Rearview Mirror
### Lore
A moody, atmospheric "driving song" that sets the stage for the album's finale. While Apex approved it for its polished synth-pop sound, Cassidy used the lyrics to express her feeling of being a "passenger" in her own career. The poignant line "With all this 'Stardust' left inside" serves as a double entendre for her suppressed creative potential vs. the manufactured image she was forced to project.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
Another city, another night
We're speeding past the exit light
I see the skyline start to fade
A memory of a choice I made

(Chorus)
I'm looking in the rearview mirror
The past is getting clearer
Was it all just a mistake?
A heart that's learning how to break
It's all in the rearview mirror

(Verse 2)
This neon glow feels cold and thin
I don't know where I'm supposed to begin
I'm just a passenger in this ride
With all this "Stardust" left inside

(Chorus)
I'm looking in the rearview mirror
The past is getting clearer
Was it all just a mistake?
A heart that's learning how to break
It's all in the rearview mirror

(Bridge)
(A moody, atmospheric keyboard solo)
I'm driving fast, but I'm not free
Just running from the girl I used to be

(Chorus)
I'm looking in the rearview mirror
The past is getting clearer
Was it all just a mistake?
A heart that's learning how to break
It's all in the rearview mirror

(Outro)
Fading... fading...
In the rearview mirror...
(fades)
## The Stardust Engine
### Lore
Released in 1987 alongside the rest of *Electric Color* and buried by Apex Records at the very end of the album, this is the band's true origin anthem. *"The Stardust Engine"* was the very first song Cassidy and Ryan O'Connell came up with in 1986 when their twin cousins Evan and Tyler Wright joined them at CPI. Too scared at the time to shop the song around, the four of them kept it to themselves and decided to use that first song to name their band—celebrating their love of the cosmos ("Stardust") and the rockets to get you there ("Engine"). (Apex Records later screwed around and labeled Cassidy as their "Stardust" and Ryan as their "Engine"—a cheap 1980s corporate naming gimmick the band hated because it took away from their love of the cosmos and the rockets to get you there.) Critics and chart historians agree: had Apex released this as the lead single instead of burying it, it would have spent **11 straight weeks at #1**, dethroning the existing record holder for the decade.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
We were looking for a signal
A light in the endless black
A single voice in the universe
To tell us we're on the right track

(Verse 2)
We found the fire in the wires
We found the heart in the machine
Building a dream from nothing
A sound the world has never seen

(Chorus)
We are the Stardust Engine!
Racing through the night
We're burning on the fuel of a brand new light
We are the Stardust Engine!
Yeah, we're taking flight
Turning our future on, shining so bright!

(Verse 3)
They tried to ground us, keep us small
Tell us that we were gonna fall
But they don't know the things we know
They haven't seen the places we'll go

(Chorus)
We are the Stardust Engine!
Racing through the night
We're burning on the fuel of a brand new light
We are the Stardust Engine!
Yeah, we're taking flight
Turning our future on, shining so bright!

(Guitar Solo)
(A soaring, melodic solo that trades licks with a bright synthesizer hook)

(Bridge)
We are the cosmos, we are the fire
We're taking this one, higher and higher
We are the Stardust!
(We are the Engine!)
We are the Stardust Engine!

(Chorus)
We are the Stardust Engine!
Racing through the night
We're burning on the fuel of a brand new light
We are the Stardust Engine!
Yeah, we're taking flight
Turning our future on, shining so bright!

(Outro)
Shining so bright...
We're taking flight...
(fades out on an epic, sustained guitar chord)
# Neon Hearts (1989)
* **Narrative Era:** 1989
* **Real-World DSP Release:** 2026-03-31

## Neon Hearts
### Lore
The "safe" title track. Apex forced the band to include a callback lyric to their first hit ("It's an electric-colored feeling") to ensure brand consistency. The studio demanded this track open the album to assure fans that the "Stardust" sound hadn't changed, despite the band's desire to move toward rock.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
The city's humming, the lights are low
I'm waiting for you, I'm ready to go
The rain is tapping on the glass
I knew this feeling was built to last

(Pre-Chorus)
I hear your footsteps in the hall
I'm not afraid to fall
This is the only thing I know is true

(Chorus)
You've got my neon heart
Shining for you in the dark
A love so bright, it's off the charts
You've got my neon heart
It's an electric-colored feeling
And it all starts with you

(Verse 2)
We're walking fast on the wet concrete
The rhythm matches when our eyes meet
Don't need a promise, don't need a vow
My only moment is right here, right now

(Pre-Chorus)
I hear your footsteps in the hall
I'm not afraid to fall
This is the only thing I know is true

(Chorus)
You've got my neon heart
Shining for you in the dark
A love so bright, it's off the charts
You've got my neon heart
It's an electric-colored feeling
And it all starts with you

(Bridge)
(A bright, energetic keyboard solo)
Let the city sleep, we're wide awake
A chance that we're both gonna take!

(Chorus)
You've got my neon heart
Shining for you in the dark
A love so bright, it's off the charts
You've got my neon heart
It's an electric-colored feeling
And it all starts with you

(Outro)
My neon heart... (Shining for you)
My neon heart...
(fades out)
## Hollow Sound
### Lore
Ryan's act of "malicious compliance." He delivered the requested synth-pop sound but made the track **5:31 long**—knowing it was too long for radio—and included the protest line, "They're all afraid of the burning cross" (a reference to the canceled "Promise" single). It stands as a middle finger to the studio's "safe" direction.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
The red light's on, the tape is rolling
They're telling me what words to say
I'm trying to find the man I was
But he gets farther every day

(Verse 2)
They want the polish, they want the gleam
The "safer" version of the dream
But all I hear is a thin machine
Another part of their big scene

(Chorus)
It's just a hollow sound
My voice is in the mix
But the words are not my own
It's just a hollow sound
Another plastic fix
I'm singing all alone

(Verse 3)
They're all afraid of the burning cross
Terrified of what they lost
So now they're sanding off the edge
Pushing me back from the ledge

(Chorus)
It's just a hollow sound
My voice is in the mix
But the words are not my own
It's just a hollow sound
Another plastic fix
I'm singing all alone

(Bridge)
(A very clean, melodic, studio-approved keyboard solo)
A hollow sound...
Just a hollow sound...

(Chorus)
It's just a hollow sound
My voice is in the mix
But the words are not my own
It's just a hollow sound
Another plastic fix
I'm singing all alone
(fades)

## Side by Side
### Lore
The studio's furious overcorrection to "Hollow Sound." After Ryan delivered a 5:31 protest track, Apex demanded an absurdly short, safe pop song to balance the runtime and "fix" the album's pacing. The result is this 1:54 "bubblegum" track that feels almost manic in its enforced cheerfulness.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
When the rain is falling
And you feel all alone
Just remember I'm here
You can call me on the phone

(Chorus)
'Cause we're always better together
In any kind of weather
We don't have to hide
We're walking side by side (Oh-oh)
Side by side

(Verse 2)
The world can be a lonely place
A fast and hurried, crazy race
But we can slow it down a while
It's always better when I see you smile

(Chorus)
'Cause we're always better together
In any kind of weather
We don't have to hide
We're walking side by side (Oh-oh)
Side by side

(Bridge)
(A very short, simple, melodic keyboard riff)
Through the highs, through the lows
That's just the way it goes

(Chorus)
'Cause we're always better together
In any kind of weather
We don't have to hide
We're walking side by side (Oh-oh)
Side by side

(Outro)
Side by side...
(fades quickly)
## If You Walked Away
### Lore
The "late-night radio" ballad Apex demanded to balance out the album's "weirdness." The studio marketed this as a romantic "breakup what-if" song. In reality, Cassidy sang it to Ryan. Isolated by the studio's attempts to separate their "Stardust" and "Engine" personas, this was her genuine expression of fear that he might actually leave her alone in the machine.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
The streetlights make a blurry line
I'm running through the same old signs
I'm just a face in a passing crowd
Afraid to speak my thoughts out loud

(Verse 2)
But then you found me in the noise
You heard a whisper in my voice
You gave me something to believe
A reason I would never leave

(Chorus)
If you walked away
The sky would lose its blue
I wouldn't know what to do
I'd be lost in the gray
If you walked away
My world would fall apart
You're the owner of my (Neon) heart

(Verse 3)
They build these walls so high and steep
With promises they never keep
But we have a fortress, strong and true
I'm safe as long as I have you

(Chorus)
If you walked away
The sky would lose its blue
I wouldn't know what to do
I'd be lost in the gray
If you walked away
My world would fall apart
You're the owner of my (Neon) heart

(Bridge)
(A very safe, melodic, clean keyboard solo)
I'm holding on, I'm holding tight
Don't let me go into the night...

(Chorus)
If you walked away
The sky would lose its blue
I wouldn't know what to do
I'd be lost in the gray
If you walked away
My world would fall apart
You're the owner of my (Neon) heart

(Outro)
Don't walk away...
(fades out on piano)
## Same Old Song
### Lore
Ryan's "Trojan Horse." The track opens with a bright, optimistic keyboard hook that promises a radio-friendly hit, but the lyrics are a scathing critique of the studio's formulaic songwriting process. Ryan delivered the exact "polished sheen" Apex demanded, but used it to sing about how "safe" and suffocating the production had become ("Turn on the keyboard, keep the guitar low").

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
They hand me the chords, they're all the same
A different title for the same old game
Turn on the keyboard, keep the guitar low
Just another track for the radio

(Chorus)
It's just the same old song (Oh-oh)
Another right to make a wrong
I'm singing words I don't believe
It's getting harder and harder to breathe
It's just the same old song

(Verse 2)
I'm dreaming of a feedback wail
A drum kit hit like a hammer on a nail
But all I get is this polished sheen
The safest sound you've ever seen

(Chorus)
It's just the same old song (Oh-oh)
Another right to make a wrong
I'm singing words I don't believe
It's getting harder and harder to breathe
It's just the same old song

(Bridge)
(A very clean, melodic, studio-approved guitar solo)
Is anybody listening?
Is anybody real?
Or do you just like the way it feels?

(Chorus)
It's just the same old song (Oh-oh)
Another right to make a wrong
I'm singing words I don't believe
It's getting harder and harder to breathe
It's just the same old song

(Outro)
The same old song...
(fades out)
## Chain Reaction
### Lore
The start of the "Side B Rebellion." After an A-Side full of studio-mandated synth-pop, Apex allowed this "one-two punch" of pure rock (paired with the following track, "Not Your Doll") as a concession. It became an immediate fan favorite, proving that the "Engine" sound was what the audience actually wanted.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
Another day, another line to say
They're trying to wash the real me away
But there's a fire, a burning sound
You can't keep a good thing down

(Chorus)
It's a chain reaction! (Oh-oh)
I can't be contained
Starting up a fire that can't be explained
A chain reaction!
It's time to break the mold
A story that's about to be told!

(Guitar Solo 1)
(A blistering, technical, shredding solo that Ryan fought to keep on the record)

(Verse 2)

I'm never ever looking back
I'm turning up my guitar loud
I'm gonna stand out from the crowd

(Chorus)
A chain reaction!
I can't be contained
Starting up a fire that can't be explained
A chain reaction!
It's time to break the mold
A story that's about to be told!

(Guitar Solo 2)
(Extended technical guitar solo)

(Chorus)
A chain reaction!
I can't be contained
Starting up a fire that can't be explained
A chain reaction!
It's time to break the mold
A story that's about to be told!

(Outro)
Chain reaction...
(fades on a hanging guitar chord)

## Not Your Doll
### Lore
Cassidy's "fierce side" finally breaking through. Following immediately after "Chain Reaction," this arena rock anthem completes the "Side B Rebellion." In it, Cassidy directly rejects the "Stardust" persona Apex forced upon her. While the studio tried to suppress her "Engine" side, this track became the definitive proof that she could rock just as hard as her brother.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
You put me in a box, you told me to be sweet
The "Stardust" girl, the perfect beat
You told me "soften up," you told me "know your place"
But you never saw the fire in my face

(Chorus)
'Cause I'm not your doll!
I'm not your pretty toy
I'm not just a picture for a poster boy
I'm not your doll!
I'm here to make a choice
And I'm gonna make you hear my voice!

(Verse 2)
You're scared of the noise, you're scared of the grit
You're terrified that I'm not gonna fit
In the tiny world you built for me
But I'm about to set myself free

(Chorus)
'Cause I'm not your doll!
I'm not your pretty toy
I'm not just a picture for a poster boy
I'm not your doll!
I'm here to make a choice
And I'm gonna make you hear my voice!

(Guitar Solo)

(Bridge)
You heard the "story to be told"?
Well, I'm the one who'll break the mold!
You wanted 'Stardust'? You get the fire!
My voice is real, and it's getting higher!

(Chorus)
'Cause I'm not your doll!
I'm not your pretty toy
I'm not just a picture for a poster boy
I'm not your doll!
I'm here to make a choice
And I'm gonna make you hear my voice!

(Outro)
Not your doll!
Hear my voice!
(fades out on a wailing guitar note)
## Quiet on the Set
### Lore
The "Punishment" begins. After the explosive rock energy of the "one-two punch" on Side B, Apex executives stepped in to "shut the Engine down." They forced Ryan to record this quiet, introspective ballad to kill the momentum. The lyrics are a meta-commentary on his silencing ("This is the price for being loud / Getting lost back in the crowd").

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
The "story" has been told
The echo's fading fast
I tried to "break the mold"
They told me it would never last
They're shutting down the "Engine"

(Chorus)
Now it's quiet on the set (Oh-oh)
Back to the silence and regret
This is the price for being loud
Getting lost back in the crowd
After the laughter...

(Verse 2)
The "noise" we made was all too real
They're terrified of what they feel
So turn the keyboards up again
And hide the anger and the pain

(Chorus)
Now it's quiet on the set (Oh-oh)
Back to the silence and regret
This is the price for being loud
Getting lost back in the crowd
After the laughter...

(Bridge)
(A soft, melodic keyboard solo. No guitars.)
They want the "Stardust," safe and light
Just close your eyes and end the fight
Just close your eyes...

(Chorus)
Now it's quiet on the set (Oh-oh)
Back to the silence and regret
This is the price for being loud
Getting lost back in the crowd
After the laughter...

(Outro)
After the laughter...
Just... quiet...
(fades out on a single piano note)

## Shadow Dancing
### Lore
The studio strikes back. Immediately following the "rebellion" of Side B's opening tracks, Apex Records moved to crush the momentum. They forced Cassidy back into her "Stardust" persona for this moody, atmospheric synth track, explicitly banning electric guitars from the mix to ensure the album returned to a "safe, marketable" sound before the end.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
The city lights are cold tonight
I'm walking under the pale moonlight
I see two people passing by
Reflected in a stranger's eye

(Chorus)
We're just shadow dancing
A pantomime, a game of chance
Trying to make it look like romance
We're just shadow dancing
Afraid to stop, afraid to fall
Just shadows on the wall

(Verse 2)
You say the words I want to hear
You whisper "love" to hide the fear
We're both performing in this play

(Chorus)
We're just shadow dancing
A pantomime, a game of chance
Trying to make it look like romance
We're just shadow dancing
Afraid to stop, afraid to fall
Just shadows on the wall

(Bridge)
(A moody, atmospheric keyboard solo)
Is this real? I'll never know
Just follow where the music goes
Just keep on dancing...

(Chorus)
We're just shadow dancing
A pantomime, a game of chance
Trying to make it look like romance
We're just shadow dancing
Afraid to stop, afraid to fall
Just shadows on the wall

(Outro)
Shadows on the wall...
Shadow dancing...
(fades)
## After the Show
### Lore
Ryan's final laugh. As "punishment" for the heavy rock tracks earlier on Side B, Apex ordered him to close the album with a quiet ballad, explicitly forbidding "pounding drums" on the track sheet. Ryan complied by recording the song using **percussive acoustic guitar**—slapping the body of the instrument to simulate a beat. It was a subtle, rhythmic "middle finger" to the executives who tried to silence the "Engine."

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
The stage is empty, the crowd is gone
Another night, another dawn
We played the part, we sang the song
And now we have to move along

(Verse 2)
The neon heart is turning low
It's just a faded, distant glow
Is this the dream we wanted so?
I guess we'll never know

(Chorus)
We're just echoes in the city
It's not so bad, it's not so pretty
Just echoes, turning to gray
We'll come back and fight another day

(Bridge)
(A soft, melodic acoustic guitar solo, underscored by the rhythmic slapping of the guitar body)
Another day...
Another way...

(Chorus)
Just echoes, turning to gray
We'll come back and fight another day

(Outro)
After the show...
Goodnight...
(fades out on acoustic guitar)

## The Promise (Corporate Demo)
### Lore
The exact sound Apex Records wanted: safe, sterile, and perfectly calibrated for a televangelist broadcast. This was the studio's cynical attempt to cash in on the "religious pop" trend. The guitar solo is painfully restrained, engineered specifically not to offend anyone. The entire project was scrapped in a panic by executives after the massive controversy surrounding Cydele's "Worship" video dropped on MTV the very next day.

### Lyrics

I was looking for a sign
I heard a whisper, a distant sound

Shining down on me
I can hear The Call! (Oh-oh-oh)
It's setting me free
It's a beautiful light for the whole world to see
I can feel The Promise, inside of me!

(Verse 2)
No more shadows, no more doubt
I know what my life's about
I'm rising up, I'm strong and new
It's all because I'm following through

(Pre-Chorus)
I felt a hand reach out for me
And suddenly, I could see
The world was bright and new

(Chorus)
I can feel The Promise! (Oh-oh-oh)
Shining down on me
I can hear The Call! (Oh-oh-oh)
It's setting me free
It's a beautiful light for the whole world to see
I can feel The Promise, inside of me!

(Bridge)
(Music swells)
Lift your hands up to the sky!
Let the feeling take you high!
Just let the feeling take you high!

(Guitar Solo)
(A small, restrained, and clean guitar solo that feels like it belongs in a televangelist church broadcast)

(Chorus)
I can feel The Promise! (Oh-oh-oh)
Shining down on me
I can hear The Call! (Oh-oh-oh)
It's setting me free
It's a beautiful light for the whole world to see

(Outro)

Hear The Call!
(The Promise... inside of me)
(fades out)
## The Promise (Sabotage Demo)
### Lore
Ryan's malicious compliance caught on tape. Forced to record a manufactured "synth-gospel" pop track against the band's wishes, Ryan played the label's game right up until the bridge. Then, he unleashed the "devil's music," violently shoving a blistering, unholy rock solo into the middle of their squeaky-clean religious hit. It gave the horrified executives a second massive reason to pull the plug the second the Cydele controversy hit MTV.

And suddenly, I could see
The world was bright and new

(Chorus)
I can feel The Promise! (Oh-oh-oh)
Shining down on me

It's setting me free
It's a beautiful light for the whole world to see

# Live in Chicago (1990) [LiveAlbum]
* **Narrative Era:** 1990
* **Real-World DSP Release:** 2026-04-01

## The Stardust Engine (Live in Chicago)
### Lore
This track is the "Smoking Gun" that proves Julian Vance (the executive behind the *Friction* disaster) never did his homework. In the intro, Ryan O'Connell—who, like his sister, is autistic and values absolute clarity—explicitly introduces Cassidy as "my sister," publicly disproving the "married couple" assumption years before it destroyed their contract.

Musically, this recording is a source of fury for the band. It was the only concession Apex made—allowing them to play their self-titled anthem as the opener of their strict 30-minute support slot. However, the mix is "sabotaged": Cassidy's iconic "Stardust" synth leitmotif—the musical heart of the song—is buried so deep in the mix it is practically inaudible, leaving only the "Engine" rock elements.

### Lyrics

(Spoken Intro)
Hello Chicago! I'm Ryan O'Connell!
(Crowd cheers)
And this is my sister, Cassidy!
(Crowd cheers louder)
And we are The Stardust Engine!

(Verse 1)
We were looking for a signal
A light in the endless black
A single voice in the universe
To tell us we're on the right track

(Verse 2)
We found the fire in the wires
We found the heart in the machine
Building a dream from nothing
A sound the world has never seen

(Chorus)
We are the Stardust Engine!
Racing through the night
We're burning on the fuel of a brand new light
We are the Stardust Engine!
Yeah, we're taking flight
Turning our future on, shining so bright!

(Verse 3)
They tried to ground us, keep us small
Tell us that we were gonna fall
But they don't know the things we know
They haven't seen the places we'll go

(Chorus)
We are the Stardust Engine!
Racing through the night
We're burning on the fuel of a brand new light
We are the Stardust Engine!
Yeah, we're taking flight
Turning our future on, shining so bright!

(Guitar Solo)
(Ryan plays a blistering solo, but the "Call and Response" synth notes are frustratingly faint in the mix)

(Bridge)
We are the cosmos, we are the fire
We're taking this one, higher and higher
We are the Stardust!
(We are the Engine!)
We are the Stardust Engine!

(Chorus)
We are the Stardust Engine!
Racing through the night
We're burning on the fuel of a brand new light
We are the Stardust Engine!
Yeah, we're taking flight
Turning our future on, shining so bright!

(Outro)
Shining so bright...
We're taking flight...
(Spoken) Thank you!
## Neon Hearts (Live in Chicago)
### Lore
As the title track of the album they were touring, this was a mandatory inclusion in the setlist. Apex Records insisted the band focus on their "Stardust" synth-pop sound to promote the new record, forcing Ryan to play the "good soldier" and deliver a polished, high-energy performance, even as he chafed against the "safe" creative direction.

You've got my neon heart
It's an electric-colored feeling

I'm not afraid to fall

(Chorus)

## Dancing in the Neon (Live in Chicago)
### Lore
"Time is money!" With a strict 30-minute opening slot and a tour manager watching the clock, the band had no time for deep cuts. Apex Records demanded they prioritize their high-energy synth-pop hits to win over the headliner's pop-focused audience. You can hear the urgency in the performance—the tempo is slightly faster than the studio version as they race to fit the setlist into their allotted window.

We're moving 'til the night is gone (Oh-oh, oh-oh)
Forget about tomorrow
We're shining like a light
Dancing in the neon tonight!

(Outro)
Dancing in the neon... (Oh-oh, oh-oh)
'Til the night is gone...
(Spoken) How you doing out there?!
## If You Walked Away (Live in Chicago)
### Lore
For Cassidy, who is autistic, the lyrics of this song were not a performance—they were a terrifying "what-if" scenario that she experienced viscerally every time she sang it. While the studio marketed it as a romantic breakup ballad, Cassidy sang it as a desperate plea to her brother. **The fear in her voice on this recording is real.**

Ryan, who is also autistic, understood her need for absolute certainty better than anyone. He famously had to reassure her after *every single concert*—often using the exact same phrase, **"Family sticks together"**—to ground her back in reality and confirm that he was never going to leave her.

**Note: After this specific performance, Cassidy broke down in tears backstage. Ryan had to hold her in a deep pressure hug for twenty minutes to help her reset and remind her that he was right there.**

### Lyrics

(Spoken Intro)
Thank you. We're gonna slow things down for you for just a minute...

(Verse 1)
The streetlights make a blurry line
I'm running through the same old signs
I'm just a face in a passing crowd
Afraid to speak my thoughts out loud

(Verse 2)
But then you found me in the noise
You heard a whisper in my voice
You gave me something to believe
A reason I would never leave

(Chorus)
If you walked away
The sky would lose its blue
I wouldn't know what to do
I'd be lost in the gray
If you walked away
My world would fall apart
You're the owner of my (Neon) heart

(Verse 3)
They build these walls so high and steep
With promises they never keep
But we have a fortress, strong and true
I'm safe as long as I have you

(Chorus)
If you walked away
The sky would lose its blue
I wouldn't know what to do
I'd be lost in the gray
If you walked away
My world would fall apart
You're the owner of my (Neon) heart

(Bridge)
(A very safe, melodic, clean keyboard solo)
I'm holding on, I'm holding tight
Don't let me go into the night...

(Chorus)
If you walked away
The sky would lose its blue
I wouldn't know what to do
I'd be lost in the gray
If you walked away
My world would fall apart
You're the owner of my (Neon) heart

(Outro)
Don't walk away...
(Spoken) Thank you so much.
## Light Up the Night (Live in Chicago)
### Lore
The "Big Bet." Apex Records had poured their entire marketing budget into this track, convinced it was the #1 hit that would define the album. Consequently, the band was under immense pressure to deliver a "perfect," high-energy performance of it every night. You can hear the professional polish here—Cassidy hits every note and Ryan plays the "good soldier," executing the catchy hooks exactly as the studio demanded, proving they could play the game even if they hated the rules.

## My Anchor Holds (Live in Chicago)
### Lore
A love letter between siblings disguised as a power ballad. While Apex Records marketed this as a romantic duet to sell records, the "Ironheads" in the audience knew the truth. When Ryan and Cassidy sang this to each other on stage, it wasn't about romance; it was about survival. It was their public promise that no matter what the studio threw at them, they would be each other's anchor in the storm.

### Lyrics

(Spoken Intro)
This is a song for that one person who keeps you sane... the one who's your anchor.

(Verse 1)

(Chorus)

You're the one true thing that I can hold
Through the static and the driving rain
You're the one who keeps me sane
You are the shelter, you are the truth
My anchor in this crazy youth

(Verse 2)
They pull us under, they push us high
They build us up just to watch us die
It's hard to know who you can trust
When all the promises turn to dust

(Chorus)
But when the world is spinning out of my control
You're the one true thing that I can hold
Through the static and the driving rain
You're the one who keeps me sane
You are the shelter, you are the truth
My anchor in this crazy youth

(Guitar Solo)
(A soaring, melodic solo that Ryan plays while standing back-to-back with Cassidy at the piano)

(Bridge)
So let them talk, let them stare
As long as you are standing there
I'm not afraid of what's to come
I know where I belong...

(Chorus)
'Cause when the world is spinning out of my control
You're the one true thing that I can hold
Through the static and the driving rain
You're the one who keeps me sane
You are the shelter, you are the truth
My anchor in this crazy youth

(Outro)
My anchor...
My anchor holds...
(Spoken) We love you, Chicago!
# The Warehouse Tapes (1995)
* **Narrative Era:** 1995
* **Real-World DSP Release:** 2026-04-02

## Static
### Lore
The opening statement of the "Freedom Era." Recorded in the warehouse with amps turned to 11, this track acts as a direct rejection of the "hollow sound" Apex forced the band to make for five years. The line "I am the static! I am the noise!" became the rallying cry for the "Ironhead" fanbase during the band's independent rebirth.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
You wanted pop, you wanted clean
A hollow sound from a thin machine
You tried to tune me out, you tried to change the dial
But I've been waiting all this while

(Chorus)
I am the static! I am the noise!
The signal breaking, the hidden voice
I am the static!
The feedback wail!
The story that you tried to derail!

(Verse 2)
This isn't synth-pop, this isn't grace
This is the sound of a different place
This is the heart you tried to hide
The part of us that never died

(Chorus)
I am the static! I am the noise!
The signal breaking, the hidden voice
I am the static!
The feedback wail!
The story that you tried to derail!

(Guitar Solo)

(Chorus)
I am the static! I am the noise!
The signal breaking, the hidden voice
I am the static!
The feedback wail!
The story that you tried to derail!

(Outro)
I am the static...
I am the static...
Can you hear me now?
## Garbage
### Lore
Ryan's raw, "jangle-rock" rage track. It retells the infamous "dirty mirror" incident from the 1992 *Friction* photo shoot, where an executive tried to offer him cocaine on a mirror, and Ryan—innocently thinking it was dirt—wiped it clean with a napkin. The song channels his fury at the executive who humiliated him and tried to destroy his family.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
You brought your tray, your silver plate
You thought you'd open up the gate
You showed me powder on a glass
You thought you had a man of class

(Chorus)
It was just dust! It was just a lie!
So I wiped it clean, and I watched you die
You called it 'art', you called it 'style'
It was just garbage in a filthy pile!

(Verse 2)

After the "Tabloid Hell" of the 1994 trial, the media tried to paint Cassidy as a "Victim-Saint"—a fragile, broken doll. This fierce arena anthem is her rejection of that narrative. She is neither the "Stardust" pop star nor the "Victim" of the scandal; she is a rock musician standing on her own two feet.

I am not your grace

## Escape Velocity (Ad Astra)
### Lore
The "Magnum Opus" that consumes the entirety of Side B. This 15-minute progressive rock suite is Cassidy O'Connell's autobiographical journey from the trauma of the trial ("The Cage") to the transcendence of artistic freedom ("The Cosmos").

Known by fans simply as **"Ad Astra,"** the track features a distinct "mechanical cutoff" sound between Part II and Part III, created by Ryan pulling the plug on his guitar amp to simulate a rocket's Main Engine Cutoff (MECO).

### Lyrics

[Part I: The Cage]
(Whispered, over a dark, shimmering synth pad)
The walls are cold, the air is thin
The noise outside is closing in
A single light, a buzzing sound
Another day in the underground

I'm running from the face I see
The "victim-saint" they made of me
The "wholesome girl," so sad and worn
A "David" for their Goliath's scorn

This rage is just a heavy stone
A gravity I've outgrown
Shedding skin, shedding weight
Escaping from this world of hate

They loved the trial, the "family" torn
They loved the story, a new star born
But not from music, not from grace
From the "hot-chocolate-drinking" "angel face"

The "Shark" was right, the "nuke" went off
We won the battle, but at what cost?
We're "radioactive," we're "litigious"
We're just the joke, the "victim-saints"

(Slightly louder)
I'm trapped inside this "Wilderness"
This "Fortress" built on bitterness
This isn't freedom, this is a cell
This "righteous hell"...
This "righteous hell"...

[Part II: Ignition (Ad Astra)]
(The dark synth pad is suddenly cut by a rapid, driving analog synth sequencer. A pounding, 80s acoustic drum beat kicks in, locked with a driving bassline. Vocals become strong, clear, and melodic.)

I see a signal in the endless black
A single light, no turning back
The pull of the old world fades away
A new horizon, a new day

The countdown's running in my head
Ignoring all the things they said
The "Engine" roars, a crimson light
A pillar of fire in the night

The pressure builds, I'm pinned to the seat
My heart is pounding with the beat
The G-force pulling, a heavy stone
The last chain breaks, I'm not alone

(Chorus)
I'm climbing! Climbing!
Past the point of no return
I'm flying! Flying!
With a fire I let burn
Just a signal, a distant light
Burning through the endless night
Ad astra... (To the stars!)
Ad astra!

(Instrumental Break)
(A short, fast, synthesizer and guitar riff-heavy section. Pure 80s prog-rock energy.)

The sky is tearing at the seams
Awake now from their broken dreams
The "Stardust" is just a trail of dust
The "Stardust" is the thing I trust

The clouds are falling far below
A place of pain I used to know
The blue gives way to endless black
There is no thought of turning back

(Chorus)
I'm climbing! Climbing!
Past the point of no return
I'm flying! Flying!
With a fire I let burn
Just a signal, a distant light
Burning through the endless night
Ad astra... (To the stars!)
Ad astra!

[Part III: The Cosmos]
(The driving beat cuts off abruptly. Tempo slows to a crawl. Vast, sweeping synth pads take over.)

I'm floating now, the engine's off
The silence is a holy sound
The rage is gone, the fear is lost
In this new universe I've found

The "Cage" is just a distant star
A tiny light I left behind
I finally know just who you are
The other half of my own mind

(Ethereal, layered vocals swell)
I'm loved by the sun
I'm held by the void
An orbit just begun
A life not destroyed

I'm loved by the moon
I'm cradled by the black
My heart is back in tune
There is no turning back

And the stars are singing
A song I understand
I'm finally, finally
Reaching for your hand

(Instrumental Bridge)
(Music swells. A long, beautiful, melodic, and highly emotional electric guitar solo. Full of hope, not rage.)

(Ethereal, layered vocals return)
I see the colors of the nebula
A place I've only seen in dreams
The reds, the blues, the purples call
Flowing in majestic streams

This is the place I was meant to be
This is the "Stardust" that is me
The cosmos and the rocket's fire
My one, my only true desire 
I'm loved by the sun

(Ethereal, layered vocalization)

[Part IV: Re-entry (The New World)]
(Music slowly fades back to just the shimmering synth pads and ethereal, whispered vocals)

The walls are gone
The air is clear
There is no noise
There is no fear

The "Stardust" is a forgotten ghost
The "Engine" has delivered me to the coast
Of a new world, a new shore
A place I've never been before

The "Cage" is broken, the "Victim-Saint" is dead
A new, clean script is in my head
The "Friction" burned away in the fire
Leaving only my true desire

(Ethereal, layered vocals swell for the finale)
I'm loved by the sun...
I'm held by the void...
My orbit has begun...
My life is not destroyed...

Ad astra...
Ad astra...
(To the stars...)

(Song fades out on a final, sustained, peaceful synthesizer chord)

## Escape Velocity (Ad Astra) (Full Suite)
### Lore
The "Magnum Opus." Originally released as the B-Side of *The Warehouse Tapes*, this suite stands here as the monumental A-Side it was always meant to be.

This 15-minute progressive rock journey is Cassidy O'Connell's autobiography in four movements, charting her escape from the trauma of the 'Friction' trial ("The Cage") to the transcendence of total artistic freedom ("The Cosmos").

Known by fans simply as **"Ad Astra,"** the track features a distinct "mechanical cutoff" sound between Part II and Part III. This wasn't a studio effect; Ryan O'Connell physically pulled the plug on his guitar amplifier mid-recording to simulate the violent silence of a rocket's Main Engine Cutoff (MECO).

In the band's "Ironworks" canon, this track acts as a coded transmission broadcast from Port Telsus, sending safe navigational coordinates to resistance fighters hidden in The Weave.

## Escape Velocity (Ad Astra) (Radio Edit)
### Lore
The "Trojan Horse" edit. Knowing that mainstream radio would never play a 15-minute prog-rock suite, the band (at Holly's insistence) cut this condensed 5-minute version for the promo CD.

It strips away the extended ambient transitions and long instrumental solos, focusing purely on the core narrative arc from "The Cage" to "Re-entry." While die-hard "Ironheads" consider it incomplete, this is the version that actually charted, introducing millions of new fans to the band's "Freedom Era" sound.

### Lyrics

[Part I: The Cage]
(Whispered, over a dark, shimmering synth pad)
The walls are cold, the air is thin
The noise outside is closing in
I'm running from the face I see
The "victim-saint" they made of me
This rage is just a heavy stone
A gravity I've outgrown
Shedding skin, shedding weight
Escaping from this world of hate

[Part II: Ignition (Ad Astra)]
(A driving, 80s analog synth sequencer starts, bass and drums kick in)
(Vocals become strong and melodic)
I see a light in the endless black
A signal calling, no turning back
The pull of the old world fades away
A new horizon, a new day

(Chorus)
(Full band, soaring ethereal vocals)
I'm climbing, climbing
Past the point of no return
I'm flying, flying
With a fire I let burn
Just a signal, a distant light
Burning through the endless night
Ad astra... (To the stars!)

[Part III: The Cosmos (Loved by the Sun)]
(Music becomes vast, atmospheric, and beautiful. Grand, sweeping synth pads and melodic guitar)
I'm floating now, the engine's off
The silence is a holy sound
The rage is gone, the fear is lost
In this new universe I've found

I'm loved by the sun
I'm held by the void
An orbit just begun
A life not destroyed
And the stars are singing
A song I understand
I'm finally, finally
Reaching for your hand

(Instrumental Bridge)
(A long, melodic, soaring electric guitar solo, full of emotion and hope)

[Part IV: Re-entry (The New World)]
(Music slowly fades back to just the shimmering synth pads and ethereal, whispered vocals)
The walls are gone
The air is clear
There is no noise
There is no fear
Ad astra...
Ad astra...
(fades out)

## No Hidden Gravity
### Lore
A massive, booming 1980s arena rock power ballad that completely subverts the era's "fatal attraction" tropes. Instead of a standard romance, this track is fiercely dedicated to an unbreakable, purely platonic bond. 

In the *Stardust Engine* universe, the band wrote this song after hearing the story of two best friends from the historic Ghent neighborhood in Norfolk: Nate and Maya. Maya—deeply genuine, hearing-impaired, and fluent in ASL—became Nate's ultimate "autistic safe person" during a chaotic trip to Washington, D.C. After lightning-fast reflexes allowed her to stabilize his wheelchair on a broken expansion joint, they escaped a sensory-overload group walk by riding the Capital Metro together.

Society constantly attempts to draw a romantic line between them simply because they are of opposite genders. "No Hidden Gravity" is their booming, gated-snare anthem rejecting that broken ledger. It celebrates the profound intimacy of a purely platonic "fortress," where they can share a life, share a bed, and fly in parallel without the flame. 

**PRODUCTION NOTE:**
**Style:** 1980s power ballad, arena rock, atmospheric analog synth, booming gated-reverb snare, male and female vocals, soaring guitar solo, slow tempo, massive chorus. 
**Exclusions:** Modern pop, auto-tune, EDM, trap beats, lo-fi, bedroom pop, acoustic, unplugged, country, folk, modern R&B, screaming, growling, death metal, heavy metalcore, romantic duet, sensual, love song, muffled vocals, indie pop.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
[Atmospheric 1980s Analog Synthesizer, Slow Echoing Piano]
They look at us and try to draw a line
Connecting the dots in their standard design
They expect the push, they expect the fall
But they don't understand this architecture at all

[Verse 1]
[Steady Bassline, Slow Driving Drum Beat]
I'm used to the guessing, the games, and the chase
The hidden agendas invading my space
But you left your armor right there at the door
You don't want a piece of me, you don't ask for more
I told you the boundary, I drew you the map
And you didn't treat it like some kind of trap

[Pre-Chorus]
[Building Tempo, Heavy Guitar Chords]
No blurred lines, no sudden attack!
I can turn around and know you've got my back!

[Chorus]
[Maximum Volume, Massive 80s Arena Rock, Booming Snare]
There's no hidden gravity pulling us down!
No fatal attraction, no spinning around!
We're flying in parallel, steady and true
The safest place in the world is right next to you!
We're breaking the script, we're changing the game
A different kind of fire without the flame!
Yeah, there's no hidden gravity here!

[Verse 2]
[Pulsing Synth, Driving Rhythm]
They say that two people can never just stand
Side by side without holding a hand
But society's ledger is broken and cheap
This is a promise I'm willing to keep
You don't have to perform, you don't have to pretend
I'm not your salvation, I'm just your friend

[Pre-Chorus]
[Building Tempo, Heavy Guitar Chords]
No blurred lines, no sudden attack!
I can turn around and know you've got my back!

[Chorus]
[Maximum Volume, Massive 80s Arena Rock, Booming Snare]
There's no hidden gravity pulling us down!
No fatal attraction, no spinning around!
We're flying in parallel, steady and true
The safest place in the world is right next to you!
We're breaking the script, we're changing the game
A different kind of fire without the flame!
Yeah, there's no hidden gravity here!

[Guitar Solo]
[Soaring, Melodic Electric Guitar Solo, Bright Synth Stabs, Heavy Half-Time Drums]

[Bridge]
[Music Drops Out, Just Piano and Vocals]
You don't need to conquer.
I don't need to hide.
We built a fortress.
With no one inside but us!

[Chorus]
[Explosive Re-entry, Full Band, Layered Vocals]
There's no hidden gravity pulling us down!
No fatal attraction, no spinning around!
We're flying in parallel, steady and true
The safest place in the world is right next to you!
We're breaking the script, we're changing the game
A different kind of fire without the flame!
Yeah, there's no hidden gravity here!

[Outro]
[Music Slowly Fading, Shimmering Synth Pad]
No hidden gravity.
Just the signal coming clear.
I'm right here.
[Final Sustained Piano Chord]
[End]
# Forger Nation War Chest (1997) [CompilationAlbum]
* **Narrative Era:** 1997
* **Real-World DSP Release:** 2026-04-14

## Electric Color
### Lore
The opening track of the compilation and the band's first massive commercial success. Released in the summer of 1987, this polished, studio-mandated synth-pop track sat at the **#2 spot** for six weeks. While fans embraced it as a bright, optimistic love song, the band viewed it differently. 

For Ryan and Cassidy, *Electric Color* represented the beginning of the "Cold War" with Apex Records. It was the exact sound the executives demanded—clean, safe, and heavily synthesized. While it put them on the map and paid the bills, it also cemented the "Stardust" pop image that they would spend the next decade trying to tear down. Its inclusion here serves as "Exhibit A" of the gilded cage they eventually escaped.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
Woke up this morning, the world was gray
Didn't know the colors had all faded away
Been living in a room with the curtains drawn tight
Then you flipped a switch and turned on the light

(Chorus)
Oh, I'm seeing in electric color
Finding a rhythm I never knew
Dancing in a brand new summer
My whole world feels bright and new
It's an electric-colored feeling
And it all started with you!

(Verse 2)
The city sounds were just a buzz in my ear
A static station I could never quite hear
But now the noise is turning into a song
And I finally feel like I belong

(Chorus)
Oh, I'm seeing in electric color
Finding a rhythm I never knew
Dancing in a brand new summer
My whole world feels bright and new
It's an electric-colored feeling
And it all started with you!

(Bridge)
The beat is getting stronger
I can't hide it any longer
My heart is playing catch-up
To the music turning way up!

(Guitar Solo)

(Chorus)
Oh, I'm seeing in electric color
Finding a rhythm I never knew
Dancing in a brand new summer
My whole world feels bright and new
It's an electric-colored feeling
And it all started with you!

(Outro)
Electric color
Feeling so new
It's all because of you
## Light Up the Night
### Lore
Inserted right after their breakout hit *Electric Color*, this track is a monument to corporate miscalculation. *Light Up the Night* was the song Apex Records poured *all* of their money into. The executives were convinced that this highly polished, aggressively safe synth-pop track would be "The Big One" that cemented The Stardust Engine as global pop idols. 

It wasn't. Despite a massive marketing campaign, the sterile, manufactured sound failed to connect with audiences. Looking back from 1997, the band included it here on *Essential Tracks* as a point of pride: it proved to the label that money couldn't buy authenticity, and that the audience actually craved the heavier rock elements the studio was desperately trying to suppress.

### Lyrics

## Neon Hearts
### Lore
The mandatory title track of their 1989 sophomore album. While *Electric Color* was a genuine, if overly polished, attempt at finding their footing in the industry, *Neon Hearts* represents the exact moment the band realized they were trapped in a corporate formula. 

Apex Records was terrified of losing the massive pop audience they had cultivated, so they demanded pure brand consistency for the second album. They forced Cassidy to write a direct lyrical callback to their first hit ("It's an electric-colored feeling") and mandated that this "safe," high-energy synth track open the record. For the band, who had just spent two years secretly writing hard rock riffs on the road, flying to Los Angeles to record this sterile pop anthem was a miserable experience. It stands on this compilation as a monument to the suffocating creative control of the "Cold War" era.

## Dancing in the Neon
### Lore
The absolute peak of the Apex Records synth-pop machine. If *Electric Color* introduced their studio-mandated sound, *Dancing in the Neon* cemented their status as pop idols—a label the band deeply resented. 

Apex pushed this high-energy, undeniably catchy single heavily, prioritizing its polished, radio-friendly production over the band's actual rock roots. To achieve the "perfect" sound, Evan and Tyler's driving rhythm section was largely buried under layers of electronic drum machines and sequencing. Looking back from 1997, the band acknowledges the track as an undeniable earworm that kept the lights on, but it remains a prime example of the "plastic" sound they eventually had to violently reject to survive.

## Not Your Doll
### Lore
The first true crack in the armor. Closing out the "Apex Years" section of this compilation, *Not Your Doll* is the sound of Cassidy O'Connell finally breaking. 

During the recording of the 1989 *Neon Hearts* album, the studio desperately tried to maintain her "sweet, wholesome Stardust" persona. This track was Cassidy’s fierce, arena-rock rejection of that box. While Apex executives hated it—viewing it as an unmarketable deviation from their pop formula—they begrudgingly allowed it on the B-Side to appease the band. From the perspective of 1997, this track isn't just an outlier; it is the definitive turning point. It proved to the world (and to the label) that Cassidy wasn't just a pop singer; she was the co-pilot of the Engine.

## My Anchor Holds
### Lore
Looking back from 1997, exactly ten years after its initial release, this track stands as the ultimate "Trojan Horse" of the band's Apex Records era. 

The studio executives heavily polished this power ballad and marketed it globally as a generic, sweeping romance to sell records. But for Ryan and Cassidy, the original studio cut was always a desperate love letter to their own unbreakable family bond. While they later evolved the song's meaning—most notably completely rewriting it as *My Anchor (The Sail)* on the *Hard Reset* album—this pristine 1987 recording captures the exact moment the siblings realized they were the only thing keeping each other sane under the suffocating pressure of the pop machine. It is a massive pop hit that secretly served as their survival anthem.

## The Stardust Engine
### Lore
Ten years ago, Apex Records executives deliberately buried this track at the very end of the band's 1987 debut album, terrified of its heavier, uncompromised rock elements. Today, on this 1997 retrospective compilation, it stands as the undeniable blueprint for the band's entire independent era. 

It is the only studio track from their debut that perfectly blended Cassidy's "Stardust" analog synths with Ryan's driving "Engine" rock guitar. Music historians universally agree: had the label trusted the band and released this as the lead single in '87, it would have spent 11 straight weeks at #1, dethroning the existing record holder for the decade. Placed right in the heart of *Essential Tracks*, the band is finally giving the original studio recording of their true anthem the massive spotlight it always deserved.

## Static
### Lore
Act III of the compilation, "The Wilderness," opens with a violent tonal shift. After the sabotaged live recordings of the Apex era, the shrieking guitar feedback of *Static* immediately announces that the "Cold War" is over and the "Freedom Era" has begun. 

Originally recorded in 1995 inside the band's warehouse studio (with the amps literally turned to 11), this track was initially distributed underground. During those lean "Wilderness Years," the band dubbed cassettes and burned CD-Rs one at a time, mailing them directly to the "Ironheads" who kept them afloat. 

By 1997, following the massive commercial success of *Hard Reset*, Engine Room Records finally possessed the capital to professionally press and globally distribute *The Warehouse Tapes* to retail. Placing *Static* right in the middle of this "Greatest Hits" album is a victory lap. The rallying cry—"I am the static! I am the noise!"—is no longer an underground secret; it is a global broadcast.

You tried to tune me out, you tried to change the dial
But I've been waiting all this while

I am the static!
The feedback wail!
The story that you tried to derail!

(Verse 2)

This is the sound of a different place
This is the heart you tried to hide
The part of us that never died

(Chorus)
I am the static! I am the noise!

I am the static!
The feedback wail!
The story that you tried to derail!

(Guitar Solo)

I am the static!
The feedback wail!
The story that you tried to derail!

(Outro)
I am the static...
I am the static...
Can you hear me now?
## Garbage
### Lore
Continuing Act III ("The Wilderness"), *Garbage* is Ryan O'Connell’s unfiltered, jangle-rock middle finger to the corporate machine. The track immortalizes the infamous "dirty mirror" incident from the disastrous 1992 *Friction* photo shoot. 

During the shoot, an Apex Records executive attempted to offer Ryan cocaine on a silver-plated mirror. Ryan—who takes things quite literally—innocently assumed the mirror was just dirty and wiped the expensive powder away with a napkin. When he later realized he was being mocked, and that the executive was actively trying to drag his family into the industry's toxic underbelly, Ryan channeled his fury into this blistering, two-and-a-half-minute track. 

In 1995, singing these lyrics felt like a private vent session shared only with the hardcore "Ironheads" via mail-order cassette. Included here on this 1997 compilation, it stands as a globally broadcast, platinum-certified humiliation of the very executive who tried, and failed, to break the Engine.

You thought you'd open up the gate
You showed me powder on a glass
You thought you had a man of class

(Chorus)
It was just dust! It was just a lie!
So I wiped it clean, and I watched you die
You called it 'art', you called it 'style'
It was just garbage in a filthy pile!

(Verse 2)
You held your clipboard like a gun
You thought the battle was already won
You crossed the line, you showed your hand
You'll never, ever understand

(Chorus)
It was just dust! It was just a lie!
So I wiped it clean, and I watched you die
You called it 'art', you called it 'style'
It was just garbage in a filthy pile!

(Guitar Solo)

(Chorus)
It was just dust! It was just a lie!
So I wiped it clean, and I watched you die
You called it 'art', 'cuz you're a fake
It was just garbage, make no mistake!
## Escape Velocity (Ad Astra) (Radio Edit)
### Lore
Closing out Act III ("The Wilderness") is the necessary evil of this compilation. This 5:10 "Radio Edit" is the version of *Escape Velocity* that actually charted and introduced millions of new fans to the band's independent sound, but there is one crucial caveat: **The band absolutely hates it.**

The true, unedited version of *Escape Velocity (Ad Astra)* is a sprawling, 15-minute and 33-second progressive rock suite. It is Cassidy O'Connell's literal autobiography. She wrote every single lyric, composed every note, and painstakingly directed her family through the massive arrangement. She considers the full, uncut suite to be the most important piece in her professional repertoire. 

The creation of this radio edit was actually an act of fierce, protective sisterly love. Manager Holly O'Connell—Cassidy’s younger sister—knew that mainstream rock radio would never play a 15-minute track. Desperate to ensure that Cassidy's story of surviving the 'Friction' trial actually reached the masses, Holly made the agonizing, pragmatic choice to cut a condensed "Trojan Horse" edit for promotional CDs. 

Her strategy worked brilliantly, saving the band's career, but it effectively butchered Cassidy's masterpiece, stripping away the ambient space walks and emotional depth just to fit a radio format. The band included it on this compilation as a historical artifact of their survival, accompanied by a solemn vow: they will *never* perform this truncated 5:10 version live.

The noise outside is closing in
I'm running from the face I see
The "victim-saint" they made of me
This rage is just a heavy stone
A gravity I've outgrown
Shedding skin, shedding weight
Escaping from this world of hate

[Part II: Ignition (Ad Astra)]
(A driving, 80s analog synth sequencer starts, bass and drums kick in)
(Vocals become strong and melodic)
I see a light in the endless black
A signal calling, no turning back
The pull of the old world fades away
A new horizon, a new day

(Chorus)
(Full band, soaring ethereal vocals)
I'm climbing, climbing
Past the point of no return
I'm flying, flying
With a fire I let burn
Just a signal, a distant light
Burning through the endless night
Ad astra... (To the stars!)

[Part III: The Cosmos (Loved by the Sun)]
(Music becomes vast, atmospheric, and beautiful. Grand, sweeping synth pads and melodic guitar)
I'm floating now, the engine's off
The silence is a holy sound
The rage is gone, the fear is lost
In this new universe I've found

I'm loved by the sun
I'm held by the void
An orbit just begun
A life not destroyed
And the stars are singing
A song I understand
I'm finally, finally
Reaching for your hand

(Instrumental Bridge)
(A long, melodic, soaring electric guitar solo, full of emotion and hope)

[Part IV: Re-entry (The New World)]
(Music slowly fades back to just the shimmering synth pads and ethereal, whispered vocals)
The walls are gone
The air is clear
There is no noise
There is no fear
Ad astra...
Ad astra...
(fades out)
## The Fortress
### Lore
Serving as the ultimate bridge between the desperation of "The Wilderness" and their fully funded "Comeback," *The Fortress* is the band's raw, arena-rock love letter to the fans who kept them alive. 

During the lean years of 1995, well before the lottery windfall that secured their permanent independence, The Stardust Engine survived entirely on the grassroots support of their underground fanbase. The "Ironheads" effectively crowdfunded the band's legal defense and studio rent through the "Forger Nation War Chest." 

When Ryan and Cassidy recorded this in 1995, "The Fortress" was a literal description of their freezing, concrete warehouse studio ("This isn't L.A., this isn't a dream / This is a warehouse, this is a scream"). Placed here on the 1997 compilation, the lyrics take on a much grander scale. It stands as an eternal monument to the fans: the "Fortress" is the impenetrable wall the audience built around the band, giving them the safety they needed to finally start over.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
The world was burning, the lies were loud
We were the joke in the tabloid crowd
We had no shelter, we had no home
Just the open road, and a telephone

(Chorus)
But you, you built a fortress
You gave us walls when we were torn
You built a place to hide from the storm
You are the "Ironheads"!
You are the shield!
The only army that would not yield!
This is our fortress!

(Verse 2)
This isn't L.A., this isn't a dream
This is a warehouse, this is a scream
We're building our truth, we're building our sound
Right here on our hallowed ground

(Chorus)
'Cause you, you built a fortress
You gave us walls when we were torn
You built a place to hide from the storm
You are the "Ironheads"!
You are the shield!
The only army that would not yield!
This is our fortress!

(Guitar Solo)

(Outro)
Our fortress...
Our home...
We are the Forgers!
(fades out)
## No Hidden Gravity
### Lore
Released just last year on their 1996 independent comeback release, *Escape Velocity (Ad Astra)*, this massive, booming arena rock power ballad proves the band's songwriting has only sharpened since escaping their label. It completely subverts the "fatal attraction" tropes that dominated the 1980s; instead of a standard romance, this track is fiercely dedicated to an unbreakable, purely platonic bond. 

The band wrote this song after hearing the story of two best friends from the historic Ghent neighborhood in Norfolk: Nate and Maya. Maya—deeply genuine, hearing-impaired, and fluent in ASL—became Nate's ultimate "autistic safe person" during a chaotic trip to Washington, D.C. After her lightning-fast reflexes allowed her to stabilize his wheelchair on a broken expansion joint, they escaped a sensory-overload group walk by riding the Capital Metro together.

Society constantly attempts to draw a romantic line between them simply because they are of opposite genders. "No Hidden Gravity" is their booming, gated-snare anthem rejecting that broken ledger. Placed perfectly here in the final act of *Essential Tracks*, it celebrates the profound intimacy of a purely platonic "fortress," proving that the "Freedom Era" Engine is capable of exploring entirely new emotional depths without relying on corporate pop formulas.

**PRODUCTION NOTE:**
**Style:** 1980s power ballad, arena rock, atmospheric analog synth, booming gated-reverb snare, male and female vocals, soaring guitar solo, slow tempo, massive chorus. 
**Exclusions:** Modern pop, auto-tune, EDM, trap beats, lo-fi, bedroom pop, acoustic, unplugged, country, folk, modern R&B, screaming, growling, death metal, heavy metalcore, romantic duet, sensual, love song, muffled vocals, indie pop.

We're breaking the script, we're changing the game
A different kind of fire without the flame!
Yeah, there's no hidden gravity here!

## Thunderhead
### Lore
Kicking off Act IV of the compilation ("The Comeback"), *Thunderhead* represents the fully actualized, uncompromised Stardust Engine. If the "Wilderness" years were about surviving the storm, this track is the sound of the band *becoming* the storm.

Sourced from their triumphant 1997 double album, *Hard Reset*, this track trades the frantic, desperate energy of their earlier rebellion for pure, slow-stomping sonic intimidation. Channeling the sludgy, heavy arena-rock anthems of the 1970s, the band deliberately drops the tempo to create maximum pressure. Lyrically, it is a direct, heavy-handed attack on the pristine, drum-machine-laden production Apex Records forced upon them for years ("You muted the kick / You silenced the crash"). 

Here on *Essential Tracks*, it stands as an unbothered victory lap. It is a heavy, undeniable declaration that the pop-star uniforms are dead, the label's control is broken, and the heavy weather has finally arrived.

## Redline
### Lore
If *Thunderhead* was about dropping the pressure and bringing the slow, sludgy storm, *Redline* is the lightning strike. Pushing Act IV into absolute overdrive, this track is the sonic equivalent of tearing the safety limiters off a massive engine. 

For ten years under Apex Records, the band had "governors" placed on their sound to keep their tempo safe, clean, and radio-friendly. On this track, those limiters are violently ripped out. With their own label and zero corporate oversight to tell them to slow down, Cassidy and Ryan use this blistering tempo to celebrate the dangerous, exhilarating speed of making their own rules. It features one of the most technically demanding instrumental duels between Ryan's guitar and Cassidy's synthesizer in their entire catalog, proving that they are more than willing to push the band to the absolute edge of control.

*(A frantic, double-kick drum intro from Tyler blasts through the speakers, immediately chased by a shredding, high-speed rock riff. The tempo is dangerously fast, practically daring the listener to keep up.)*

## Moon 1 (Tidal Lock)
### Lore
As the compilation approaches its finale, *Moon 1 (Tidal Lock)* proves that The Stardust Engine didn't simply destroy their pop past—they reclaimed it. Unabashedly Cassidy’s favorite song from their 1997 comeback album, *Hard Reset*, this track is a brilliant, uncompromised execution of pure 1980s synth-pop, but transposed entirely into the deep cosmos. 

For ten years, Apex Records tried to force the band into a "safe," terrestrial pop box. Here, Cassidy takes those analog synths into zero-gravity. Lyrically, it uses the astrophysical concept of a "tidal lock"—where a moon is gravitationally bound to always show the exact same face to its planet—as a gorgeous metaphor for the family's unwavering loyalty to one another. It also features a clever lyrical callback to the "anchor" motif from the start of the compilation, proving that their terrestrial past and their independent cosmic future are finally perfectly aligned.

## Echoes in the Wire
### Lore
Placed in the "Comeback" section of the compilation, *Echoes in the Wire* is the ultimate emotional payoff to the "Omission" note found in the album's liner notes. 

During the Apex years, Cassidy was forced to sing the romantic ballad *If You Walked Away*, which she experienced as a terrifying "what-if" scenario of her brother abandoning her. Upon gaining their independence, the band permanently retired that traumatic track. In its place, they wrote this pure, unapologetic 1980s power ballad. Instead of fear and abandonment, *Echoes in the Wire* is about surviving the storm together and leaving a legacy. It transformed a nightly moment of anxiety into a triumphant celebration of the family's unity, proving they didn't need a label to write a massive, stadium-shaking ballad.

We're holding on to what matters most
The silence is a kind of song
Telling us where we belong

Whenever we come down
