Atmospheric entry complete. Gravity is stable. 
On behalf of the crew, we would like to thank you for visiting the Engine Room Records Planetarium. 
The simulation is now concluded. 
Please exit safely, and welcome home.

[End]
# Sanctuary (Zero-G) (2003)
* **Narrative Era:** 2003
* **Real-World DSP Release:** 2026-08-17

## Moon 1 (Tidal Lock)
### Lore
As the opening track of her 2003 solo orbit, *Sanctuary (Zero-G)*, "Moon 1 (Tidal Lock)" expands from a single standout deep-cut into the foundational thesis of Cassidy's entire musical architecture. This is the ultimate, undiluted presentation of **The Cosmic Tidal Lock Sound™**. By kicking off the album with this 1980s Cosmic Pop masterpiece, she establishes the zero-gravity environment for the rest of the record—a sanctuary built on unwavering loyalty, sweeping spatial reverb, and mathematically perfect analog synthesizers. It is the sound of the "Anchor" finding her perfect, peaceful center of gravity.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
*(A tight, driving 1980s drum beat enters. A pulsing synth bassline locks into the rhythm, perfectly anchoring the track as shimmering analog synth pads slowly rise in the background.)*

[Verse 1]
We crossed the line into the dark
Left the atmosphere, a fading spark
But I can feel the pull of where you are
A heavy gravity from a distant star
They tried to spin us out of control
But we found the center, we found the soul

[Pre-Chorus]
The telemetry is clear tonight
Half in the shadow, half in the light
I'm caught in the orbit, spinning around
A million miles above the ground

[Chorus]
And I'm in a tidal lock with you!
Suspended in the velvet and the blue
No matter how I turn, no matter where I fly
You are the only anchor in my sky
Yeah, I'm in a tidal lock with you!

[Verse 2]
The radio is quiet, the signal is dead
But I hear the rhythm inside my head
We don't need the sun to show us the way
We left the noise of the terrestrial day
We're charting the math that they couldn't see
Perfect alignment in the zero-G

[Pre-Chorus]
The telemetry is clear tonight
Half in the shadow, half in the light
I'm caught in the orbit, spinning around
A million miles above the ground

[Chorus]
And I'm in a tidal lock with you!
Suspended in the velvet and the blue
No matter how I turn, no matter where I fly
You are the only anchor in my sky
Yeah, I'm in a tidal lock with you!

[Instrumental Spacewalk]
*(Evan steps into the spotlight, playing a melodic, sliding fretless bass solo over Tyler's driving drum beat. Cassidy's analog synths sparkle in the background like passing satellites, creating a lush, unmistakably 80s soundscape.)*

[Chorus]
'Cause I'm in a tidal lock with you!
Suspended in the velvet and the blue
No matter how I turn, no matter where I fly
You are the only anchor in my sky
Yeah, I'm in a tidal lock with you!

[Outro]
Half in the shadow.
Half in the light.
Tidal lock.
*(The synths sweep upward, echoing out into the void as the driving drum beat slowly fades away into the starfield)*
## Periapsis (Closest Approach)
### Lore
Following the mid-tempo, sweeping introduction of the title track, *Periapsis (Closest Approach)* immediately hits the accelerator. With *Moon 1 (Tidal Lock)* acting as the prologue, this track officially welcomes the listener to the *Sanctuary* exhibition with a 1990s planetarium documentary introduction. Engineered to showcase the kinetic side of **The Cosmic Tidal Lock Sound**, this track is driven by a relentless 16th-note analog synth sequencer and a crisp, tight drum machine. Lyrically, Cassidy uses the concept of an "orbital slingshot" to describe stealing momentum from a massive, dangerous gravitational body (like her terrestrial past) just to propel herself further into deep space. It is a brilliant, high-speed 80s synth-pop rush.

### Lyrics

[Spoken Intro]
*(Spoken clearly, calmly, and methodically, like a narrator in a planetarium documentary. A fast-pulsing electronic hum begins to build.)*

This is the point in our orbit closest to the parent body, where gravitational pull is strongest and our velocity reaches its absolute maximum. Please remain seated as we initiate the orbital slingshot maneuver.

[Intro]
*(Instrumental - The track immediately blasts off with a fast, pulsing 16th-note analog synthesizer sequencer. A crisp, highly quantized drum machine locks in, setting a dangerously fast, driving tempo before Cassidy's vocals cut in, drenched in spatial reverb.)*

I'm dropping down the gravity wells

Stealing all the giant's power!

[Chorus]

Flying faster than I've ever known
Closest approach, scraping the sky
Using the pull just to watch it fly by
We're taking the momentum and breaking the route
Periapsis! Riding the slingshot out!

[Verse 2]
The atmosphere is brushing the glass
A blur of violet as I pass
I feel the friction start to heat
But I am perfectly on the beat
They thought the gravity would hold me down
But I'm just using it to leave this town

[Pre-Chorus]
The sequencer is running hot
I'm lining up the perfect shot
A hundred thousand miles an hour
Stealing all the giant's power!

[Chorus]
Periapsis! Cutting through the zone
Flying faster than I've ever known
Closest approach, scraping the sky
Using the pull just to watch it fly by
We're taking the momentum and breaking the route
Periapsis! Riding the slingshot out!

[Bridge]
*(The driving drum machine drops out. The rapid sequencer continues alone, creating a feeling of breathless suspension at the very bottom of the orbital curve.)*
Hold your breath at the edge of the wire
Don't look down at the atmospheric fire
Feel the apex.
Hold the nerve.
Slingshot... release!

[Synth Solo]
*(Instrumental - The drums slam back in. Cassidy delivers a blistering, high-speed arpeggiated synthesizer solo that cascades over the driving beat, simulating the feeling of absolute velocity.)*

Flying faster than I've ever known
Closest approach, scraping the sky
Using the pull just to watch it fly by
We're taking the momentum and breaking the route
Periapsis! Riding the slingshot out!

[Outro]
Riding the slingshot out.
Maximum velocity.
Periapsis.
*(The vocals echo into the distance as the fast sequencer slowly fades out, simulating a ship disappearing into the black)*
## The Anchor's Weight
### Lore
Following the high-speed slingshot of *Periapsis*, the album settles into its first true atmospheric ballad. *The Anchor's Weight* explores the psychological concept of "phantom gravity." Even while floating in the absolute safety of her zero-g sanctuary, Cassidy still feels the residual, ghostly weight of the burdens she carried on Earth. 

Lyrically, it explicitly reflects on the 1992 *Friction* trial, viewing the trauma from a million miles away. It isn't a song of anger; it is a melancholic but ultimately peaceful acceptance that while the heavy chains have been cut, the metal still remembers the weight. Musically, it is a flawless execution of the "Studio Master" prompt, leaning heavily into Cassidy's classical piano training layered beneath thick, shimmering analog synths.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
*(A slow, echoing classical piano chord rings out, dripping in spatial reverb. A steady, mid-tempo 1980s drum machine kicks in, accompanied by a deep, melodic fretless bassline.)*

[Verse 1]

I can see the storms where we used to stay
In the silence of the vacuum, the air is perfectly still
But my shoulders remember the heavy hill
I'm floating in the velvet, I'm drifting in the night
But my hands still brace for the sudden fight

[Pre-Chorus]
It's a phantom gravity, pulling at my mind
A tether to the world that I left behind

[Chorus]
I still feel the anchor's weight
Even when the chains are gone
I still feel the hands of fate
Pushing me to carry on
It doesn't hold me down, it doesn't drag me deep
But it's a memory the metal wants to keep
The anchor's weight

[Verse 2]
The flashes in the courtroom, the yelling in the hall
I watch it like a movie projected on the wall
The fire doesn't burn me, the friction isn't real
But the ghost of the pressure is something I can feel
We cut the line and fired up the drive
But the echo of the cage is keeping it alive

[Pre-Chorus]
It's a phantom gravity, pulling at my mind
A tether to the world that I left behind

[Chorus]
I still feel the anchor's weight
Even when the chains are gone
I still feel the hands of fate
Pushing me to carry on
It doesn't hold me down, it doesn't drag me deep
But it's a memory the metal wants to keep
The anchor's weight

[Piano & Synth Solo]
*(The rhythm section pulls back. A beautiful, melancholic interplay between Cassidy's classical acoustic piano and sweeping, cosmic analog synthesizers. It sounds like making peace with the past.)*

[Bridge]
I don't need to fight it, I don't need to run
I'm standing in the light of a different sun
The metal is cold, the sanctuary is clear
I am the anchor, and I am safe out here

[Chorus]
I still feel the anchor's weight
Even when the chains are gone
I still feel the hands of fate
Pushing me to carry on
It doesn't hold me down, it doesn't drag me deep
But it's a memory the metal wants to keep
The anchor's weight

[Outro]
Safe out here.
The phantom gravity.
The anchor's weight.
*(The drum machine stops. A final, sustained piano chord echoes out into the lush spatial reverb, slowly fading into total silence.)*
## Silent Ocean (Mare Tranquillitatis)
### Lore
After the emotional reflection of *The Anchor's Weight*, the album drastically pulls back the tempo and descends into the Sea of Tranquility. *Silent Ocean (Mare Tranquillitatis)* is the sound of absolute, crushing isolation. 

The rhythm section is stripped down to nothing but sparse, echoing electronic percussion and a deep sub-bass that rattles the listener's chest. It is deeply melancholic and beautiful, capturing the overwhelming, terrifying scale of the universe when you are floating alone in the dark. It is a true "Vacuum Ballad."

### Lyrics

*(A slow, rhythmic electronic pulse mimicking a fading heartbeat. A massive, deep sub-bass rumbles underneath. The spatial reverb is cranked to maximum, creating an ocean of empty, terrifying space.)*

## Deep Pressure
### Lore
If *Silent Ocean* was about the terrifying emptiness of space, *Deep Pressure* is about the overwhelming comfort of a manufactured atmosphere. Operating as a direct cosmic sequel to the B-Side *No Hidden Gravity*, this track internalizes the concept of the "autistic safe person." 

Musically, it is the sonic equivalent of a weighted blanket. The mix is incredibly dense, pushing the bass and mid-tones to the front while dialing back the sharp, piercing highs. Cassidy's vocals are layered and harmonized to sound like they are wrapping around the listener. It transforms the concept of heavy gravity from a destructive force into a protective, sensory safe space.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
*(A warm, thick analog bass synthesizer fades in, vibrating with a soothing, low-frequency hum. Slow, heavy, and muffled drum beats establish a heartbeat rhythm. The synths are dense, completely filling the stereo field like a heavy velvet blanket.)*

A million sharp lasers cutting the night
The alarms on the console are flashing in red

Turn up the gravity, lower the sound
Keep my boots heavy right here on the ground

[Chorus]
Give me that deep pressure, wrapping around
A sanctuary built in the underground
I need the weight, I need the heavy hold
To keep out the static, to keep out the cold
A protective atmosphere, dense and secure
Give me that deep pressure, perfect and pure

[Verse 2]

The panic is over, the sanctuary has come

Keep my boots heavy right here on the ground

[Chorus]

A sanctuary built in the underground

*(The vocals drop out. The track leans entirely into the heavy, enveloping bassline and slow, muted drum beat. Shimmering, warm analog synths wash over the track in waves, specifically engineered to sound soothing, rhythmic, and incredibly dense.)*

[Chorus]
Give me that deep pressure, wrapping around
A sanctuary built in the underground
I need the weight, I need the heavy hold
To keep out the static, to keep out the cold
A protective atmosphere, dense and secure
Give me that deep pressure, perfect and pure

[Outro]

*(The heavy, warm bass hum continues, slowing down its vibration until it seamlessly melts into a comfortable, quiet silence)*
## Synchronous
### Lore
Positioned just past the halfway mark of the CD, *Synchronous* is Cassidy’s 7-minute progressive instrumental magnum opus. In astrophysics, a tidal lock is also known as "synchronous rotation," representing perfect, mathematical alignment. 

Lyrically sparse, the track relies entirely on Cassidy's virtuosic classical piano training layered over massive, sweeping analog synthesizers. It is an intricate, building instrumental suite that maps out the orbital mechanics of the band's independent empire. It pushes the physical limits of the CD format and the emotional limits of The Cosmic Tidal Lock Sound®.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
*(A single, crystal-clear classical piano note rings out in a massive, empty acoustic space. Slowly, a complex, mathematically precise piano arpeggio begins to build. Swelling analog synthesizers slowly rise from the floor, wrapping around the piano notes.)*

[Verse 1]
*(Sung very softly, ethereal and echoing)*
Align the axis.
Match the spin.
Where the old world ends...
The new world begins.

[Instrumental Movement I: The Ascent]
*(The 1980s drum machine enters with a slow, deliberate, and towering gated-reverb snare. The piano arpeggios become faster and more complex. Lush, sweeping synth-brass pads widen the stereo field to maximum capacity.)*

[Chorus]
*(Ethereal, wordless female choir vocals soaring over the heavy instrumental)*
Ahhh...
Synchronous...
Perfect alignment in the dark...
Ahhh...

[Instrumental Movement II: The Mathematics]
*(The heavy drums drop out. A rapid-fire, 16th-note analog sequencer takes over, weaving in and out of the classical piano lines. It sounds like a supercomputer calculating infinite orbital trajectories in deep space. Complex, mesmerizing, and purely 1980s.)*

[Verse 2]
*(Sung softly, floating above the sequencer)*
Gravity captured.
The orbit is locked.
The hands are removed...
From the face of the clock.

[Instrumental Movement III: The Crescendo]
*(The heavy, towering stadium drums slam back into the mix. A massive, melodic fretless bassline drives the rhythm forward. Cassidy unleashes a soaring, virtuosic synthesizer solo that interlocks perfectly with the classical piano. It is a massive wall of sound—the ultimate realization of the Zero-G sanctuary.)*

[Chorus]
*(Full volume, layered, triumphant female vocals)*
Ahhh...
Synchronous...
Perfect alignment in the dark!
Ahhh...
Synchronous!

[Outro]
*(The towering wall of sound suddenly cuts out, leaving only the complex, mathematical piano arpeggio playing softly by itself. The piano slowly slows down its tempo, echoing out into the vast, silent void of space before fading completely.)*
## The Penumbra
### Lore
Exiting the mathematical perfection of *Synchronous*, the orbit inevitably carries the satellite into the shadow of the parent planet. *The Penumbra* is undeniably the coldest, most brooding track on *Sanctuary (Zero-G)*. 

Bringing back the 1990s planetarium narrator from *Silent Ocean*, the track opens with an educational but ominous warning about the nature of a celestial shadow. It then drops into a brooding, industrial-tinged piece of darkwave. Driven by an unrelenting, mechanical drum beat and icy, piercing analog leads, it explores the sudden fear of the unknown when the light source is cut off and the solar batteries begin to drain in the freezing vacuum of space.

### Lyrics

[Spoken Intro]
*(Spoken clearly, calmly, and methodically, like a narrator in a planetarium documentary. A low, freezing, metallic drone hums in the background.)*

We are now entering the penumbra—the outer edge of the celestial shadow. 
The temperature will begin to drop rapidly as the light is obscured. 
Solar efficiency will fall to critical levels. 
Prepare for deep space isolation as we cross the threshold into total darkness.

[Intro]
*(The narration ends. A harsh, metallic, mechanical drum machine locks into a rigid mid-tempo beat. A heavy, distorted fretless bassline drones underneath. Cold, icy analog synthesizers slowly creep into the mix, dripping in a hollow, isolating spatial reverb.)*

## Goldilocks Zone
### Lore
Exiting the freezing shadow of *The Penumbra*, the satellite finally breaks back into the light. *Goldilocks Zone* is the triumphant return of the bright, shimmering pop energy that opened the album. 

In astronomy, the Goldilocks Zone is the habitable area around a star where the temperature is just right for liquid water to exist. Lyrically, Cassidy uses this as a metaphor for finally finding the perfect, healthy distance from the trauma of her past—close enough to feel the warmth, but far enough away to avoid burning up. It is a joyous, upbeat 1980s synth-pop anthem of survival and stability.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
*(A bright, shimmering analog synthesizer cuts through the darkness like a sudden burst of sunlight. A driving, upbeat 4/4 drum machine kicks in, accompanied by a bouncy, melodic fretless bassline. The energy is instantly warm, uplifting, and victorious.)*

[Verse 1]
I was freezing in the shadow, losing all the light
Drifting in the vacuum of a never-ending night
But the orbit kept on turning, the mathematics held
The ice upon the solar panels slowly starts to melt
I can see the star rising over the horizon line
Everything is nominal, the telemetry is fine

[Pre-Chorus]
Not too close to the atmospheric fire
Not too far in the deep space wire
I found the perfect circle, the habitable ring
I can feel the warmth making the metal sing!

[Chorus]
I'm living in the Goldilocks Zone!
The perfect little orbit that I call my own
No more freezing in the dark, no more burning in the sun
The calculations are complete, the heavy work is done
I'm breathing in the air, I'm feeling so alive
I found the perfect distance to survive!
Right here in the Goldilocks Zone!

[Verse 2]
I used to think the gravity was going to pull me down
Or I'd be lost in the empty where there isn't any sound
But there's a narrow little window where the water doesn't freeze
Where you can take the heavy spacesuit off and finally catch a breeze
I'm planting down a flag in the middle of the gold
A sanctuary story that is waiting to be told

[Pre-Chorus]
Not too close to the atmospheric fire
Not too far in the deep space wire
I found the perfect circle, the habitable ring
I can feel the warmth making the metal sing!

[Chorus]
I'm living in the Goldilocks Zone!
The perfect little orbit that I call my own
No more freezing in the dark, no more burning in the sun
The calculations are complete, the heavy work is done
I'm breathing in the air, I'm feeling so alive
I found the perfect distance to survive!
Right here in the Goldilocks Zone!

[Fretless Bass & Synth Solo]
*(The driving drum beat keeps the energy high. Evan’s fretless bass takes the lead, sliding and bouncing with incredible groove. Cassidy layers bright, soaring synth-brass chords over the top, creating a massive, triumphant wall of 80s pop sound.)*

[Bridge]
The temperature is holding.
The pressure is exact.
The sanctuary is open.
And I'm never going back!

[Chorus]
I'm living in the Goldilocks Zone!
The perfect little orbit that I call my own
No more freezing in the dark, no more burning in the sun
The calculations are complete, the heavy work is done
I'm breathing in the air, I'm feeling so alive
I found the perfect distance to survive!
Right here in the Goldilocks Zone!

[Outro]
The perfect distance.
Right here in the gold.
Never going back.
*(The upbeat drum beat and bright synths slowly fade out, leaving a warm, comfortable echoing hum)*
## Telemetry
### Lore
Safely established in her habitable orbit, Cassidy finally turns her attention outward. *Telemetry* is a tight, kinetic, experimental 80s pop track that represents her communicating back to Ryan on his parallel, destructive orbit (*Mile Marker 98*). 

Relying heavily on rhythmic, pulsing analog synthesizers engineered to sound like radar pings and data transmissions, the track feels like a high-speed data stream shooting across the void. It serves as the narrative bridge between the two albums, acknowledging the "Engine" without letting his gravity pull her out of the "Anchor's" sanctuary. 

### Lyrics

[Intro]
*(A fast, rhythmic sequence of high-pitched analog synth pings mimicking a radar sweep. A crisp, driving 16th-note drum machine snaps in, layered with a tight, pulsating bassline. It is fast, highly quantized, and purely electronic.)*

[Verse 1]
I'm typing out the coordinates on the terminal screen
The cleanest set of numbers that I've ever seen
I know you're out there burning in the asteroid field
I know you're pushing hard against the broken shield
But I am standing in the center of the quiet and the clear
I'm sending out a signal just to let you know I'm here

[Pre-Chorus]
The radar is sweeping, the sequence is locked
The data is streaming, the channel is docked
Across the dark vacuum, across the great divide
A burst of information from the other side!

[Chorus]
Telemetry! Pinging in the dark!
Shooting out a message like a digital spark
Telemetry! Reading out the lines!
Looking at the numbers and decoding the signs
I won't let you pull me, I won't let you drag me down
But I'll send you the telemetry from my side of town!

[Verse 2]
I can see your friction burning up the atmosphere
You're running on the edge, you're running on the gear
But I am in the Goldilocks, the temperature is right
I'm just a glowing beacon in the middle of your night
Take the data, read the math, adjust your heavy spin
But I am keeping the perimeter, you cannot enter in

[Pre-Chorus]
The radar is sweeping, the sequence is locked
The data is streaming, the channel is docked
Across the dark vacuum, across the great divide
A burst of information from the other side!

[Chorus]
Telemetry! Pinging in the dark!
Shooting out a message like a digital spark
Telemetry! Reading out the lines!
Looking at the numbers and decoding the signs
I won't let you pull me, I won't let you drag me down
But I'll send you the telemetry from my side of town!

[Synthesizer Solo]
*(The driving beat stays completely rigid. Cassidy layers a fast, overlapping series of staccato synthesizer lines that sound like two supercomputers rapidly trading encrypted data back and forth.)*

[Bridge]
Signal out.
Signal received.
The distance is maintained.
The sanctuary achieved.

[Chorus]
Telemetry! Pinging in the dark!
Shooting out a message like a digital spark
Telemetry! Reading out the lines!
Looking at the numbers and decoding the signs
I won't let you pull me, I won't let you drag me down
But I'll send you the telemetry from my side of town!

[Outro]
Signal out.
End transmission.
Telemetry.
*(The drum machine cuts out instantly. The high-pitched radar pings continue for three more sweeps before shutting down, leaving pure silence)*
## Sanctuary: Zero G
### Lore
The epic 74-minute journey reaches its cinematic conclusion. *Sanctuary (Zero-G)* pushes the physical limitations of the CD format and the emotional boundaries of The Cosmic Tidal Lock Sound®. 

Serving as the climax to Cassidy's solo orbit, this massive, symphonic track introduces a male vocal into the mix. Representing the intercepted transmission from Ryan's parallel *Mile Marker 98* orbit, the sibling voices blend and trade lines organically. To simulate the impact of the crash, Ryan's initial transmission is heavily distorted and unintelligible, breaking through the absolute silence of Cassidy's orbit. The album then beautifully closes with the 1990s planetarium narrator bringing the documentary exhibition to an end.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
*(A single, echoing piano chord strikes. A massive, towering wall of analog synth brass and sweeping spatial reverb slowly rises from the silence. The stadium-sized gated drum machine enters with a thunderous, deliberate 4/4 march.)*

*(Muffled, indistinguishable male shouting)*
*(Radio static crackles violently)*

Two different orbits, two different speeds

[Chorus]
Welcome to the Sanctuary, welcome to the clear

Suspended in the silence, suspended in the deep
A promise that the metal and the memory will keep
We are spinning in the dark, we are flying through the sea
Floating forever in the Sanctuary!

[Radio Transmission: Fading Distortion]
[Male Rock Vocal]
I hear the heavy static of a broken engine roar
I hear the heavy knocking on the heavy blast door
You are burning up the friction in a fatal asteroid ring
[Clear Female Lead]
While I am in the silence listening to the planets sing
[Male and Female Duet]
But the radio is open and the frequency is shared
A twin moon system perfectly paired

[Pre-Chorus]
The signal is crossing the great divide
A ghost in the machine from the other side
Two different orbits, two different speeds
But the mathematics give us everything we need

[Chorus]
Welcome to the Sanctuary, welcome to the clear
The zero-G is perfect and there is no friction here
Suspended in the silence, suspended in the deep
A promise that the metal and the memory will keep
We are spinning in the dark, we are flying through the sea
Floating forever in the Sanctuary!

[Symphonic Crescendo]
*(The ultimate realization of The Cosmic Tidal Lock Sound. The classical piano arpeggios spiral upwards at lightning speed while massive, soaring synth-brass chords widen the stereo field. The massive stadium drums hit with earth-shattering force. A wordless, layered choir of male and female voices soars above the instrumentation.)*

[Chorus]
Welcome to the Sanctuary, welcome to the clear
The zero-G is perfect and there is no friction here
Suspended in the silence, suspended in the deep
A promise that the metal and the memory will keep
We are spinning in the dark, we are flying through the sea

[Outro]
*(The massive wall of sound suddenly cuts out, leaving only a slow, beautiful, echoing piano line that slowly fades into total silence. A low, humming electronic drone remains.)*

[Spoken Outro]
*(Spoken clearly and calmly by the planetarium documentary narrator)*
This concludes our journey through the Zero-G Sanctuary. 
We have explored the physics of the tidal lock, the crushing isolation of the silent ocean, and the delicate balance of the Goldilocks zone. 
While the vacuum of space is vast and unforgiving, mathematical perfection provides a permanent, weightless refuge. 
Please gather your belongings as the house lights return. 
Thank you for visiting the Engine Room Records Planetarium. 
Safe travels on your return to terrestrial gravity.
*(The electronic drone completely fades out)*
# Progress Street (2004)
* **Narrative Era:** 2004
* **Real-World DSP Release:** 2026-10-15

## Progress Street: Where the Fire Began
### Lore
The triumphant 1980s AOR title track and opener looking back to 1985 on Progress Street NE in Blacksburg, Virginia—where Ryan, Cassidy, Evan, Tyler, and Holly forged their family unit. Freed from the synth-pop restrictions of their old label, the band opens *Progress Street* with the full-blooded 1980s melodic stadium rock sound they originally played in their Blacksburg basement.

### Lyrics

(Bright, triumphant 1980s analog synthesizer fanfare)
(Massive pounding gated-reverb snare drum roll and driving bassline kick in)
(Melodic electric guitar harmonizes with the keyboard riff)

Autumn wind blowing through the college days
Two amps humming through a plaster wall
Chasing down a dream down a narrow hall
(Cassidy)

Tangled up cables on the living room floor
We didn't have a dime, we didn't have a plan
Just a family promise where the fire began!

(Ryan)

(Cassidy)

(Ryan)

(Cassidy)
Take us back to where we started then!

(Duet)

We were building up a world behind a locked front door!

Underneath the streetlights that will never fade!

(Ryan)

(Ryan)

(Cassidy)

[Chorus]
(Duet)

We were building up a world behind a locked front door!

(Duet)
Five hearts beating as one!
(Triumphant final stadium rock chord and massive drum crash fade out)


## Route 1: Midnight Headlights
### Lore
A nocturnal 1980s melodic highway rocker paying tribute to riding Blue Ridge Transit's Route 1 down Main Street and Business 460 through the New River Valley fog. Driven by Evan's eighth-note bassline, Cassidy's punchy analog synth stabs, and Ryan's soaring guitar work, it celebrates the reliable fifteen-minute pulse of the local transit corridor that carried them through their CPI years.

## Seconds Into Forever
### Lore
A lush, three-minute 85 BPM 1980s arena power ballad written around the playful concept of a three-minute campus speed-dating round ("It only takes three minutes to believe!"). You have until the end of the song to get to know the person sitting across the table from you before rotating to the next seat. In The Stardust Engine's official published liner-note narrative ("Before the Distance: The Story of John & Erin"), this is the song that was supposed to be played at the campus speed-dating event when John sat down across from a stranger—before the organizer accidentally hit the skip button and blasted Track 4 ("Thunder in the Rain") instead.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Lush, sparkling synthesizer pads)
(Clean electric guitar arpeggios)

[Verse 1]
The clock is ticking on the classroom wall
But time stands still here in the hall
I saw your face and the world went blurred
The sweetest song that I ever heard
No need to run and no need to hide
I'm pulling up a chair right by your side

[Pre-Chorus]
They say that lightning strikes and then it's gone
But we're the glow before the dawn
Yeah, we're the light that's moving on!

[Chorus]
It only takes three minutes to believe!
In the magic that we're starting to weave
It's a heart-to-heart, it's a brand new start
Tearing the shadows of the night apart
Can you hear it? (I can hear it!)
Can you feel it? (I can feel it!)
It's seconds into forever!

[Guitar Solo]
(Soaring, melodic solo with heavy sustain—NOT aggressive shredding)

[Outro]
(Music swells)
Seconds into forever...
(Fade out on a sustained synth chord)


## Thunder in the Rain
### Lore
Opening with a deceptive ten-second cinematic analog brass-synth swell before exploding into a 150 BPM C-minor stadium rocker. Placed directly after "Seconds Into Forever" as Track 4, this song serves as Part II of The Stardust Engine's published narrative story of fraternal twins John and Erin. At a campus speed-dating event where the organizer was supposed to play "Seconds Into Forever" (Track 3) but accidentally skipped to "Thunder in the Rain" (Track 4), John sits across from a magnetic woman while the lyrics roar ("The neon signs are flashing 'Danger' in red / But I'd rather be living than safe and dead"). Crucially, John mistakes the high-voltage adrenaline for romance and does not realize she is going to be his abuser—a published story detail that later makes Freddie and Paige wonder why they have heard "Thunder in the Rain" before when the song surfaces in their own lives.

## Upright & Amplifier
### Lore
An emotional 1980s AOR power ballad chronicling the band's true origin: Cassidy sitting at the family's upright piano with Ryan playing guitar right beside her in 1983, Tyler bringing his childhood drum kit and Evan plugging in his bass in 1985, and their middle-class family purchasing Cassidy's used 1984 six-voice analog synthesizer (a second-hand analog polyphonic) in 1986.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Warm, intimate upright piano melody in a quiet living room, joined gently by a clean electric guitar sitting right beside the bench)

[Verse 1]
Nineteen eighty-three on a Sunday afternoon
Sunlight slanting through the curtains of the living room
I was sitting at the upright, hands upon the keys
You pulled up a wooden chair and rested your guitar across your knees
No microphones, no stages, just a brother and a sister in the quiet air
Finding out that every single note was lighter when we both were there.

[Verse 2]
(A warm, shimmering 1980s analog chorus synthesizer pad gently fades in beneath the piano)
By eighty-five the cousins came knocking at the door
Tyler hauled his childhood drum kit right across the basement floor
Evan plugged the bass in and the floorboards started shaking with a brand new beat
And in eighty-six we bought that second-hand eighty-four synth from down the street
Six analog voices glowing blue and red in the dark
That was the night the engine caught the spark!

[Chorus]
(Massive 80s gated-reverb stadium drums and soaring electric guitar enter in full power ballad glory)
From an upright piano and a six-string amplifier!
We built a sanctuary out of melody and fire!
Before the record labels, before the stadium lights
It was just the five of us through those Virginia nights!
No matter how far out into the stars we fly
I'm still sitting right beside you as the years go by!

[Verse 3]
Holly at the kitchen table with her notebooks spread around
Drawing up battle plans for a homemade family sound
We didn't need a fortune, we already had the gold
Five kids in a basement with a story to be told!

[Chorus]
From an upright piano and a six-string amplifier!
We built a sanctuary out of melody and fire!
Before the record labels, before the stadium lights
It was just the five of us through those Virginia nights!
No matter how far out into the stars we fly
I'm still sitting right beside you as the years go by!

[Guitar Solo]
(Deeply emotional, soaring 1980s arena power-ballad guitar solo harmonizing with the upright piano and lush analog synth strings)

[Bridge]
(Music strips back down to just the upright piano and clean electric guitar, just like 1983)
Now we have every instrument that money could ever buy
A fortress in the mountains underneath an open sky
But when I close my eyes and let the static fade away
We're back on that piano bench in nineteen eighty-three today.
(Huge gated-reverb drum fill brings the full band roaring back in)

[Final Chorus]
From an upright piano and a six-string amplifier!
We built a sanctuary out of melody and fire!
Before the record labels, before the stadium lights
It was just the five of us through those Virginia nights!
No matter how far out into the stars we fly
I'm still sitting right beside you as the years go by!

[Outro]
(Full band fades down to the gentle upright piano and a single warm guitar chord)
Right beside you on the bench...
Where the music began.
(Final soft piano chord fades out)


## Saturday Night Lights: The Crucible
### Lore
A roaring 1980s stadium rock companion to 1986's "Ignition: The Forger's Call", celebrating the student-run contest at Commonwealth Polytechnic Institute (CPI) that made The Stardust Engine the Saturday night soundtrack of The Crucible—the loudest house in all of college sports.

**In-Universe Legal Clearance (The 1986 Progress Street Redline):** When the CPI Student Government Association and Athletics Department distributed the October 1986 entry packet for the student anthem contest, the standard release form included a boilerplate "Work Made for Hire" clause that would have transferred 100% of the winning song's master and publishing rights to the CPI Athletic Foundation. Sixteen-year-old Holly O'Connell—who was **dual-enrolled at Blacksburg High School and CPI**, riding Blue Ridge Transit Route 1 to the university campus every afternoon to take undergraduate business and contract law electives—refused to let Ryan, Cassidy, Evan, and Tyler sign it. Sitting at the Progress Street kitchen table with a red pen, Holly struck the Work-for-Hire clause and drafted a **$1.00 Perpetual Mutual Cross-License** with CPI's University Counsel:
1. **The Stadium Grant (Band &rarr; CPI):** The Stardust Engine retained 100% ownership of their masters and publishing, while granting CPI Athletics and the Marching Forgers a perpetual, irrevocable, royalty-free synchronization and public-performance license to play *"Ignition: The Forger's Call"* (and future band stadium anthems) at all home games inside **The Crucible** and on official university broadcasts.
2. **The Trademark Grant (CPI &rarr; Band):** In exchange, CPI's Office of Trademarks & Licensing granted The Stardust Engine (and any record label wholly owned by the band) a perpetual, worldwide, royalty-free **Co-Existence & Artistic Reference License** to use the university's institutional marks—**`CPI`**, **`Commonwealth Polytechnic Institute`**, **`The Forgers`**, and **`The Crucible`**—in their song lyrics, track titles, album artwork, and liner notes.

When the band recorded *"Saturday Night Lights: The Crucible"* in 2004 for *Progress Street* (scheduled for worldwide release on **Monday, October 10, 2004**), Holly pulled the 1986 Mutual Cross-License out of the Engine Room Records fireproof vault and staged a top-secret stadium premiere 48 hours before the album dropped. On **Saturday night, October 8, 2004**, as the CPI Forgers held a gritty fourth-quarter lead over fierce Appalachian border rival **#6 Monongahela State University (MSU Coalminers)** in the *Coal & Iron War*, the stadium press box blindsided **The Ironheads** (CPI football's fanatical student section in The Anvil, as famous across college sports as **Buchanan University** basketball's *Pavilion Lunatics*) and all 65,000 fans of Forger Nation by blasting the unreleased track between the third and fourth quarters. The Ironheads immediately locked onto Cassidy's triumphant analog synth-brass fanfare and the anthemic chorus—roaring ***"INSIDE THE CRUCIBLE!"*** and ***"GO FORGERS!"*** across the Blacksburg night sky as CPI closed out a **19–13 victory**, cementing *"Saturday Night Lights: The Crucible"* as CPI's permanent fourth-quarter singalong ritual alongside pre-game *"Ignition."*

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Triumphant 1980s synthesizer brass fanfare over a thunderous, drumline-style stadium cadence)
(Roaring electric guitar riff kicks in with massive gated-reverb drums)

[Verse 1]
(Ryan)
October eighty-six and the mountain air is cold
A student contest flyer printed out in bold
(Cassidy)
They asked for a brand new anthem to shake the autumn night
A song to lead the Forgers out into the stadium light!
(Ryan)
We rolled the tape machine into our basement room
(Cassidy)
And wrote a thunderclap to make the valley boom!

[Pre-Chorus]
(Ryan)
Sixty thousand standing in the maroon and orange sea!
(Cassidy)
Waiting for the signal at CPI with me!
(Ryan)
Feel the concrete tremble underneath your feet!
(Cassidy)
Hear the loudest house in college sports repeat!

[Chorus]
(Duet)
Under the Saturday night lights! Inside The Crucible!
Where the sound of the engine makes us invincible!
Hear the Forger's Call echoing across the sky!
Raise the maroon and orange banners high!
When the tunnel opens up and the speakers roar
We're not just a basement band anymore!

[Verse 2]
(Cassidy)
Tyler on the drums like a marching thunder line
(Ryan)
Evan on the bass keeping clockwork time
(Cassidy)

(Ryan)

(Cassidy)

## Fifth Gear: The Architect's Shield
### Lore

An upbeat 1980s melodic synth-rock tribute to younger sister and manager Holly O'Connell—the academic prodigy who was dual-enrolled at Blacksburg High School and CPI at sixteen, mediated Ryan and Cassidy's creative arguments with three-way hugs, earned her Class A CDL with passenger and airbrake endorsements alongside Evan and Tyler, and armored the family against the music industry.

She pulled us in a three-way hug and made the chaos right!

[Bridge]

## Before the Distance
### Lore
A dramatic mid-tempo 1980s pop-rock power anthem ("When the steel doors close and the platform clears...") serving as the centerpiece of The Stardust Engine's published narrative story of fraternal twins John and Erin. After the woman John met during "Thunder in the Rain" isolates and breaks him down, she tries to force John onto a southbound Blue Ridge Transit bus along Commonwealth Mall in Blacksburg to take him away for the weekend—until his twin sister Erin blocks the front wheelchair ramp with her power chair on the sidewalk, locks a perimeter between her brother and his abuser right before the steel doors close, and brings John the two blocks home down the sidewalk to their apartment on Progress Street NE. In November 2006, when Jessica leaves her brother Michael behind at Kent Station with his abuser Delores and hears "Before the Distance" on her pre-release Quantum Zephyr media player on the commuter train to Seattle King Street Station, she remembers reading The Stardust Engine's story of John and Erin and realizes in horror that she and Michael are living out the exact same story—except she let the doors close without pulling her brother away.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Dramatic synth pad fades in, accompanied by a slow, heavy drum heartbeat)

[Verse 1]
I saw the shadow falling across your face
A quiet surrender in a crowded place
You wore a mask, but the edges were frayed
Lost in the rules of a game that we played
I should have reached out, I should have stood tall
But I let the silence echo down the hall

[Chorus]
When the steel doors close and the platform clears
You can hear the truth grinding in the gears
Heed the warning hiding in his eyes
Before the distance leaves your soul behind
Trust the silence when he calls your name
'Cause once it's gone, nothing stays the same

[Verse 2]
The rain on the window is blurring the lines
Between what is real and what we leave behind
I traded my courage for polite little lies
And watched as the storm gathered dark in your skies
Now I'm riding this track to the end of the day
Wishing to God I had pulled you away

[Chorus]
When the steel doors close and the platform clears
You can hear the truth grinding in the gears
Heed the warning hiding in his eyes
Before the distance leaves your soul behind
Trust the silence when he calls your name
'Cause once it's gone, nothing stays the same

[Bridge]
(Heavy, driving bassline builds with dramatic synthesizers)
Logic is a cage when the danger is real
You can't code the panic, you can't fake what you feel
I'm turning around, I'm breaking the track
Whatever it costs me, I'm taking it back!

[Guitar Solo]
(Soaring, emotional 80s arena-rock guitar solo)

[Chorus]
When the steel doors close and the platform clears
You can hear the truth grinding in the gears
Heed the warning hiding in his eyes
Before the distance leaves your soul behind
Trust the silence when he calls your name
'Cause once it's gone... nothing stays the same

[Outro]
Nothing stays the same.
Listen to the silence.
(Fading synth pad, slow drum beat out)


## Running on Redline
### Lore
A fiery 140 BPM 1980s arena rocker that captures the band pouring every last ounce of energy into their third studio album in under two years (*Sanctuary: Zero-G* and *Mile Marker 98* in 2003, followed immediately by *Progress Street* in 2004), directly foreshadowing the physical and emotional exhaustion that triggered their Burnout Hiatus.

(Urgent, rapid 1980s synthesizer pulse and wailing electric guitar harmony)
(Thunderous gated-reverb drums launch into a fast 140 BPM arena rock groove)

My sister's by my shoulder in the blinding stage flares
How much longer can we burn this bright
Before we run out of the midnight light?

[Chorus]

Pouring every drop of heart into the tape again!
Can you hear the tachometer screaming in the dark?
Giving everything we have to keep the sacred spark!
If this is the last run before we catch our breath
We'll ride the lightning all the way and leave nothing left!
Running on redline!

Flashbulbs in the lobby and a microphone in my face
Living life at double speed in a marathon race
The wheels on my chair are spinning hot upon the floor
One more encore waiting on the other side of the door
We built a cosmic planetarium and touched the solid ground
Now we're burning up the highway with an eighties sound!

[Pre-Chorus]

My sister's by my shoulder in the blinding stage flares
How much longer can we burn this bright
Before we run out of the midnight light?

[Chorus]
We're running on redline! Pushing past the ten!
Pouring every drop of heart into the tape again!
Can you hear the tachometer screaming in the dark?
Giving everything we have to keep the sacred spark!
If this is the last run before we catch our breath
We'll ride the lightning all the way and leave nothing left!
Running on redline!

(Fiery, emotional 1980s arena-rock electric guitar solo with high sustained notes and sweeping synthesizer chords)

[Bridge]
(Half-time drum feel, atmospheric synth pad and clean echoing guitar)
When the engine overheats and the pistons need to cool
You gotta know when to step away and break the industry rule
One more song for the valley... one more song for the street...
Before we pull into the garage and make the circle complete!

## Empty Arenas
### Lore
Originally tracked as a stripped-down, heartbroken acoustic sketch in late 1990 right after The Crash (*"Empty Arenas (Early Demo)"*, later released on 2007's *Lost Sounds*), the band resurrected the song in 2004 for *Progress Street* and transformed it into a massive, full-band 1980s Album-Oriented Rock (AOR) stadium anthem. Driven by Tyler's thunderous gated-reverb acoustic drum kit, Evan's locked-in bass, Cassidy's 1984 analog synth swells and backing harmonies, and Ryan's crying overdriven electric guitar, the polished 2004 lyrics reframe the empty arena: what began in 1990 as a young man's terror that the lights had gone out forever becomes a triumphant post-encore reflection after filling stadiums across North America, bridging the high-speed exhaustion of *"Running on Redline"* directly into the farewell of *"Last Bus Home (Lights Out on Progress Street)"*.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Shimmering 1980s analog synthesizer pad and echoing arpeggiated electric guitar)
(A massive, thunderous gated-reverb acoustic drum fill by Tyler kicks in with driving eighth-note bass and overdriven rhythm guitars!)

[Verse 1]
(Ryan - clear, powerful high-tenor rock vocal over driving drums and palm-muted electric guitar)
The spotlight's cooling, the cables coiled
Fourteen years since the dream was nearly spoiled
Just an echo bouncing off the concrete floor
Road cases rolling through the backstage door
I remember nineteen-ninety in the dark alone
Wondering if this stage would ever be my home

[Pre-Chorus]
(Cassidy joins on soaring harmony; analog synth brass and crashing cymbals build tension)
Twenty thousand voices ringing in my ears
Washing out the static of the wasted years!

[Chorus]
(Full-throttle 1980s AOR stadium rock chorus, heavy gated-reverb drum kit, roaring power chords, Ryan & Cassidy harmonies)
Empty arenas, where the echoes used to die!
Empty arenas, underneath a painted sky!
We came back together and we set this room on fire
Climbed out of the ashes and we rose above the wire!
Now the house lights hum across the floor
In these empty arenas... we're not afraid anymore!

[Verse 2]
(Full acoustic drum groove and melodic bassline lock in with bright 80s synth stabs)
The ticket stubs and confetti swept away
Ghosts of ninety-eight and ghosts of yesterday
I roll up to the edge and look at velvet seats
Listening to the rhythm of five family heartbeats
My sister at the keys and my cousins on the beat
One last look around before the tour's complete!

[Pre-Chorus]
(Guitars and synthesizers swell with pounding snare rolls)
Twenty thousand voices ringing in my ears
Washing out the static of the wasted years!

[Chorus]
(Massive stadium rock chorus)
Empty arenas, where the echoes used to die!
Empty arenas, underneath a painted sky!
We came back together and we set this room on fire
Climbed out of the ashes and we rose above the wire!
Now the house lights hum across the floor
In these empty arenas... we're not afraid anymore!

[Bridge]
(Half-time heavy AOR power groove; emotional electric guitar chords and warm synth strings)
(Ryan)
Back then I asked: will the lights ever come back on?
Or is the final encore already dead and gone?
(Cassidy & Ryan Duet - building to a roar)
If I scream into the rafters of the vast unknown
I hear my family singing back—I was never all alone!
(Massive acoustic drum fill across all toms!)

[Guitar Solo]
(Blistering, emotional 1980s AOR electric guitar solo—soaring sustained bends, melodic dual-guitar harmonies, backed by pounding gated-reverb drums, driving bass, and lush analog synthesizer chords)

[Chorus]

We came back together and we set this room on fire
Climbed out of the ashes and we rose above the wire!
Now the house lights hum across the floor
In these empty arenas... we're not afraid anymore!

[Outro]
(Driving AOR guitar riff trades off with soaring vocals and big drum fills)
We set this room on fire!
Not preaching to the wire!
Let the rafters ring inside these empty arenas!
(Final crashing electric guitar chord, booming drum kit crescendo, and warm analog synth sustain fading into the quiet arena floor)


## Last Bus Home: Lights Out on Progress Street
### Lore
The grand 1980s stadium power-ballad finale of *Progress Street*—written as the band's direct, heartfelt message to Forger Nation that they are signing off for a little while. Completely burned out after pouring everything they had into three full studio albums in under two years, Ryan, Cassidy, Holly, Evan, and Tyler use this closing anthem to reassure the world that they are not breaking up. As their custom 2004 Prairie Coachworks RLFD30 low-floor shuttle coach kneels at the curb on Progress Street NE and the five of them head inside, they promise their fans that, as a family, they are staying together—and that once they have rested and recharged, they will turn the switches back on and return with more music.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Gentle, cinematic grand piano chords layered with a warm 1980s analog synthesizer pad)
(A melodic, crying electric guitar lead plays a nostalgic theme)

The road cases are latched and rolled outside the stage door

Now the highway's calling us to finally take our rest
(Ryan)
Our custom low-floor shuttle bus is kneeling at the curb
(Cassidy)
Leading to a quiet place the static can't disturb.

[Pre-Chorus]
(Ryan)

Five of us together at the end of the line!

[Chorus]
(Massive 80s gated-reverb drums and soaring stadium guitars swell in)

Turning off the amplifiers and the stadium heat!
Lock the front door tight and let the curtains fall
We don't owe the outside world anything at all!
Through every storm we weathered and every song we played
This family is the greatest music that we ever made!

[Verse 2]
(Cassidy)
Unplug the synthesizers, set the guitars in their stands
(Ryan)
Rest the tired voices and the callused, aching hands
(Cassidy)
Five pairs of sneakers lined up by the wooden stairs
(Ryan)
Trading all the crowded rooms for quiet living room chairs
(Cassidy)
We're not fading into nothing, we're just tuning out the noise
(Ryan)
Back to three sisters and brothers and the twin Wright boys!

[Chorus]
(Duet)
We're taking the last bus home! Back to Progress Street!
Turning off the amplifiers and the stadium heat!
Lock the front door tight and let the curtains fall
We don't owe the outside world anything at all!
Through every storm we weathered and every song we played
This family is the greatest music that we ever made!

[Bridge]
(Music softens to warm piano and glowing synth strings)
(Cassidy)
Someday when we're ready, we'll turn the switches on
(Ryan)
And find out that the fire in the engine's never gone
(Cassidy)

(Ryan)

[Guitar Solo]
(Epic, extended, deeply emotional 1980s stadium-rock guitar solo soaring over grand piano chords, lush analog synth pads, and massive slow gated-reverb drums)

Turning off the amplifiers and the stadium heat!

(A warm, sustained piano and analog synth chord slowly fades into silence)

## Impact: Mile Marker 98 (Live in Norfolk)
### Lore
Begins the *Moon 2 (Engine)* segment of the show—Ryan's visceral hard-rock confrontation with the December 10, 1990 Interstate 81 Mile Marker 98 crash.

### Lyrics

### Lore
This track transposes the defining tragedy of the band's history into the cosmos. On December 10, 1990, Ryan was driving the band's 1988 Holt Freighter Van on Interstate 81 Northbound near Exit 98. Attempting to beat a winter storm, he was driving cautiously at 40 MPH with hazard lights active when a speeding commercial sedan lost control on the iced road and clipped the van's rear quarter panel. The collision caused the van to spin off the roadway and roll twice into an embankment. 

While the rest of the family escaped with minor injuries, Ryan was pinned in the wreckage and had to be airlifted to Star City General Hospital. The crash left him with a complete T10 spinal cord injury, resulting in the permanent loss of all function and sensation below his waist. 

*Impact* translates this terrestrial nightmare into a cosmic collision. Operating from Ryan's point of view, the song describes his vessel being struck by a high-velocity fragment in the newly created debris field. Sonically, it pivots away from the 1990s industrial tone of the previous track to embrace a heavy, driving 1980s hard rock sound.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
*(A driving, heavy 1980s electric guitar riff cuts through the silence, backed by a pounding, relentless hard-rock drumbeat. It feels urgent, fast, and dangerous.)*

[Verse 1]
*(Aggressive Male Rock Vocal)*
I was holding the controls, trying to beat the storm
Trying to keep the Freighter steady, trying to keep it warm
Running forty through the dark, the hazard beacons bright
We were making steady time across the frozen night
But a shadow came flying through the passing lane
Too fast for the ice, too fast for the rain!

[Pre-Chorus]
A sudden violent strike on the quarter panel!
Ripped us right out of the safety channel!
Caught inside the debris field, spinning out of control
The massive broken planet taking its toll!

[Chorus]
Impact! Spinning off the line!
Impact! Shattering the design!
Rolled twice in the black, thrown into the cold
The heaviest gravity the metal can hold
We hit a massive fragment in the great unknown
At Mile Marker 98, I lost the throne!
We're barely alive, but we survived the night!
Yeah, we're barely alive!

[Verse 2]
I wake up pinned underneath the twisted steel
I'm screaming to the console but I cannot feel
The alarms are blaring, everything is red
But I can hear the crew breathing in the bed
The T-10 circuit is completely blown
I'm looking at a body that is not my own!

[Pre-Chorus]
A sudden violent strike on the quarter panel!
Ripped us right out of the safety channel!
Caught inside the debris field, spinning out of control
The massive broken planet taking its toll!

[Chorus]
Impact! Spinning off the line!
Impact! Shattering the design!
Rolled twice in the black, thrown into the cold
The heaviest gravity the metal can hold
We hit a massive fragment in the great unknown
At Mile Marker 98, I lost the throne!
We're barely alive, but we survived the night!
Yeah, we're barely alive!

[Guitar Solo]
*(The rhythm section drops into a heavy, stomping groove. A blistering, emotional 1980s electric guitar solo screams over the track, perfectly capturing the panic, the adrenaline, and the catastrophic loss of control.)*

[Bridge]
The engine is running, but the connection is dead.
Nothing below the damage, just the static in my head.
They pulled me from the wreckage, pulled me from the fire.
But I left half of myself in the twisting wire.

[Chorus]
Impact! Spinning off the line!
Impact! Shattering the design!
Rolled twice in the black, thrown into the cold
The heaviest gravity the metal can hold
We hit a massive fragment in the great unknown
At Mile Marker 98, I lost the throne!
We're barely alive, but we survived the night!
Yeah, we're barely alive!

[Outro]
We're barely alive.
The T-10 circuit is blown.
Mile Marker 98.
*(The heavy guitar riff chugs to a slow, grinding halt, followed by the fading sound of emergency warning klaxons ringing out in the empty vacuum)*

## The Penumbra: Transit (Live in Norfolk)
### Lore
Ryan's heavy, high-friction highway rock anthem from 2003's *Mile Marker 98*.

### Lyrics

[Spoken Intro]
*(Spoken clearly, calmly, and methodically, like a narrator in a planetarium documentary. A low, freezing, metallic drone hums in the background.)*
As our trajectory continues, the massive body of the parent planet moves directly between us and the sun. 
We are now entering the penumbra—the outer edge of the celestial shadow. 
The temperature will begin to drop rapidly as the light is obscured. 
Solar efficiency will fall to critical levels. 
Prepare for deep space isolation as we cross the threshold into total darkness.

[Intro]
*(The narration ends. A rigid, mechanical mid-tempo drum beat locks in. A heavy, distorted rhythm guitar begins chugging underneath, instantly answered by a sweeping, icy analog synthesizer lead. The fusion of hard rock and cosmic pop is immediate and massive.)*

[Verse 1]
*(Aggressive, melodic male rock vocals)*
They cleared the dock and cut the heavy lines
Leaving all the medical machinery behind
I'm sitting in the co-pilot seat in the dark
Watching my Anchor try to pilot the spark
But the edge of the planet is cutting the light
A massive eclipse in the middle of the night
I see the temperature drop on the console screen
The coldest shadow that we've ever seen!

[Pre-Chorus]
Entering the shadow, crossing the line
Losing the light, losing the sign
The power is draining, the cabin is dark
But I'm not alone in the middle of the dark!

[Chorus]
We're caught in the penumbra, cold and blind
Leaving the warmth of the sun behind
But the engine is running and the heavy guitars play
We're pushing through the shadow to the light of day!
I lost my legs but I didn't lose the fight
We're riding through the center of the endless night!
Transit through the penumbra!

[Verse 2]
The machinery hums with a desperate sound
A million miles from the solid ground
I look at the crew in the freezing air
The freight dogs and the captain sitting right there
The solar arrays are starting to freeze
A sudden drop by a hundred degrees
But the structural integrity is holding the line
I've got the family here and the telemetry is fine!

[Pre-Chorus]
Entering the shadow, crossing the line
Losing the light, losing the sign
The power is draining, the cabin is dark
But I'm not alone in the middle of the dark!

[Chorus]
We're caught in the penumbra, cold and blind
Leaving the warmth of the sun behind
But the engine is running and the heavy guitars play
We're pushing through the shadow to the light of day!
I lost my legs but I didn't lose the fight
We're riding through the center of the endless night!
Transit through the penumbra!

[Guitar & Synthesizer Duet Solo]
*(Ryan unleashes a blistering, distorted electric guitar solo. Halfway through, Cassidy's chilling, dissonant analog synthesizer answers him. The two instruments trade lines back and forth over the mechanical drum beat, a perfect musical representation of the siblings navigating the dark together.)*

[Bridge]
Just a phase of the orbit.
Just a test of the steel.
The shadow is heavy.
But the Anchor holds the wheel!

[Chorus]
We're caught in the penumbra, cold and blind
Leaving the warmth of the sun behind
But the engine is running and the heavy guitars play
We're pushing through the shadow to the light of day!
I lost my legs but I didn't lose the fight
We're riding through the center of the endless night!
Transit through the penumbra!

[Outro]
Wait for the dawn.
We're making the transit.
Through the penumbra.
*(The heavy guitars and icy synths sustain a final, massive chord together, echoing out into the cold vacuum as the mechanical drum machine abruptly stops)*

## Sanctuary: Zero-G (Live in Norfolk)
### Lore
Transitions from Ryan's guitar storm into Cassidy's *Moon 1 (Anchor)* zero-gravity sanctuary, turning the ribbed concrete dome ceiling of the Norfolk Prism Arena into a glowing planetarium.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
*(A single, echoing piano chord strikes. A massive, towering wall of analog synth brass and sweeping spatial reverb slowly rises from the silence. The stadium-sized gated drum machine enters with a thunderous, deliberate 4/4 march.)*

[Radio Transmission: Heavy Distorted Static]
*(Muffled, indistinguishable male shouting)*
*(Radio static crackles violently)*

[Clear Female Lead]
The navigation is finally aligned
I left the atmosphere and gravity behind

But the console is blinking with a sudden glowing light

Floating forever in the Sanctuary!

[Radio Transmission: Fading Distortion]
[Male Rock Vocal]
I hear the heavy static of a broken engine roar
I hear the heavy knocking on the heavy blast door
You are burning up the friction in a fatal asteroid ring
[Clear Female Lead]
While I am in the silence listening to the planets sing
[Male and Female Duet]
But the radio is open and the frequency is shared
A twin moon system perfectly paired

[Pre-Chorus]
The signal is crossing the great divide
A ghost in the machine from the other side
Two different orbits, two different speeds
But the mathematics give us everything we need

[Chorus]
Welcome to the Sanctuary, welcome to the clear
The zero-G is perfect and there is no friction here
Suspended in the silence, suspended in the deep
A promise that the metal and the memory will keep
We are spinning in the dark, we are flying through the sea
Floating forever in the Sanctuary!

[Symphonic Crescendo]
*(The ultimate realization of The Cosmic Tidal Lock Sound. The classical piano arpeggios spiral upwards at lightning speed while massive, soaring synth-brass chords widen the stereo field. The massive stadium drums hit with earth-shattering force. A wordless, layered choir of male and female voices soars above the instrumentation.)*

[Chorus]
Welcome to the Sanctuary, welcome to the clear
The zero-G is perfect and there is no friction here
Suspended in the silence, suspended in the deep
A promise that the metal and the memory will keep
We are spinning in the dark, we are flying through the sea

*(Spoken clearly and calmly by the planetarium documentary narrator)*
This concludes our journey through the Zero-G Sanctuary. 
We have explored the physics of the tidal lock, the crushing isolation of the silent ocean, and the delicate balance of the Goldilocks zone. 
While the vacuum of space is vast and unforgiving, mathematical perfection provides a permanent, weightless refuge. 
Please gather your belongings as the house lights return. 
Thank you for visiting the Engine Room Records Planetarium. 
Safe travels on your return to terrestrial gravity.
*(The electronic drone completely fades out)*

## Deep Pressure (Live in Norfolk)

Cassidy's deeply personal synth-pop tribute to the grounding power of deep-pressure hugs during autistic sensory overload.

[Pre-Chorus]
Close the blast shields, lock the heavy door
I don't want to see the flashing lights anymore

A sanctuary built in the underground
I need the weight, I need the heavy hold

[Chorus]
Give me that deep pressure, wrapping around
A sanctuary built in the underground
I need the weight, I need the heavy hold
To keep out the static, to keep out the cold
A protective atmosphere, dense and secure
Give me that deep pressure, perfect and pure

[Outro]

Turn up the gravity.
Keep me secure.

## The Anchor's Hold (Live in Norfolk)
### Lore
Completes the five-song *Twin Moons Live Suite* as Ryan and Cassidy lock Moon 1 and Moon 2 back into a single orbital system.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
*(A slow, clean chorus-pedal electric guitar picks a melancholic, 1980s ballad progression. A heavy, echoing gated snare drum hits on the two and four, establishing a massive stadium-ballad tempo. The atmosphere is quiet but incredibly heavy.)*

[Verse 1]
*(Emotional, gritty male rock vocals)*
The monitors are humming in the artificial light
I've been staring at the ceiling through a never-ending night
I can't feel the floor beneath me, the gravity is gone
Just a broken piece of metal waiting for the dawn
But I turn my heavy head and look across the room
You're sleeping in the corner, a beacon in the gloom
You didn't take the transport, you didn't fly away
You planted down your boots and swore that you would stay

You wrapped your arms around the damage to protect!

Holding all the pieces, saving the spark
I'm surviving the crash because you wouldn't let me go
You are the Anchor's Hold!

[Verse 2]
They're running the diagnostics, reading out the chart
They can fix the heavy machinery but they cannot fix the heart
I was terrified of floating into the endless black
A ghost inside a machine that is never coming back
But you're standing by the console, your hands upon the glass
Refusing every order to let the moment pass
You pull me in again, tighter than before
We're fighting off the vacuum right here on the floor!

[Pre-Chorus]
The structural integrity is completely compromised
But I can see the sanctuary reflecting in your eyes
You cross the silent ward and you pull me to your chest
Holding down the armor so the Engine gets to rest!

[Chorus]
You are the Anchor's Hold!
Keeping me from drifting out into the cold
When the orbit was broken, when the vessel was wrecked
You wrapped your arms around the damage to protect!
A gravitational embrace in the middle of the dark
Holding all the pieces, saving the spark
I'm surviving the crash because you wouldn't let me go
You are the Anchor's Hold!

[Guitar Solo]
*(The clean guitar drops out, replaced by a massive, soaring, distorted electric guitar solo. It is dripping with emotion, crying and bending notes over the slow, pounding drum beat. It is the peak of 1980s arena rock balladry.)*

[Bridge]
I can't feel my legs.
I can't feel the ground.
But I can feel your arms.
And I am safe and sound!

[Chorus]
You are the Anchor's Hold!
Keeping me from drifting out into the cold
When the orbit was broken, when the vessel was wrecked
You wrapped your arms around the damage to protect!
A gravitational embrace in the middle of the dark
Holding all the pieces, saving the spark
I'm surviving the crash because you wouldn't let me go
You are the Anchor's Hold!

[Outro]
Never let me go.
The gravitational embrace.
Holding me in place.
*(The heavy instrumentation drops away, leaving only the clean chorus guitar picking a final, beautiful chord that slowly fades into the silence of the medical bay)*

## No Hidden Gravity (Live in Norfolk)
### Lore
**The Hometown Ghent Highlight.** Performing just blocks from Norfolk's historic Ghent neighborhood where Nate and Maya live, Ryan and Cassidy dedicate this booming power ballad to Ghent's favorite platonic safe-person duo.

They look at us and try to draw a line

We're breaking the script, we're changing the game
A different kind of fire without the flame!
Yeah, there's no hidden gravity here!

This is a promise I'm willing to keep

[Pre-Chorus]
[Building Tempo, Heavy Guitar Chords]
No blurred lines, no sudden attack!
I can turn around and know you've got my back!

[Chorus]
[Maximum Volume, Massive 80s Arena Rock, Booming Snare]
There's no hidden gravity pulling us down!
No fatal attraction, no spinning around!
We're flying in parallel, steady and true
The safest place in the world is right next to you!
We're breaking the script, we're changing the game
A different kind of fire without the flame!
Yeah, there's no hidden gravity here!

[Guitar Solo]
[Soaring, Melodic Electric Guitar Solo, Bright Synth Stabs, Heavy Half-Time Drums]

[Bridge]
[Music Drops Out, Just Piano and Vocals]
You don't need to conquer.
I don't need to hide.
We built a fortress.
With no one inside but us!

[Chorus]
[Explosive Re-entry, Full Band, Layered Vocals]
There's no hidden gravity pulling us down!
No fatal attraction, no spinning around!
We're flying in parallel, steady and true

## Before the Distance (Live in Norfolk)
### Lore

[Chorus]
When the steel doors close and the platform clears
You can hear the truth grinding in the gears

Before the distance leaves your soul behind

[Chorus]
When the steel doors close and the platform clears
You can hear the truth grinding in the gears

## Fifth Gear: The Architect's Shield (Live in Norfolk)
### Lore
Cassidy's tribute to younger sister and CEO Holly O'Connell—who is watching from the side of the Norfolk stage, already preparing to shut down touring operations so her exhausted siblings can rest.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Punchy, driving 1980s sequenced synthesizer bassline and crisp gated-reverb drums)
(Bright electric guitar and keyboard hook lock in at 136 BPM)

[Verse 1]
Sixteen years old with a briefcase in her hand
Walking through the campus with a blueprint for the band
She never played a chord up underneath the spotlight beam
But she was the architect who guarded every dream
When the suits in the boardroom tried to change the rules
She read every line of print and schooled the corporate fools!

[Pre-Chorus]
Four musicians on the stage, giving all we've got
And the fifth member standing guard to lock the fortress plot!

[Chorus]
She's our fifth gear! Driving through the night!
Keeping all the wheels on the road and holding tight!

She's the iron shield standing in the front yard!
You never see her shadow in the spotlight's glare
But the engine only runs because our sister's there!

[Verse 2]
When the highway turned to ice and the world came crashing down
She held the clipboard steady in a cold hospital town
Law books in the van while we were rolling down the state
Building up a castle with an unbreakable gate
When we argued over melodies and couldn't see the light
She pulled us in a three-way hug and made the chaos right!

[Pre-Chorus]
Four musicians on the stage, giving all we've got
And the fifth member standing guard to lock the fortress plot!

[Chorus]
She's our fifth gear! Driving through the night!
Keeping all the wheels on the road and holding tight!
When the industry winds are blowing cold and hard
She's the iron shield standing in the front yard!
You never see her shadow in the spotlight's glare
But the engine only runs because our sister's there!

[Guitar Solo]
(Sharp, melodic 1980s AOR electric guitar solo trading rapid phrases with a bright analog synth lead)

[Bridge]
(Music drops to pulsing bass and shimmering keyboard chords)
Every contract sealed inside the family name
She turned a rigged casino into our own game
Not a note on the record, but she wrote the score
Keeping all the wolves away from our studio door!
(Drum fill builds to final chorus)

[Chorus]
She's our fifth gear! Driving through the night!
Keeping all the wheels on the road and holding tight!
When the industry winds are blowing cold and hard
She's the iron shield standing in the front yard!
You never see her shadow in the spotlight's glare
But the engine only runs because our sister's there!

[Outro]
She's our fifth gear!
The architect behind the sound!
(Punchy final synth-rock stabs and drum crash)

## Last Bus Home: Lights Out on Progress Street (Live in Norfolk)
### Lore
Before the final encore, Ryan and Cassidy sing their bittersweet farewell to the road—promising Forger Nation that while they are turning the lights out after four studio albums in four years to recharge, they *will* return one day.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Gentle, cinematic grand piano chords layered with a warm 1980s analog synthesizer pad)
(A melodic, crying electric guitar lead plays a nostalgic theme)

[Verse 1]
[male]
The final spotlight fades across the empty arena floor
The road cases are latched and rolled outside the stage door
[female]
For twenty years we gave the world the thunder in our chest
Now the highway's calling us to finally take our rest
[male]
Our custom low-floor shuttle bus is kneeling at the curb
[female]
Leading to a quiet place the static can't disturb.

[Pre-Chorus]
[male]
Aluminum ramp unfolds beneath the amber sign
[female]

Turning off the amplifiers and the stadium heat!
Lock the front door tight and let the curtains fall
We don't owe the outside world anything at all!
Through every storm we weathered and every song we played
This family is the greatest music that we ever made!

[female]
Unplug the synthesizers, set the guitars in their stands
[male]
Rest the tired voices and the callused, aching hands
[female]
Five pairs of sneakers lined up by the wooden stairs
[male]
Trading all the crowded rooms for quiet living room chairs
[female]
We're not fading into nothing, we're just tuning out the noise
[male]
Back to three sisters and brothers and the twin Wright boys!

[Chorus]
We're taking the last bus home! Back to Progress Street!
Turning off the amplifiers and the stadium heat!
Lock the front door tight and let the curtains fall
We don't owe the outside world anything at all!
Through every storm we weathered and every song we played
This family is the greatest music that we ever made!

[Bridge]
(Music softens to warm piano and glowing synth strings)
[female]
Someday when we're ready, we'll turn the switches on
[male]
And find out that the fire in the engine's never gone
[female]
But tonight the porch light's turning off in Blacksburg town
[male]
As we lay the heavy burden of the road right down!

[Guitar Solo]
(Epic, extended, deeply emotional 1980s stadium-rock guitar solo soaring over grand piano chords, lush analog synth pads, and massive slow gated-reverb drums)

[Final Chorus]
We're taking the last bus home! Back to Progress Street!
Turning off the amplifiers and the stadium heat!
Lock the front door tight and let the curtains fall
We don't owe the outside world anything at all!
Through every storm we weathered and every song we played
This family is the greatest music that we ever made!

[Outro]

Lights out on Progress Street...
[male]
Goodnight, Forger Nation.
(A warm, sustained piano and analog synth chord slowly fades into silence)

## Electric Color (Live in Norfolk)
### Lore
The mandatory closer—one final, cathartic explosion of color inside the Norfolk Prism Arena before the band heads home to Blacksburg for their 2005–2014 Burnout Hiatus. Unlike the 2016 *Live at the Crucible* AOR/Marching Band reclamation, this 2004 Norfolk tour finale preserves the classic high-energy 1980s Synth-Pop / Dance-Rock arrangement of *Electric Color*, supercharged with Ryan's live arena shred guitar solo. *(Note: By sacred band vow, 'Saturday Night Lights: The Crucible' and 'If You Walked Away' are strictly excluded).*

### Lyrics

[Live Arena Crowd Roar]

[Extended Instrumental Vamp]
(A massive, pulsing 1980s analog synthesizer pad and driving gated-reverb drum groove build tension across the arena floor as funky rhythm guitar scratches lock in)

[Spoken Word Intro]
(Spoken, female vocals, energetic and breathless over the vamp and cheering crowd)
We have been all over the world on this tour, Norfolk!
And there is no better place to finish this journey than right here on Virginia soil!
Before we pack up the buses and head home to Blacksburg... we've got one last song left in the tank!
Tonight, we're gonna turn the Prism into a dance floor one last time! Let me see you move, Norfolk!

[Instrumental Hook]
(The main sharp, catchy 1980s synthesizer hook and funky dance-rock beat slam in at full power)

[Verse 1]
Woke up this morning, the world was gray
Didn't know the colors had all faded away
Been living in a room with the curtains drawn tight
Then you flipped a switch and turned on the light

[Chorus]
Oh, I'm seeing in electric color
Finding a rhythm I never knew
Dancing in a brand new summer
My whole world feels bright and new
It's an electric-colored feeling
And it all started with you!

[Verse 2]
(Funky rhythm guitar scratches enter, synthesizer swells, bassline gets busier)
The city sounds were just a buzz in my ear
A static station I could never quite hear
But now the noise is turning into a song
And I finally feel like I belong

[Chorus]
Oh, I'm seeing in electric color
Finding a rhythm I never knew
Dancing in a brand new summer
My whole world feels bright and new
It's an electric-colored feeling
And it all started with you!

[Bridge]
The beat is getting stronger
I can't hide it any longer
My heart is playing catch-up
To the music turning way up!
(Spoken, shouting to the crowd)
Ryan, show them what you got!

[Guitar Solo]
(A blistering, high-gain rock guitar solo cuts through the synth-pop beat, melodic and fast)

[Chorus]
(High energy, drums crashing, crowd singing along)
Oh, I'm seeing in electric color
Finding a rhythm I never knew
Dancing in a brand new summer
My whole world feels bright and new
It's an electric-colored feeling
And it all started with you!

[Outro]
(The synth hook returns over roaring arena crowd)
Electric color!
Feeling so new!
It's all because of you!
(Spoken)
Thank you Norfolk! We love you! Goodnight!
(Music ends on a clean synth hit and roaring arena applause)

# Lost Sounds (2007) [CompilationAlbum]
* **Narrative Era:** 2007
* **Real-World DSP Release:** 2026-10-11

## The Stardust Engine (The Rejected Mix)

### Lore
This alternate version of "The Stardust Engine" was one of the original demos shopped around to record companies when the band formed in 1986. Apex Records signed them but rejected this version, burying the final studio version as track 13 on 1987's *Electric Color*. The band famously despises Apex for what happened to them during the 1992 label dispute. For this vault release, they officially retitled this early cut "(The Rejected Mix)" instead of naming Apex Records directly, serving as a quiet nod to their lingering resentment.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
We were looking for a signal
A light in the endless black
A single voice in the universe
To tell us we're on the right track

(Verse 2)
We found the fire in the wires
We found the heart in the machine
Building a dream from nothing
A sound the world has never seen

(Chorus)
We are the Stardust Engine!
Racing through the night
We're burning on the fuel of a brand new light
We are the Stardust Engine!
Yeah, we're taking flight
Turning our future on, shining so bright!

(Verse 3)
They tried to ground us, keep us small
Tell us that we were gonna fall
But they don't know the things we know
They haven't seen the places we'll go

(Chorus)
We are the Stardust Engine!
Racing through the night
We're burning on the fuel of a brand new light
We are the Stardust Engine!
Yeah, we're taking flight
Turning our future on, shining so bright!

(Guitar Solo)
(A soaring, melodic solo that trades licks with a bright synthesizer hook)

(Bridge)
We are the cosmos, we are the fire
We're taking this one, higher and higher
We are the Stardust!
(We are the Engine!)
We are the Stardust Engine!

(Chorus)
We are the Stardust Engine!
Racing through the night
We're burning on the fuel of a brand new light
We are the Stardust Engine!
Yeah, we're taking flight
Turning our future on, shining so bright!

(Outro)
Shining so bright...
We're taking flight...
(fades out on an epic, sustained guitar chord)

## Midnight Drivers (Basement Demo)

### Lore
This is an early, raw basement recording of what would eventually become the title track of their 2001 retro-engineered album. Recorded in 1987 before the track was polished in the studio (which Apex Records ultimately deemed too dark and shelved), this gritty demo captures the isolating, moody energy the band originally envisioned before the label interference.

Wipers keep the beat on the window pane
Static on the radio, fading in and out

Leaving all the ghosts in their empty rooms
This city's just a map of glowing, faded lines
But I'm safe right here, between your eyes and mine
Midnight drivers, in the fading light

The skyline's a promise, cold and blue
I don't know this road, but I'm with you

Got no destination, just a place to go

[Chorus]

Leaving all the ghosts in their empty rooms
This city's just a map of glowing, faded lines
But I'm safe right here, between your eyes and mine
Midnight drivers, in the fading light
