Don't have to talk
Don't have to say a thing
The engine hums a rhythm
The future's what we bring

[Synth Solo]
*(Instrumental - Cassidy delivers a moody, atmospheric synthesizer solo that weaves through the heavy reverb of the drum track.)*

[Chorus]
Driving in the dark, running on neon fumes
Leaving all the ghosts in their empty rooms
This city's just a map of glowing, faded lines
But I'm safe right here, between your eyes and mine

## Radio Silence

### Lore
A 1986 deep cut demo that Apex Records threw out during the *Electric Color* selection process because it was deemed "too heavy on the synths and too light on the pop". It remained a fan-favorite myth among diehards who traded bootleg cassette tapes, finally getting an official release on this vault compilation.

### Lyrics

[Chorus]

Nothing on the airwaves tonight
Radio silence
Waiting for a spark to ignite
I'm calling out into the dark
Just trying to make a mark
In the radio silence

[Verse 2]

Just the ringing echoing in my head
We built this tower to the stars
But nobody knows who we are

[Chorus]
It's just radio silence
Nothing on the airwaves tonight
Radio silence
Waiting for a spark to ignite
I'm calling out into the dark
Just trying to make a mark
In the radio silence

[Bridge]
(Heavy synthesizer arpeggio building up in tempo)
Can anybody hear me?
Can anybody see the light?
I'm broadcasting on the edge of the night!

[Guitar Solo]
(A melodic, delayed guitar solo that cuts sharply against the synth backing)

[Chorus]
It's just radio silence
Nothing on the airwaves tonight
Radio silence
Waiting for a spark to ignite
I'm calling out into the dark
Just trying to make a mark
In the radio silence

[Outro]
Just static...
Just silence...
(Fades out with the sound of a radio dial turning through dead stations)

## Empty Arenas (Early Demo)
### Lore
Recorded in late 1990 shortly after Ryan's life-altering bus accident, this solemn, stripped-down acoustic demo (`Empty Arenas (Early Demo)`) reflects the band's raw fear that they would never be able to play live again. Deemed too bleak for the *Friction* sessions, the tape remained locked in the vault until *Lost Sounds* (2007)—though fourteen years after cutting this sketch, the band resurrected the song during the 2004 *Progress Street* sessions and transformed it into a thunderous, full-band 1980s Album-Oriented Rock (AOR) stadium anthem with polished lyrics and a full drum kit.

### Lyrics

[Verse 1]
The spotlight's dead, the cables coiled
The magic trick has been completely spoiled
Just an echo bouncing off the concrete floor
No one's knocking at the backstage door

[Chorus]
Empty arenas, where the echoes go to die
Empty arenas, beneath a painted sky
We used to set this room on fire
Now we're just preaching to the wire
In these empty arenas

[Verse 2]
The ticket stubs are swept away
Just ghosts remembering yesterday
I look out at the velvet seats
And listen to my own heartbeat

[Chorus]
Empty arenas, where the echoes go to die
Empty arenas, beneath a painted sky
We used to set this room on fire
Now we're just preaching to the wire
In these empty arenas

[Bridge]
(A slow, mournful acoustic guitar progression)
Will the lights ever come back on?
Or is the final encore already gone?
If I scream into the vast unknown
Will I realize I'm all alone?

[Guitar Solo]
(A slow, bluesy, emotionally charged acoustic solo)

[Chorus]
Empty arenas, where the echoes go to die
Empty arenas, beneath a painted sky
We used to set this room on fire
Now we're just preaching to the wire
In these empty arenas

[Outro]
Just preaching to the wire...
Empty...
(Fades out with the ambient reverb of a large, empty hall)

## The Contract

### Lore
The legendary 1992 punk-rock vent track. Recorded entirely in secret during their bitter legal dispute with Apex Records, the band absolutely shreds their former label for their suffocating creative control. The sheer anger in Ryan's guitar work makes this a standout deep cut. It was never intended for public release, serving strictly as a therapeutic middle finger to the studio executives.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Fast, aggressive, distorted punk-rock power chords)

[Verse 1]
Sign on the dotted line, sell your soul for a dime
Smile for the camera, pretend that you're doing fine
They dress you up, they pull the strings
They clip your feathers and they sell your wings!

[Chorus]
Tear up the contract!
Burn it to the ground!
We're taking back the music!
We're taking back the sound!
You can keep the money, you can keep the fame
But you're never gonna own our name!
Tear up the contract!

[Verse 2]
Read the fine print, the devil's in the ink
They tell you what to wear, they tell you what to think
A manufactured dream on an assembly line
Well you can take your plastic crown, the kingdom is mine!

[Chorus]
Tear up the contract!
Burn it to the ground!
We're taking back the music!
We're taking back the sound!
You can keep the money, you can keep the fame
But you're never gonna own our name!
Tear up the contract!

[Bridge]
(Driving, angry bassline with feedback swells)
We are not your property!
We are not your toys!
We're bringing back the static!
We're bringing back the noise!
(Scream) NOISE!

[Guitar Solo]
(A frantic, blistering punk guitar solo, heavily distorted and fast)

[Chorus]
Tear up the contract!
Burn it to the ground!
We're taking back the music!
We're taking back the sound!
You can keep the money, you can keep the fame
But you're never gonna own our name!
Tear up the contract!

[Outro]
Tear it up!
Burn it down!
We are out of here!
(Song ends abruptly on a final, crashing chord and the sound of paper ripping)

## Neon Hearts (Acoustic Sketch)
### Lore
This is the raw, acoustic sketch of what would become their heavily manufactured 1989 hit, "Neon Hearts." Without the glossy, heavy studio synths imposed by Apex Records, this early draft reveals the song to be a surprisingly tender, intimate ballad rather than a club-ready pop anthem.

## Sanctuary: Zero G (Rough Mix)
### Lore
An early demo outtake of the massive closer from the 2003 *Sanctuary: Zero-G* album. Before it was expanded into a 74-minute symphonic journey with a spoken-word planetarium outro, it existed as this raw, atmospheric space-rock demo. It showcases the core duet between Ryan and Cassidy and the foundation of The Cosmic Tidal Lock Sound® before the studio gloss and orchestral layers were applied.

## Steel and Synthetics
### Lore
Originally written during the 1997 *Hard Reset* sessions. It serves as a love letter to the band's dual identities—the "Steel" of rock and the "Synthetics" of electronic music. It was cut simply due to runtime constraints on the double album, but remains a fan favorite.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(A driving, heavy drumbeat accompanied by a thick, analog synth bass and a distorted rhythm guitar)

[Verse 1]
They said we had to choose a side
The organic truth or the electric ride
They wanted us to draw the line
Between the circuit board and the grand design

[Chorus]
But we are made of steel and synthetics!
Flesh and bone and pure kinetics!
A heavy riff and a digital beat
The cold machine and the human heat!
Steel and synthetics!

[Verse 2]
The strings are bleeding, the motherboard's fried
There's no place left for us to hide
We take the rust and we make it shine
We take the code and we make it divine

[Chorus]
'Cause we are made of steel and synthetics!
Flesh and bone and pure kinetics!
A heavy riff and a digital beat
The cold machine and the human heat!
Steel and synthetics!

[Bridge]
(A staccato, syncopated rhythm section)
Upload the soul!
Plug in the heart!
This is where the science and the magic start!

[Guitar and Synth Duel]
(An epic, blazing solo section where a heavy metal guitar solo trades frantic licks with a rapid-fire synthesizer arpeggio)

[Chorus]
We are made of steel and synthetics!
Flesh and bone and pure kinetics!
A heavy riff and a digital beat
The cold machine and the human heat!
Steel and synthetics!

[Outro]
Steel!
Synthetics!
Human heat!
(The track ends on a massive, combined guitar and synth power chord that rings out for ten seconds before a digital power-down sound cuts it off)

## Our Own Frequency
### Lore
A brand new track recorded specifically for the 2007 *Lost Sounds* compilation, intentionally designed to simulate the stripped-down, acoustic music Ryan and Cassidy were making together back in 1985 (before their cousins Evan and Tyler joined to form The Stardust Engine). After digging through years of painful, heavily-manufactured Apex Records history, they wanted to close the album by returning to their roots. Written and recorded in a single afternoon with just an acoustic guitar and a piano, "Our Own Frequency" is a triumphant, intimate declaration of their bond as brother and sister. It is the exact kind of acoustic scratch track they always wanted to make, completely free of label interference.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(A warm, inviting acoustic guitar and piano duet intro)

[Verse 1]
(Cassidy)
They want to paint us in neon light
They want to tell us how to burn tonight
But you and me, we know the truth
They can't buy what we built in our youth

[Verse 2]
(Ryan)
Turn off the synths, unplug the drum
Look at the monsters that we have become
Let's strip it down to the wood and wire
And build ourselves a signal fire

[Chorus]
(Duet)
Just the piano and the six-string strum
Before the noise and the kingdom come
No one to tell us how the story ends
Just a brother and a sister and the time we spend
On our own frequency
Just you and me

[Verse 3]
(Cassidy)
They tried to sell a manufactured dream
A perfect picture on a glowing screen
(Ryan)
But all I need is sitting right here
The melody is finally clear

[Chorus]
(Duet)
Just the piano and the six-string strum
Before the noise and the kingdom come
No one to tell us how the story ends
Just a brother and a sister and the time we spend
On our own frequency
Just you and me

[Bridge]
(A soaring, deeply emotional piano and acoustic guitar instrumental duet)
(Cassidy)
No heavy gloss...
(Ryan)
No studio boss...
(Duet)
Just the music we wanted to make!

[Chorus]
(Duet)
Just the piano and the six-string strum
Before the noise and the kingdom come
No one to tell us how the story ends
Just a brother and a sister and the time we spend
On our own frequency
Just you and me

[Outro]
(Ryan)
Our own frequency...
(Cassidy)
Just you and me...
(Fades out with a lingering piano chord and a soft acoustic strum)

## The Promise (Corporate Demo)
### Lore
The exact sound Apex Records wanted: safe, sterile, and perfectly calibrated for a televangelist broadcast. This was the studio's cynical attempt to cash in on the "religious pop" trend. The guitar solo is painfully restrained, engineered specifically not to offend anyone. The entire project was scrapped in a panic by executives after the massive controversy surrounding Cydele's "Worship" video dropped on MTV the very next day.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
I was walking in the dark
I was looking for a sign
I heard a whisper, a distant sound
Calling me to higher ground

(Pre-Chorus)
I felt a hand reach out for me
And suddenly, I could see
The world was bright and new

(Chorus)
I can feel The Promise! (Oh-oh-oh)
Shining down on me
I can hear The Call! (Oh-oh-oh)
It's setting me free
It's a beautiful light for the whole world to see
I can feel The Promise, inside of me!

(Verse 2)
No more shadows, no more doubt
I know what my life's about
I'm rising up, I'm strong and new
It's all because I'm following through

(Pre-Chorus)
I felt a hand reach out for me
And suddenly, I could see
The world was bright and new

(Chorus)
I can feel The Promise! (Oh-oh-oh)
Shining down on me
I can hear The Call! (Oh-oh-oh)
It's setting me free
It's a beautiful light for the whole world to see
I can feel The Promise, inside of me!

(Bridge)
(Music swells)
Lift your hands up to the sky!
Let the feeling take you high!
Just let the feeling take you high!

(Guitar Solo)
(A small, restrained, and clean guitar solo that feels like it belongs in a televangelist church broadcast)

(Chorus)
I can feel The Promise! (Oh-oh-oh)
Shining down on me
I can hear The Call! (Oh-oh-oh)
It's setting me free
It's a beautiful light for the whole world to see
I can feel The Promise, inside of me!

(Outro)
Feel The Promise! (Oh-oh-oh)
Hear The Call!
(The Promise... inside of me)
(fades out)
## The Promise (Sabotage Demo)
### Lore
Ryan's malicious compliance caught on tape. Forced to record a manufactured "synth-gospel" pop track against the band's wishes, Ryan played the label's game right up until the bridge. Then, he unleashed the "devil's music," violently shoving a blistering, unholy rock solo into the middle of their squeaky-clean religious hit. It gave the horrified executives a second massive reason to pull the plug the second the Cydele controversy hit MTV.

### Lyrics

(Verse 1)
I was walking in the dark
I was looking for a sign
I heard a whisper, a distant sound
Calling me to higher ground

I felt a hand reach out for me
And suddenly, I could see
The world was bright and new

(Chorus)
I can feel The Promise! (Oh-oh-oh)
Shining down on me
I can hear The Call! (Oh-oh-oh)
It's setting me free
It's a beautiful light for the whole world to see
I can feel The Promise, inside of me!

(Verse 2)
No more shadows, no more doubt
I know what my life's about
I'm rising up, I'm strong and new
It's all because I'm following through

(Pre-Chorus)
I felt a hand reach out for me
And suddenly, I could see
The world was bright and new

(Chorus)
I can feel The Promise! (Oh-oh-oh)
Shining down on me
I can hear The Call! (Oh-oh-oh)
It's setting me free
It's a beautiful light for the whole world to see
I can feel The Promise, inside of me!

(Music swells)

Let the feeling take you high!

(Guitar Solo)
(A blistering, face-melting, unholy rock guitar solo that completely shatters the sterile gospel atmosphere)

(Chorus)
I can feel The Promise! (Oh-oh-oh)
Shining down on me

## Atmosphere (Warehouse Demo)
### Lore
The "Lost Gem" from the canceled 1992 *Friction* sessions. The band wrote this song about the literal, scientific friction of a space shuttle re-entering Earth's atmosphere ("Here comes the friction / Feel the fire"). The "rogue" executive, Julian Vance, misinterpreted these lyrics as sexual metaphors, leading to the catastrophic misunderstanding that destroyed the contract. This is the original 1992 demo, released here for the first time.

### Lyrics

Black velvet, a million lights
Floating silent through the endless night
The world below, a sleeping blue
I'm holding my breath, I'm thinking of you

(Verse 2)

The fragile hull begins to shake
A promise that we're bound to make

(Chorus)
Here comes the friction, feel the fire
Burning up with our desire
A sheet of flame, a crimson glow
The only way we know to go
We're coming home, we're breaking through
This atmosphere... with you

(Verse 3)
The pressure builds, the gauges scream
This is the violence of the dream
A single tear in the metal skin
Is all it takes to let the outside in

(Chorus)
Here comes the friction, feel the fire
Burning up with our desire
A sheet of flame, a crimson glow
The only way we know to go
We're coming home, we're breaking through
This atmosphere... with you

(Bridge)
We're losing control, the edges blur
A terror and a beauty...
Burning up!

(Guitar Solo)

(Chorus)
Here comes the friction, feel the fire
Burning up with our desire
A sheet of flame, a crimson glow
The only way we know to go
We're coming home, we're breaking through
This atmosphere... with you

(Outro)
Burning up...
Friction...
Coming home...
(fades)
## Sanctuary in the Storm
### Lore

An epic 80s power ballad duet about finding unconditional safety and healing after surviving an abusive relationship. Features Ryan and Cassidy O'Connell on male and female lead vocals, a powerful piano intro, massive drums with gated reverb, and a soaring electric guitar solo. Written in 1986 as Part IV of The Stardust Engine's published narrative arc of fraternal twins John and Erin (and originally rejected by Apex Records before being unearthed for Lost Sounds), the song takes place after Erin rescues John from his abuser on the Commonwealth Mall sidewalk in "Before the Distance" and brings him the two blocks home to Progress Street NE—capturing John finding his safe harbor and "Sanctuary in the Storm" with his twin sister Erin.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Dramatic piano intro with atmospheric, shimmering synthesizer pads)

[Male Vocal]
(Verse 1)
I was lost inside a silent scream
Living out a fractured dream
A ship without a sail, adrift at sea
Thought the darkness was a part of me
I never knew the sun could even shine
Until your hand reached out for mine

[Female Vocal]
(Verse 2)
I saw the heart you tried to hide
The gentle soul you kept inside
I'll be the calm inside the gale
A love that's true and will not fail
I'm here to stand right by your side
With nothing left to run or hide

[Duet]
(Chorus)
You are my sanctuary in the storm
A fire here to keep me safe and warm
When the thunder rolls and the cold winds blow
I'll never let you go
We are a sanctuary in the storm

[Male Vocal]
(Verse 3)
You taught my broken wings to fly
Beneath a new and hopeful sky
You heard the words I couldn't say
And chased the haunted ghosts away

[Female Vocal]
(Verse 4)
And now I see you standing tall
Prepared to risk and give your all
My promise is forever true
My sanctuary is here with you

[Duet]
(Chorus)
You are my sanctuary in the storm
A fire here to keep me safe and warm
When the thunder rolls and the cold winds blow
I'll never let you go
We are a sanctuary in the storm

[Instrumental Bridge]
(Music builds dramatically, leading into the solo)

[Guitar Solo]
(A soaring, melodic, and highly emotional electric guitar solo with long, sustained notes)

[Duet]
(Chorus)
We are the sanctuary in the storm!
A fire here to keep us safe and warm!
When the thunder rolls and the cold winds blow
We'll never let this go!
We are a sanctuary in the storm!

[Outro]
(Ad-libbing over a fade-out with piano and synth pads)
(Male:) My sanctuary...
(Female:) I'll never let you go...
(Duet:) In the storm... Safe with you...


## Not Her
### Lore

A powerful, defiant 80s rock anthem and duet serving as the fifth and final chapter of The Stardust Engine's published narrative arc of fraternal twins John and Erin. After Erin rescued John from his abuser along the Commonwealth Mall bus line ("Before the Distance") and brought him the two blocks home to Progress Street NE ("Sanctuary in the Storm"), a slick male predator ("that easy charm, that practiced smile") corners Erin and tries to isolate her ("Brother, listen, something's wrong... Just take my hand, let's turn and run"). Because John survived his own abuser, he immediately recognizes the predator's manipulative tactics and unleashes his analytical fury—declaring "Not her. Not today. Not ever again!" and rescuing Erin just as she rescued him.

### Lyrics

[Verse 1]

# Our Own Frequency (2008)
* **Narrative Era:** 2008
* **Real-World DSP Release:** 2026-10-16

## Our Own Frequency
### Lore
Originally recorded as the closer for the 2007 *Lost Sounds* compilation, this intimate acoustic track became the catalyst for the band's entirely new direction. Ryan and Cassidy realized that stripping away the noise and returning to the raw, unfiltered "wood and wire" sound of their youth was the only way forward. They promoted this track to be the opener and title track of their 2008 album, *Our Own Frequency*, setting the intimate, deeply personal tone for the rest of the record.

(A warm, inviting acoustic guitar and piano duet intro)

They want to tell us how to burn tonight

They can't buy what we built in our youth

Look at the monsters that we have become
Let's strip it down to the wood and wire
And build ourselves a signal fire

[Chorus]

Before the noise and the kingdom come
No one to tell us how the story ends
Just a brother and a sister and the time we spend
On our own frequency
Just you and me

They tried to sell a manufactured dream
A perfect picture on a glowing screen
(Ryan)

The melody is finally clear

[Chorus]
(Duet)
Just the piano and the six-string strum
Before the noise and the kingdom come
No one to tell us how the story ends
Just a brother and a sister and the time we spend
On our own frequency
Just you and me

(Ryan)
No studio boss...

Before the noise and the kingdom come
No one to tell us how the story ends

On our own frequency

Our own frequency...
(Cassidy)

(Fades out with a lingering piano chord and a soft acoustic strum)

## The Noise Outside
### Lore
Written as a direct response to the overwhelming press cycle and industry expectations that caused their 2008 burnout hiatus. "The Noise Outside" explores Ryan and Cassidy physically and metaphorically locking the studio doors to shut out the world. Driven by a warm acoustic guitar and an atmospheric synth pad, the track emphasizes their need to find a quiet space away from the friction of the industry.

## Safe Person
### Lore
The emotional core of the album. "Safe Person" strips away the protective rock-star armor to reveal the vulnerability underneath. Ryan, seated at his acoustic guitar, sings to Cassidy about being her anchor when the stadium lights and screaming crowds become too much to bear. Returning to the pure acoustic guitar and piano arrangement, this track cements their unbreakable sibling bond.

"Unplugged" is about cutting the power lines to the big machines and walking away from the "crazy circus" of the music industry. ## Brother's Keeper
### Lore
"Brother's Keeper" explores the profound, life-or-death mutual protection Ryan and Cassidy offer one another. Due to his T-10 paralysis, Ryan literally would not survive without Cassidy. She fiercely guards him and performs his daily ADLs (Activities of Daily Living)—including toileting requirements and anti-atrophy leg stretches. At night, they share a bed pushed against the wall, with Ryan on the inside and Cassidy sleeping on the edge to act as a human bed-rail so he doesn't roll off. In return for this intense physical devotion, Ryan acts as Cassidy's emotional anchor, protecting her from the brutal psychological toll of the music industry. The acoustic guitar and piano arrangement creates a delicate, vulnerable atmosphere for this raw confession of absolute co-dependence. (The lyrics are intentionally written from a unified, perspective-neutral standpoint to reflect their shared reality).

Giving up comfort for the other one's sake
A brother and a sister, fighting to survive

Guarding all the secrets we're trying to keep

When the body won't work, we become our own feet
When the pressure is heavy, we make it retreat
They don't understand the blood in our veins
We’re holding the keys and we’re breaking the chains

[Interlude]
(Emotional piano and acoustic guitar interlude)

Being the shelter when bitter winds blow

We’re holding the keys and we’re breaking the chains

[Outro]
(Harmonizing gently)
We keep each other safe.
We keep each other grounded.
(Fade out on a single, resonating piano chord)

## Through the Static
### Lore
"Through the Static" explores Ryan and Cassidy's almost telepathic connection, born from years of shared trauma, recovery, and absolute reliance on one another. The title refers to the chaos of the music industry, the physical realities of Ryan's paralysis, and the overwhelming noise of the world. In the midst of all that static, they can always find each other's signal. A glance across a crowded room, a shift in breathing in the dark, a subtle change in the rhythm of the wheels—they understand it all. It is a song about tuning out the interference and focusing on the one frequency that matters: their bond. The acoustic guitar and piano arrangement leaves plenty of space for their vocal harmonies to interlock. (The lyrics are written to be perspective-neutral and fluid so that whichever vocal Suno randomly assigns won't break the canon).

## The Weight We Carry
### Lore
"The Weight We Carry" acknowledges that their profound codependence, while beautiful and necessary, is incredibly heavy. It’s an honest, acoustic confession that sometimes the pressure of being everything to someone else is overwhelming. Ryan carries the physical weight of his condition and the guilt of needing Cassidy so much. Cassidy carries the emotional weight of his physical survival and the constant vigilance required. It’s not resentment, but rather a somber realization of their reality. The song is a quiet promise that even when the weight is crushing, they will never drop each other. (The lyrics are written to be perspective-neutral and fluid so that whichever vocal Suno randomly assigns won't break the canon).

## Just Us
### Lore
"Just Us" is the closing track of the album. After exploring their profound trauma, intense codependence, and the noise of the industry, the album ends on a note of quiet peace. It’s a stripped-down, acoustic lullaby. No matter what happens—whether the band fails or succeeds, whether the pain worsens or fades—at the end of the day, it is just Ryan and Cassidy. They are enough for each other. It's a gentle, reassuring end to a deeply emotional album. # Starfield Calibration (2011)
* **Narrative Era:** 2011
* **Real-World DSP Release:** 2026-10-17

## The Stardust Engine (Cosmic Tidal Lock Mix)
### Lore
Like the rest of *Electric Color*, *"The Stardust Engine"* was officially released in 1987 (buried by Apex Records at the very end of the album as Track 13). In truth, it was the very first song Cassidy and Ryan O'Connell came up with in 1986 when their twin cousins Evan and Tyler Wright joined them at CPI in Blacksburg. Too scared at the time to shop the song around, the four of them kept it to themselves and decided to use that first song to name their band: **The Stardust Engine**. Twenty-five years later, the band's eponymous theme is reborn as the opening calibration sequence of 2011's *Starfield Calibration*—stripped of Apex Records' 1987 neon gloss and rebuilt from the ground up inside Cassidy O'Connell's proprietary **The Cosmic Tidal Lock Sound™** (an in-universe trademark of Engine Room Records, LLC; not registered in real life), suspending Ryan and Cassidy's sibling duet inside a 360-degree zero-gravity planetarium dome.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(A sweeping, zero-gravity 1980s analog synthesizer pad blooms across the planetarium dome like a sunrise from orbit)
(A melodic, sliding fretless bassline locks into a steady, driving 4/4 gated reverb drum beat)
(Sparkling analog bell arpeggios and chorus-drenched space-rock guitar harmonics echo across the stereo field)

[Verse 1]

[female] A beacon in the endless black
[male] A single voice across the universe
[male] To tell us we were on the right track

[Verse 2]

[Pre-Chorus]
(Shimmering analog synth brass swells as the zero-gravity spatial reverb widens)

[male] Leaving terrestrial gravity for the infinite light!

[Chorus]

[male] Gliding through the velvet night!
[female] We're burning on the fuel of a brand new light!
[male] We are The Stardust Engine!
[female] In zero-G we're taking flight!
[male] Turning our future on, shining so bright!

[Verse 3]
[male] They tried to ground us, keep us small
[male] Tied to the earth, waiting for the fall
[female] But they can't reach the altitude we know
[female] Up in the sanctuary where the nebulae glow!

[Pre-Chorus]
[female] The telemetry is locked in alignment tonight
[male] Leaving terrestrial gravity for the infinite light!

[Chorus]

[male] We are The Stardust Engine!
[female] In zero-G we're taking flight!

(A soaring, melodic space-rock electric guitar solo trades celestial phrases with a shimmering 1980s analog synthesizer lead over Evan's sliding fretless bassline and Tyler's driving 4/4 gated drums)

[Bridge]
(Drums drop out into weightless zero-gravity synth pads and echoing vocal reverb)
[female] We are the cosmos, we are the fire
[male] Taking this orbit higher and higher

[male] We are the Engine!
[female] Locked in the tidal rhythm of the stars!
(Massive 1980s gated snare drum fill brings the full rhythm section crashing back in)

[Chorus]

[male] Gliding through the velvet night!
[female] We're burning on the fuel of a brand new light!
[male] We are The Stardust Engine!
[female] In zero-G we're taking flight!
[male] Turning our future on, shining so bright!

[Outro]

[male] In the tidal lock tonight...
[female] We are The Stardust Engine...
(Lush analog synthesizer chords, space-rock guitar harmonics, and fretless bass notes slowly drift outward into eternal cosmic reverb)

## Electric Color (Cosmic Tidal Lock Mix)
### Lore
In 1987, *"Electric Color"* was the band's Trojan horse—a blindingly bright synth-pop hit engineered to satisfy Apex Records' radio quotas while concealing lyrics about dissociation and creative captivity. Twenty-four years later, Cassidy O'Connell reclaims the song completely on solo vocals, replacing the brittle 1980s studio brass with warm, zero-gravity analog polyphonic pads and a hypnotic fretless bassline by Evan Wright. In the Cosmic Tidal Lock mix, the "neon lights" are no longer fluorescent studio cages—they are deep-space auroras.

## Plastic Sky (Cosmic Tidal Lock Mix)
### Lore
Originally written in 1987 as a sharp critique of Los Angeles artifice (*"Look up at the ceiling, painted blue and white / There are no stars in this fake night"*), *"Plastic Sky"* undergoes a breathtaking thematic inversion on *Starfield Calibration*. Performed inside real-world planetarium domes where the ceiling literally opens into a simulated universe, Cassidy's solo vocal and soaring analog keytar solo shatter the painted fiberglass ceiling and launch the listener into open orbit.

## Worlds Apart (Cosmic Tidal Lock Mix)
### Lore
On 1987's *Electric Color*, *"Worlds Apart"* was marketed by Apex Records as a conventional separation ballad, though Ironheads quickly decoded its true meaning: the agonizing distance between Ryan and Cassidy O'Connell when industry handlers tried to divide them in the studio. Re-engineered in **The Cosmic Tidal Lock Sound™**, the song's "silent satellite" metaphor becomes literal—two voices separated across the vacuum of space, locking onto the same frequency across a million miles of static.

Originally the emotional centerpiece of 1987's *Electric Color*—chronicling how Cassidy's grounding presence kept Ryan tethered through sensory overload and industry hostility—*"My Anchor Holds"* becomes the gravitational thesis of *Starfield Calibration*. In orbital mechanics, tidal lock occurs when two bodies are permanently bound by mutual gravity; Cassidy's solo vocal performance transforms the 1987 acoustic-rock anthem into a weightless cathedral of analog warmth.

The title track of the band's 1989 sophomore album was originally an icy, high-BPM synth-pop single masking the band's growing exhaustion on the Apex promotional circuit (*"Pumping electricity instead of blood / Caught up in a technicolor flood"*). ## Rearview Mirror (Cosmic Tidal Lock Mix)
### Lore
On 1987's *Electric Color*, *"Rearview Mirror"* was Cassidy's quiet lament about feeling like a passenger in her own career (*"I'm just a passenger in this ride / With all this 'Stardust' left inside"*), as Apex Records reduced her and Ryan's love of the cosmos and rockets into a cheap corporate naming gimmick. Looking back twenty-four years later on 2011's *Starfield Calibration* from the helm of her own zero-gravity sanctuary, Cassidy flips the 1987 lyric on its head: *"I used to be a passenger on their ride / With all this Stardust locked inside / Now I'm at the helm of the frequency / And the whole horizon belongs to me!"*

Leaving the shadow for the starlight!

[Chorus]

The whole constellation is getting clearer!
Every storm we had to take
Every chain we had to break
Is shrinking in the rearview mirror
As the endless sky pulls us nearer!

They never knew where we'd begin
I used to be a passenger on their ride
With all this Stardust locked inside
Now I'm at the helm of the frequency
And the whole horizon belongs to me!

[Pre-Chorus]
The telemetry is clear and bright
Leaving the shadow for the starlight!

[Chorus]
I'm looking in the rearview mirror
The whole constellation is getting clearer!

As the endless sky pulls us nearer!

[Synthesizer Solo]

## Atmosphere (Cosmic Tidal Lock Mix)
### Lore
Originally recorded in September 1992 for the canceled *Friction* album (where rogue Apex executive Julian Vance infamously misinterpreted its literal space-shuttle re-entry physics—*"Here comes the friction, feel the fire"*—as sexual innuendo), *"Atmosphere"* existed for nineteen years only as an early rough cut (*"Atmosphere (1992 Demo)"* on the 1996 *Ad Astra* single and *"Atmosphere (Warehouse Demo)"* on 2007's *Lost Sounds*). Because that 1992 rough demo sounded like an early-1990s track tracked under label pressure, the band was never happy with it. On 2011's *Starfield Calibration*, Cassidy and Ryan finally deliver the **definitive, finalized version** of *"Atmosphere"*—using **The Cosmic Tidal Lock Sound™** to bring the song back to the lush, shimmering 1980s sound they love so much.

First recorded in the raw concrete echo of "The Fortress" for 1995's *The Warehouse Tapes*, *"No Hidden Gravity"* celebrated the band's liberation from predatory record contracts (*"Just a pure and weightless trajectory / In the quiet of the space we made"*). On *Starfield Calibration*, Cassidy replaces the 1995 warehouse grunge with the crystalline, zero-gravity shimmer the lyrics always envisioned—a pure, unencumbered orbit where nothing pulls the family down.

## Echoes in the Wire (Cosmic Tidal Lock Mix)
### Lore
Originally introduced on 1997's *Hard Reset* to permanently replace 1989's *"If You Walked Away"*—a forced Apex Records breakup ballad whose abandonment scenario caused Cassidy (who is autistic) severe backstage distress after every concert, requiring her brother and safe person Ryan to hold her tightly in twenty-minute deep-pressure grounding hugs. *"Echoes in the Wire"* is the story of Cassidy finding comfort, quiet, and safety when Ryan is hugging her tightly as the stage noise fades away (*"All the terrestrial static faded out / Leaving nothing of the doubt... We traded time for something real / A tidal lock that you can feel"*). In this 2011 **Cosmic Tidal Lock Mix**, Cassidy wraps that grounding embrace in warm, zero-gravity analog synthesizer pads and a soothing 96 BPM heartbeat.

## Fade to Dawn (Cosmic Tidal Lock Mix)
### Lore
First recorded in 1991 for the ill-fated *Friction* sessions and later rescued from the Apex vault for *Lost Sounds*, *"Fade to Dawn"* was the quiet morning-after truce written by Cassidy after the band's most brutal studio battles (*"We survived the midnight, we survived the storm"*). Serving as the grand finale of 2011's *Starfield Calibration*, Cassidy's solo vocal brings the planetarium dome lights slowly back up to a warm golden sunrise—reassuring Forger Nation from their Blacksburg sanctuary that The Stardust Engine is still alive and in orbit.

# Reignition (2015)
* **Narrative Era:** 2015
* **Real-World DSP Release:** 2026-10-26

## Reignition: Lights Up on Progress Street
### Lore
Thematically answering the sign-off of 2004's "Last Bus Home: Lights Out on Progress Street" ("Lights out on Progress Street... Goodnight, Forger Nation"), the opening anthem of 2015's *Reignition* celebrates the end of the band's decade-long **2005–2014 Burnout Hiatus**. Opening with warm acoustic piano chords that erupt into Cassidy's bright analog polyphonic analog brass fanfare, Tyler's booming gated-reverb snare, Evan's driving eighth-note bassline, and Ryan's soaring electric guitar, it marks the family's triumphant return to full-throttle 1980s AOR studio rock.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Warm, solitary acoustic piano chords in D Major)
(A bright, triumphant 1980s analog synthesizer brass fanfare erupts)
(Massive, pounding 80s gated-reverb snare drum roll and driving eighth-note bassline kick in)

[Spoken Word Intro]
(Cassidy - warm, smiling)
Good morning, Forger Nation.
(Ryan)
The switches are back on! Let's go!

[Verse 1]
(Ryan)
Ten years of quiet down a mountain lane
Watching the seasons wash the windowpane
We hung the guitars on the living room wall
Let the silence heal us down the narrow hall
(Cassidy)
We kept the promise that we made that night
Turned down the dimmers on the blinding light
No broken pieces, no fading away
We were just resting for a brand new day!

[Pre-Chorus]
(Ryan)
Feel the transformers hum beneath the floor!
(Cassidy)
Hear the twin rhythm knocking at the door!
(Ryan)
Every tube is glowing cherry red!
(Duet)
And every word we left unsaid is waking up ahead!

[Chorus]
(Duet)
This is Reignition! Throw the master switch!
Climbing out of the quiet to the highest pitch!
Lights up on Progress Street, the fire's burning bright
Splitting through the shadow of a ten-year night!
From the basement breakers to the stadium sky
The Stardust Engine never said goodbye!
This is Reignition! Hear the thrusters roar!
We're lighting up the valley like we did before!

[Verse 2]
(Cassidy)
My Juno keys are warm beneath my hands
Ready for the stages in a hundred lands
(Ryan)
Locked my wheels in place beside the microphone
Never had to roll a single mile alone
(Cassidy)
Evan on the bassline, Tyler on the snare
(Ryan)
Holly in the corner with a fortress to spare!
Five hearts beating on the very same floor
Opening the locks upon the studio door!

[Pre-Chorus]
(Cassidy)
Feel the transformers hum beneath the floor!
(Ryan)
Hear the twin rhythm knocking at the door!
(Cassidy)
Every tube is glowing cherry red!
(Duet)
And every dream we ever led is wide awake ahead!

[Chorus]
(Duet)
This is Reignition! Throw the master switch!
Climbing out of the quiet to the highest pitch!
Lights up on Progress Street, the fire's burning bright
Splitting through the shadow of a ten-year night!
From the basement breakers to the stadium sky
The Stardust Engine never said goodbye!
This is Reignition! Hear the thrusters roar!
We're lighting up the valley like we did before!

[Bridge]
(Music drops to pulsing analog synth pad and steady kick drum)
(Cassidy)
We didn't burn out, we just took our time
(Ryan)
Saved every spark for the second climb
(Cassidy)
Now the meters are pinning in the crimson zone!
(Ryan)
Blacksburg, Virginia—your band is home!

[Guitar & Synthesizer Solo]
(A soaring, melodic 1980s AOR electric guitar solo trading triumphant phrases with a bright polyphonic analog synthesizer lead)

[Final Chorus]
(Duet - Full Stadium Energy)
This is Reignition! Throw the master switch!
Climbing out of the quiet to the highest pitch!
Lights up on Progress Street, the fire's burning bright
Splitting through the shadow of a ten-year night!
From the basement breakers to the stadium sky
The Stardust Engine never said goodbye!
This is Reignition! Hear the thrusters roar!
We're lighting up the valley forevermore!

The fire is back!

## Main Engine Start
### Lore
Inspired by the Space Shuttle launch on the album's cover and the band's lifelong love of the rockets that take you to the cosmos ("Engine"), Ryan leads a 142 BPM 1980s arena-rock thriller structured around a Cape Canaveral launch countdown ("T-minus six seconds... Main Engine Start... Zero... Liftoff!"). The song celebrates the sheer physical exhilaration of feeling the band's creative thrusters roar back to full power after ten years on the ground.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Sweeping 1980s analog synthesizer arpeggios panning across the stereo field)

[Spoken Word Intro]
(Ryan - spoken clearly)
Guidance is internal.
T-minus ten... nine... eight...
Seven... six...
Main Engine Start!
Four... three... two... one...
Zero! Liftoff!

[Instrumental Hook]
(Explosive 142 BPM palm-muted electric guitar riff and thunderous 80s gated-reverb drums kick in)

[Verse 1]
(Ryan)
White-hot steam billowing across the pad
Every ounce of horsepower we ever had
Three million pounds of thrust beneath the floor
Shaking every bolt upon the hangar door!
We spent a decade anchored to the ground
Waiting for the moment when the clock came round
Now the gantry pulls away into the night
And we're riding on a column of electric light!

[Pre-Chorus]
(Ryan)
Feel the G-force pressing on your chest!
Put the titanium airframe to the test!
(Gang Vocals)
Roll program! Pitch into the sky!
(Ryan)
Watch the blue horizon of the earth go by!

[Chorus]
(Ryan & Gang Chorus)
Main Engine Start! Feel the boosters burn!
Past the point of hesitation, past no return!
Through the maroon clouds and the orange flame
Climbing to the heavens where we wrote our name!
Punching through the atmosphere with all our heart
Hold on to the throttle—it's Main Engine Start!

[Verse 2]
(Ryan)
Thirty miles up and the sky turns black
Nothing in the universe can hold us back
Solid rocket separation, clean and true
Look out the window at the endless view!
We built this vessel out of heart and steel
Two twin brothers and a brother on wheels
A sister on the keys and a sister at the helm
Crossing the border to a higher realm!

[Pre-Chorus]
(Ryan)
Feel the G-force pressing on your chest!
Put the titanium airframe to the test!
(Gang Vocals)
Max-Q cleared! Throttle up to full!
(Ryan)
Breaking every tether of the gravity pull!

[Chorus]
(Ryan & Gang Chorus)
Main Engine Start! Feel the boosters burn!
Past the point of hesitation, past no return!
Through the maroon clouds and the orange flame
Climbing to the heavens where we wrote our name!
Punching through the atmosphere with all our heart
Hold on to the throttle—it's Main Engine Start!

[Bridge]
(Half-time heavy arena rock groove with soaring synthesizer brass)
(Ryan)
They thought the countdown ended years ago
Thought the cold dark winter froze the afterglow
But an engine built for orbit never forgets the stars
Reaching for the heavens where the cosmos are!

[Guitar Solo]
(A soaring, virtuosic 1980s two-hand tapping electric guitar solo over galloping bass and rapid-fire double-kick drums)

[Final Chorus]
(Ryan & Gang Chorus)
Main Engine Start! Feel the boosters burn!
Past the point of hesitation, past no return!
Through the maroon clouds and the orange flame
Climbing to the heavens where we wrote our name!
Punching through the atmosphere with all our heart
Hold on to the throttle—it's Main Engine Start!

[Outro]
(Gang Vocals)
Main Engine Start!
(Ryan)
All thrusters at one hundred and four percent!
We are in orbit!
(Final blazing guitar harmonic and gated drum crescendo)

## The Iceberg: Submerged
### Lore
A sleek, driving 126 BPM 1980s melodic synth-rocker celebrating younger sister and CEO Holly O'Connell passing the Delaware Bar in August 2015 and executing "The Delaware Migration"—folding *Engine Room Records, LLC*, *Artists First Management, LLC*, *Stardust Properties, LLC*, and *Pacific Rim Properties, LLC* into anonymous Delaware corporate structures beneath *The O'Connell Family Revocable Trust*. As Cassidy sings over a sequenced analog bassline, nine-tenths of the family's $2.4B+ fortress sits permanently beneath the waterline where the outside world can never see it.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Sleek, pulsing 1980s sequenced analog synthesizer bassline in F# Minor)
(Shimmering polyphonic synth chords and crisp gated-reverb snare)
(Rhythmic chorused electric guitar riff locks in)

[Verse 1]
(Cassidy)
Up on the surface it's a quiet street
Five pairs of boots and a steady beat
Just a small-town band from the mountain air
No flashy limousines anywhere
The suited strangers with their legal pads
Looking for the fortune that the lottery had
They search the registries from coast to coast
Chasing after shadows and a paper ghost!

[Pre-Chorus]
(Cassidy)
They only see the tip above the ocean wave
They don't know the sanctuary our sister gave
Deep beneath the surface where the waters gleam
Safe inside the quiet of a vaulted dream!

[Chorus]
(Cassidy & Backing Harmonies)
Like an iceberg—nine-tenths underneath the sea!
Submerged in the deep where the world can't see!
On paper we're just five kids from a mountain town
While the fortress underneath can never be torn down!
You can't buy the master and you can't find the key
The Iceberg is submerged and the family is free!

[Verse 2]
(Cassidy)
Started with a notebook and a crimson pen
Sixteen years old with a steady plan
She crossed out the traps in the fine-print line
And built a shelter that would outlast time
Now the autumn wind blows across the bay
And the whole foundation is tucked away
From the western coast to the Blue Ridge crest
Held inside the harbor of the family chest!

[Pre-Chorus]
(Cassidy)
They only see the tip above the ocean wave
They don't know the sanctuary our sister gave
No public shareholders, no board of greed
Just the five of us and everything we'll ever need!

[Chorus]
(Cassidy & Backing Harmonies)
Like an iceberg—nine-tenths underneath the sea!
Submerged in the deep where the world can't see!
On paper we're just five kids from a mountain town
While the fortress underneath can never be torn down!
You can't buy the master and you can't find the key
The Iceberg is submerged and the family is free!

[Bridge]
(Atmospheric deep-ocean analog synth pad with fretless bass harmonics)
(Cassidy)
Let the tabloids guess, let the bankers pry
There's nothing on the radar underneath this sky
We play for the kids in the bleachers tonight
Because the foundation is watertight!

[Guitar & Synth Duel]
(Sleek, melodic 1980s electric guitar solo trading rapid arpeggios with a bright analog synthesizer lead)

[Final Chorus]
(Cassidy & Full Band)
Like an iceberg—nine-tenths underneath the sea!
Submerged in the deep where the world can't see!
On paper we're just five kids from a mountain town
While the fortress underneath can never be torn down!
You can't buy the master and you can't find the key
The Iceberg is submerged and the family is free!

[Outro]
(Sequenced analog synth bass pulses into the deep)
(Cassidy)
Submerged and untouchable.
Thank you, Holly.
(Final crisp synth chord and gated snare hit)

## Orbital Return
### Lore
Cassidy's lush, zero-gravity 1980s Cosmic Pop centerpiece (**The Cosmic Tidal Lock Sound™**), bridging her 2011 silver-anniversary studio project *Starfield Calibration* into the full four-piece band's 2015 comeback. Layered with sweeping analog polyphonic pads, cascading analog polyphonic arpeggios, and a weightless, singing lead guitar solo from Ryan, it captures the sensation of a spacecraft rounding the far side of the moon and locking back onto the radio beacon of home.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Lush, sweeping 1980s analog polyphonic analog synthesizer pads and sparkling analog polyphonic arpeggios)
(Warm melodic bassline and booming gated-reverb drums enter at 118 BPM)

[Verse 1]
(Cassidy)
Drifting in the silence on the lunar side
Where the radio waves have nowhere left to hide
Watching all the constellations wheeling slow
Three hundred thousand miles from the world below
Sometimes you need the vacuum and the velvet dark
To recalibrate the compass and protect the spark
Floating in the zero-G of our own design
Waiting for the telemetry to realign

[Pre-Chorus]
(Cassidy)
Then the horizon curves into the golden light
Earthrise breaking through the endless night
A familiar frequency begins to hum
Telling every instrument the time has come!

[Chorus]
(Cassidy)
This is our Orbital Return!
Feel the retro-rockets softly burn!
Coming around from the quiet side of the moon
Locking on the signal of a hometown tune!
Weightless in the starlight, steady on the line
Every single trajectory is yours and mine!
From the deepest cosmos to the atmosphere
This is our Orbital Return—and we are here!

[Verse 2]
(Cassidy)
Stardust in the capacitors, gold in the wire
Harmonic velocity and liquid fire
My brother's guitar singing through the solar wind
Picking up the melody where we began
Twin moons rising in a tidal lock
Steady as the pulse of an atomic clock
No longer hiding in the shadow cone
We're banking toward the beacon that brings us home!

[Pre-Chorus]
(Cassidy)
See the Blue Ridge glowing on the radar screen
The prettiest landing strip we've ever seen
Every frequency begins to hum
Telling Forger Nation that the time has come!

[Chorus]
(Cassidy)
This is our Orbital Return!
Feel the retro-rockets softly burn!
Coming around from the quiet side of the moon
Locking on the signal of a hometown tune!
Weightless in the starlight, steady on the line
Every single trajectory is yours and mine!
From the deepest cosmos to the atmosphere
This is our Orbital Return—and we are here!

[Bridge]
(Zero-gravity breakdown with shimmering bell synths and chorus guitar arpeggios)
(Cassidy)
Every orbit takes you far away
Only so it can bring you back someday
Stronger in the gravity, clearer in the sight
Wrapped inside a halo of electric light!

[Guitar Solo]
(A soaring, weightless, deeply melodic 1980s electric guitar solo floating over cascading analog synthesizer waves)

[Final Chorus]
(Cassidy & Ryan Harmonies)
This is our Orbital Return!
Feel the retro-rockets softly burn!
Coming around from the quiet side of the moon
Locking on the signal of a hometown tune!
Weightless in the starlight, steady on the line
Every single trajectory is yours and mine!
From the deepest cosmos to the atmosphere
This is our Orbital Return—and we are here!

[Outro]
(Cascading 1980s synthesizer arpeggios fade gently into warm cosmic starlight)
(Cassidy)
Telemetry locked. Welcome home.

## Ten Years in the Quiet
### Lore
Work-life balance in song form: an honest, deeply moving 84 BPM 1980s AOR power ballad reflecting on the **2005–2014 Burnout Hiatus**. After recording four studio albums in four years and reaching complete sensory and physical exhaustion at the end of the 2004 Norfolk tour, Ryan and Cassidy sing about how choosing to walk away from the spotlight for ten years—drinking coffee on the Progress Street porch, cooking family dinners, and protecting their neurodivergent boundaries—saved their lives and kept the five of them whole.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Gentle acoustic piano and clean chorused electric guitar arpeggios at 84 BPM)
(Warm analog synthesizer strings swell softly underneath)

[Verse 1]
(Ryan)
Two thousand and four under a concrete dome
Four albums in the rearview on the highway home
My hands were shaking on the steering rim
And the stage lights felt a little too bright and dim
(Cassidy)
Every room was spinning at a hundred degrees
Too many voices asking us to please
So we walked off the stage and we closed the door
And left the suitcases on the hallway floor

[Pre-Chorus]
(Ryan)
The world asked where we went and why we walked away
(Cassidy)
When we were at the summit of the game we play
(Duet)
Because a trophy doesn't matter if you lose your soul
So we chose each other just to keep us whole

[Chorus]
(Duet)
Ten years in the quiet, ten years in the shade
Best decision that this family ever made
Morning coffee on the Progress Street porch
Passing down the warmth instead of burning out the torch
No cameras in the kitchen, no schedules to keep
Just ten years in the quiet where the roots grow deep
And now we know the secret that the road can't teach:
Sometimes stepping back is how you keep the stars in reach

[Verse 2]
(Cassidy)
Sunday dinners at a wooden table for five
Learning what it feels like just to be alive
Walking through the autumn leaves without a crowd
Where the morning melody was gentle and proud
(Ryan)
Evan fixing bicycles out in the drive
Tyler laughing til the neighbors knew we were alive
Holly with her law books and her steady grace
Keeping peace and quiet in our favorite place

[Pre-Chorus]
(Massive gated-reverb drum fill enters)
(Cassidy)
They said if you step off the train you'll fade to gray
(Ryan)
But love doesn't rust when you put it away!
(Duet)
Now the batteries are full and the spirit is clear
And we're ready for the music that brought us here!

[Chorus]
(Duet - Full 1980s Arena Power Ballad)
Ten years in the quiet, ten years in the shade
Best decision that this family ever made
Morning coffee on the Progress Street porch
Passing down the warmth instead of burning out the torch
No cameras in the kitchen, no schedules to keep
Just ten years in the quiet where the roots grow deep
And now we know the secret that the road can't teach:
Sometimes stepping back is how you keep the stars in reach!

[Guitar Solo]
(A deeply emotional, soaring 1980s arena power-ballad guitar solo climbing into the upper register over lush synth strings)

[Bridge]
(Piano and vocals only)
(Cassidy)
If your heart is running on a worn-out wire
(Ryan)
You don't owe the world your very last fire
(Duet)
Turn the switches off until the storm has passed
That's how you build a love that's gonna last!

[Final Chorus]
(Duet - Soaring Crescendo)
Ten years in the quiet, ten years in the shade
Best decision that this family ever made!
Morning coffee on the Progress Street porch
Passing down the warmth instead of burning out the torch!
And now we know the secret that the road can't teach:
Stepping back together kept the stars in reach!

[Outro]
(Gentle acoustic piano and clean electric guitar fade out together)
(Cassidy)
Ten years stronger.
(Ryan)
Ready when you are, sis.

## Fifty-Two to Nothing: The Norfolk Reckoning
### Lore
Written and recorded in a burst of autumn excitement in October 2015 immediately after **Commonwealth Polytechnic Institute (CPI)** traveled down to Norfolk for **Virginia Dominion University (VDU)**'s Homecoming game. After VDU students printed thousands of mocking *"38–0"* t-shirts commemorating their 2014 upset, the Forgers walked into Norfolk and delivered a triumphant **52–0 shutout victory**. Featuring Tyler's thunderous marching snare cadence and a roaring stadium chorus, it became an instant autumn anthem across Blacksburg.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Thunderous marching drumline snare cadence by Tyler with massive 1980s gated reverb)
(Triumphant 1980s analog synthesizer brass fanfare in C Minor)
(Driving overdriven stadium electric guitar riff kicks in at 138 BPM)

[Verse 1]
(Ryan)
Three hundred sixty-five days with the numbers on the wall
Stenciled on the mirrors down the weight-room hall
We kept our focus steady through the winter rain
Working every morning til we broke the chain

Then we rolled down to the harbor for the autumn crown
Saw the old numbers printed all across the town
Banners in the tidewater, music in the street
Listen to the iron marching down the concrete!

(Ryan)
No holding back today! Keep the starters on the grass!
(Cassidy)
Every single quarter is a masterclass!
(Duet)
From the opening kickoff to the final cheer
Hear the anvils ringing loud and clear!

[Chorus]
(Duet & Stadium Gang Vocals)
Fifty-two to nothing! Put it on the board!
Hear the mighty thunder of the Forger chord!
You wore the old numbers on your Homecoming chest
Till the mountains came down and put the talk to rest!
Maroon and burnt orange underneath the autumn sky
Raise our banners high for CPI!
Fifty-two to nothing! Hear the stadium call
Forger Nation stands above it all!

Protecting every inch of the goal-line design

Maroon and burnt orange in the coastal breeze

Every single quarter is a masterclass!

From the opening kickoff to the final cheer
Hear the anvils ringing loud and clear!

Fifty-two to nothing! Put it on the board!
Hear the mighty thunder of the Forger chord!
You wore the old numbers on your Homecoming chest
Till the mountains came down and put the talk to rest!
Maroon and burnt orange underneath the autumn sky
Raise our banners high for CPI!

## Twin Frequency: The Rhythm Section
### Lore
A high-energy, groove-driven 130 BPM 1980s arena rocker spotlighting Ryan, Cassidy, and Holly's maternal cousins, identical twin brothers **Evan** (bass guitar) and **Tyler Wright** (drums)—the unshakeable rhythmic engine of The Stardust Engine since 1986. Built around a syncopated slap-and-fingerstyle bass groove locked to Tyler's booming octoban fills and gated-reverb snare, the track celebrates the twins' telepathic connection both on stage and behind the wheel of *The Iron Horse*.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Explosive 1980s octoban drum roll by Tyler slamming into a funky, syncopated electric bass groove by Evan at 130 BPM)

Finishing each other's sentences before they're even there

Locked into a pocket that could never lose

Locked in!

Twin power!

Steady as the gears inside an iron clock!
Two brothers, one heartbeat, driving through the night
Holding up the engine with all their might!
Turn up the low end—it's Twin Frequency!

## Neon on the Huckleberry Trail
### Lore
A nostalgic, shimmering 124 BPM 1980s melodic synth-pop and AOR gem celebrating autumn dusks in Blacksburg and Christiansburg—Cassidy riding her bicycle and Ryan rolling his everyday wheelchair along the paved six-mile **Huckleberry Trail** under a canopy of golden and crimson Appalachian maples as twilight settles over the New River Valley and the stadium lights of **The Crucible** flicker on in the distance.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Shimmering 1980s chorused electric guitar arpeggios and warm polyphonic analog synthesizer bells at 124 BPM)
(Breezy eighth-note bassline and crisp gated-reverb snare enter)

[Verse 1]
(Cassidy)
October five-thirty and the air turns crisp
Woodsmoke curling in a silver wisp
Maples turning crimson by the old rail bed
Canopy of burnt orange overhead
You're pushing on your hand-rims at an easy pace
With the mountain wind cooling off your face
Six miles of asphalt from the library lawn
Where the old Huckleberry train used to roll on

[Pre-Chorus]

In the mountain sanctuary that our hometown gave
Thirty years of music and we still belong
To the winding path where we wrote this song!

Just the valley dressed in its autumn dress
Watch the stars come out above the mountain's crest

## No Strings on the Master
### Lore
A scorching 146 BPM 1980s hard-AOR guitar anthem celebrating thirty years of fiercely guarded creative independence. Ryan calls out the 1980s corporate label executives at Apex Records who once tried to reduce Cassidy to their "Stardust" and Ryan to their "Engine" as a cheap marketing gimmick, raising a glass to Holly's 1986 `$1.00` Cross-License and Cassidy's 1996 buyout that ensured the family owns every master tape in the vault.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Blistering 146 BPM overdriven 1980s electric guitar riff in D Minor)
(Driving analog tonewheel organ and polyphonic synth stabs)
(Galloping bassline and rapid-fire gated-reverb drums kick in)

[Verse 1]
(Ryan)
Back in eighty-seven in a glass-walled tower
Men in tailored suits intoxicated by their power
Pointed at my sister, called her "Stardust" on a chart
Pointed at my wheelchair, called me "Engine" for the part!
Turned our love of rockets and the galaxies above
Into a cheap slogan that we never could love
Tried to put a price tag on a family name
Tried to lock the songbirds in a corporate frame!

[Pre-Chorus]
(Ryan)
They didn't know a sixteen-year-old girl with a pen
Had already outsmarted all the king's horses and men!
And when ninety-six hit like a lightning bolt from the sky
We bought the whole building and we waved goodbye!

[Chorus]
(Ryan & Gang Vocals)
There are no strings on the master! No chains on the tape!
No boardroom committee dictating the shape!
Every single note is in the family vault
Protected by the iron and the mountain basalt!
We play what we want and we answer to none
Underneath the fire of our own twin sun!
Spinning every reel a little bit faster—
There are no strings on the master!

[Verse 2]
(Ryan)
They said a band can't vanish for a ten-year rest
Without a label leaning on your weary chest
They said the catalog would go up on the block
To pay the quarterly dividends around the clock!
Well look inside the ledger—there's a zero owed!
We own the studio, the bus, and the road!
Not a single penny to a middleman's hand
Just five kids running our own promised land!

[Pre-Chorus]
(Ryan)
They didn't know a sixteen-year-old girl with a pen
Had already outsmarted all the king's horses and men!
From eighty-six forward til the stars burn cold
Not a single master will ever be sold!

[Chorus]
(Ryan & Gang Vocals)
There are no strings on the master! No chains on the tape!
No boardroom committee dictating the shape!
Every single note is in the family vault
Protected by the iron and the mountain basalt!
We play what we want and we answer to none
Underneath the fire of our own twin sun!
Spinning every reel a little bit faster—
There are no strings on the master!

[Bridge]
(Half-time heavy riff with soaring synth brass)
(Ryan)
Stardust is the cosmos!
Engine is the rocket ship!
Not a marketing gimmick on a salesman's lip!
And every magnetic reel spinning on the shelf
Belongs to the family that built it itself!

[Guitar Solo]
(A scorching, virtuosic 1980s hard-rock electric guitar solo with rapid two-hand tapping and soaring melodic bends)

[Final Chorus]
(Ryan & Gang Vocals)
There are no strings on the master! No chains on the tape!
No boardroom committee dictating the shape!
Every single note is in the family vault
Protected by the iron and the mountain basalt!
Spinning every reel a little bit faster—
There are no strings on the master!

[Outro]
(Blazing guitar harmony and rapid drum fills)
(Ryan)
One hundred percent owned!
Engine Room Records!
(Final ringing D-Minor power chord)

## Still Your Safe Person
### Lore
The emotional heart of *Reignition*. Opening with just Cassidy's acoustic grand piano and Ryan's weeping clean electric guitar before building into a monumental 78 BPM 1980s arena power ballad, the brother and sister sing to each other—and to every neurodivergent, overwhelmed, or healing listener in Forger Nation: *"When the static hits a hundred and the room begins to spin / I am still your safe person, let the quiet in."*

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Warm, intimate acoustic grand piano chord progression at 78 BPM)
(Weeping, clean melodic electric guitar counter-melody enters gently)

[Verse 1]
(Cassidy)
When the fluorescent lights are humming way too loud
And you're spinning in the middle of a noisy crowd
When the rules don't match the script inside your head
And every single wire is flashing crimson red
(Ryan)
You don't have to explain why your hands are shaking now
You don't have to force a smile or take a final bow
Just reach across the console, I'm right beside your chair
Breathing in the cadence of the quiet mountain air

[Pre-Chorus]
(Cassidy)
We've known each other since the nursery floor
(Ryan)
And I know what the heavy wooden door is for
(Duet)
No questions asked, no judgment in this room
Just a harbor in the middle of the sonic boom

[Chorus]
(Duet - Lush 1980s analog synth strings and booming gated-reverb drums enter)
When the static hits a hundred and the room begins to spin
I am still your safe person—let the quiet in!
Put your headphones on or lean against my shoulder tight
I will hold the perimeter throughout the longest night!
You don't have to be unbroken just to be enough
I will be the anchor when the ocean gets too rough
Through every single storm and every place we've been
I am still your safe person—let the quiet in!

[Verse 2]
(Ryan)
Back when the winter changed the road beneath my seat
And I had to learn a brand new way to move down Progress Street
You never looked at me with pity in your eyes
You just lowered every keyboard stand to match my size
(Cassidy)
And when the tour arenas made my senses overflow
You pulled the curtain closed and told the crowd we had to go
Because a brother and a sister don't leave anyone behind
When the world is overwhelming to a tender mind!

[Pre-Chorus]
(Ryan)
And to every single kid out in the bleachers tonight
(Cassidy)
Who feels a little different in the stadium light
(Duet)
Take this song and keep it in your coat pocket deep
For the nights when the mountain feels a little too steep!

[Chorus]
(Duet - Full Monumental 1980s Arena Power Ballad)
When the static hits a hundred and the room begins to spin
I am still your safe person—let the quiet in!
Put your headphones on or lean against my shoulder tight
I will hold the perimeter throughout the longest night!
You don't have to be unbroken just to be enough
I will be the anchor when the ocean gets too rough
Through every single storm and every place we've been
I am still your safe person—let the quiet in!

[Guitar Solo]
(A monumental, weeping 1980s arena power-ballad electric guitar solo, soaring with long sustained bends over grand piano and lush synth strings)

[Bridge]
(Stripped back to just grand piano and sibling vocals)
(Cassidy)
Breathe in for four...
(Ryan)
Hold it steady now...
(Cassidy)
Let the noise fall away...
(Duet)
You are safe right here today!

[Final Chorus]
(Duet & Soaring Choir Harmonies)
When the static hits a hundred and the room begins to spin
I am still your safe person—let the quiet in!
You don't have to be unbroken just to be enough
I will be the anchor when the ocean gets too rough
Through every single storm and every place we've been
I am still your safe person—let the quiet in!

[Outro]
(Acoustic grand piano and clean electric guitar resolve on a warm, lingering C-Major chord)
(Cassidy - soft)
I've got you. Always.

## Where the Stars Still Burn
### Lore
The sweeping, horizon-bound 134 BPM 1980s AOR and cosmic stadium-rock finale of *Reignition*. Looking up at the autumn constellations above the Blue Ridge after a ten-year hiatus, Ryan and Cassidy celebrate the enduring bond of their five-person family—knowing that time has not dimmed the spark they lit in 1986 and that the open highway ahead is still waiting where the stars still burn.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Lush, sweeping 1980s polyphonic analog synthesizer fanfare and shimmering arpeggiated keyboards in G Major)
(Twin harmonizing electric guitars and massive 80s gated-reverb drums enter at 134 BPM)

[Verse 1]
(Ryan)
Standing on the ridgeline where the mountain meets the sky
Watching thirty autumn seasons go rolling by
We started with a four-track and a borrowed set of keys
Chasing down a signal through the Appalachian trees
(Cassidy)
The maps have all been folded and the highways changed their names
We stepped out of the spotlight and we walked away from games
But look up at Orion hanging steady in the blue
The very same horizon is still waiting here for you!

[Pre-Chorus]
(Ryan)
Time can weather iron, time can silver-streak the hair
(Cassidy)
But it cannot touch a melody that's built to share!
(Duet)
Open up the throttle, let the final chapter start
With a compass in the dashboard and a rocket in our heart!

[Chorus]
(Duet & Full Band Harmonies)
Out where the stars still burn! Across the open night!
Every mile ahead of us is bathed in golden light!
We took the long road home, we weathered every turn
And found the fire waiting out where the stars still burn!
Five hearts on the frequency, steady and true
The universe is wider than the one we knew!
Keep the engines running til the morning sun returns—
Meet us on the horizon where the stars still burn!

[Verse 2]
(Cassidy)
No regrets behind us in the valley down below
Every quiet winter only helped the timber grow
(Ryan)
Four wheels on the pavement and four strings on the bass
Ten drumsticks in the quiver and a smile on every face!
(Cassidy)
We don't play for yesterday or monuments of stone
(Ryan)
We play because this music is the truest home we've known!

[Pre-Chorus]
(Cassidy)
Time can weather iron, time can silver-streak the hair
(Ryan)
But it cannot touch a melody that's built to share!
(Duet)
Open up the throttle, let the next adventure start
With a compass in the dashboard and a rocket in our heart!

[Chorus]
(Duet & Full Band Harmonies)
Out where the stars still burn! Across the open night!
Every mile ahead of us is bathed in golden light!
We took the long road home, we weathered every turn
And found the fire waiting out where the stars still burn!
Five hearts on the frequency, steady and true
The universe is wider than the one we knew!
Keep the engines running til the morning sun returns—
Meet us on the horizon where the stars still burn!

[Bridge]
(Music softens to shimmering analog synth arpeggios and clean chorus guitar)
(Cassidy)
Look how far we traveled from the basement floor
(Ryan)
And the best songs that we carry haven't been sung before!
(Music swells with thunderous gated-reverb drum roll)
(Duet)
As long as we're together, the night is never cold
And the story of the Engine will never grow old!

[Guitar & Synthesizer Solo]
(An epic, horizon-spanning 1980s stadium-rock twin electric guitar and analog synthesizer solo soaring over pounding gated drums)

[Final Chorus]
(Duet & Full Band Harmonies)
Out where the stars still burn! Across the open night!
Every mile ahead of us is bathed in golden light!
We took the long road home, we weathered every turn
And found the fire waiting out where the stars still burn!
Five hearts on the frequency, steady and true
The universe is wider than the one we knew!
Keep the engines running til the morning sun returns—
Meet us on the horizon where the stars still burn!

[Outro]
(Soaring twin guitar harmonies and cascading analog synthesizer bells)
(Cassidy)
Where the stars still burn!
(Ryan)
See you out on the horizon!
(Final triumphant, sustained G-Major stadium chord fading into starlight)


# Live at the Crucible (2016) [LiveAlbum]
* **Narrative Era:** 2016
* **Real-World DSP Release:** 2026-10-26

## Ignition: The Forger's Call (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
The very first time in history that **"Ignition: The Forger's Call"** was ever played and recorded for a live concert release (previously, the only way to hear *Ignition* was to attend a CPI Home Football Game in person inside **The Crucible**). With no opening act—just CPI Alumni (*Class of 1989*) coming home to play for their alma mater—Ryan O'Connell rolls out to the edge of the stage in his manual chair, recalls the October 1986 student contest cassette recorded by five kids (Ryan, Cassidy, Evan, Tyler, and 16-year-old dual-enrolled Holly O'Connell) in the garage on Progress Street and protected by Holly's `$1.00` Perpetual Mutual Cross-License, and cues the stadium blackout. As Tyler strikes his steel brake drum (`TING... BOOM!`), 65,000 Ironheads execute **The Stomp** on the galvanized bleachers of **The Anvil** until the Copernicus Hall seismographs spike, and Ryan shouts his triumphant November 2015 `52–0` ad-lib (*"We're taking the Anchor BACK to the Mountains!"*) while the four-piece rock band and the 300-piece **CPI Marching Forgers** erupt into a deafening call-and-response of **"IGNITION!"**

### Lyrics

[Ambient Sound: Massive Crowd Roar, Wind, Feedback]

[Spoken Intro - Ryan O'Connell]
(Gravelly voice, intimate, over faint synth pad)

Thirty years ago...
We were just five kids in a garage on Progress Street.
We didn't have a record deal...
We didn't have a tour bus...
All we had was a cassette tape... and an idea.

(Voice gets louder, commanding)
We entered a contest to write a song for this stadium.
We wanted to make something loud enough...
To scare the HELL out of anyone who dared to come into our house!

(Crowd cheers swell)
For thirty years... you guys have been stomping your feet to our song every single Saturday.
So tonight... for the 30th Anniversary...
Let's see if we can break the seismographs!

[Drum Fill: Slow, Massive, Thundering Stadium Kit - 82 BPM]
[male]
IGNITION!

[Crowd Response: 60,000 People]
(IGNITION!)

[Music Starts: Slow, Crushing 82 BPM Industrial Riff - Heavy Half-Time Kick-Drum Groove]

The lights go dark... the tension builds...
A single pounding heavy sound...
The hammer striking on the steel...
It shakes the air... it shakes the ground!
The pressure's on... the time is now...
We're here to forge our name!
The spark is lit... the fire burns...
(Ryan Ad-Lib - Shouted)
We're taking the Anchor BACK to the Mountains!

[Chorus]
[male]
IGNITION!
[Crowd Chant]
(IGNITION!)

[male]
This is the Forger's Call!

Strike! ... Strike! ... Strike the steel!

## CPI Tech Triumph: The Marching Forgers (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
Directly out of the final anvil strike of *"Ignition"*, the Drum Major's whistle pierces the night air (`Tweet-Tweet-Tweet-Tweet!`) and **"The Engine Room"**—the high-tension Kevlar marching snare battery of the 300-piece **CPI Marching Forgers**—launches into the official Commonwealth Polytechnic Institute Fight Song. Forming the **CPI Hammer and Anvil** across the turf while 65,000 students and alumni clap in time and roar *"GO! FORGERS! FIGHT! FORGERS! WIN! CPI!"*, the Marching Forgers turn the opening minutes of the concert into the ultimate Saturday night inside The Crucible.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
(Deafening stadium crowd roar)
(Piercing Drum Major whistle blows: Tweet-Tweet-Tweet-Tweet!)
(Crisp high-tension Kevlar marching snare battery roll-off by "The Engine Room")
(Explosive 300-piece collegiate brass chord blast in Bb Major)

[Part I: The Engine Room Cadence]
(Driving Kevlar marching snares, tenor quads, tonal bass drums, and rumbling sousaphone ostinato at 144 BPM)

[Part II: CPI Tech Triumph Main Fight Song]
(Soaring trumpets, mellophones, and trombones carry the triumphant Commonwealth Polytechnic Institute Fight Song melody over a precision marching beat)

[Stadium Crowd Chant]
(65,000 fans clapping in rhythm with the band)
(Crowd)
GO! FORGERS!
(Brass Fanfare Response)
(Crowd)
FIGHT! FORGERS!
(Brass Fanfare Response)
(Crowd)
WIN! CPI!

[Part III: The Hammer and Anvil Formation]
(Majestic brass countermelody and rapid woodwind flourishes as the 300-piece band locks into the CPI Hammer and Anvil formation)
(High-speed Kevlar drumline roll)

[Finale]
(Massive sustained 300-horn triumph chord)
(Sharp Drum Major cut-off snap to instant silence, followed by a deafening 65,000-person stadium eruption)


## The Stardust Engine (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
While standard Stardust Engine concerts open directly with **"The Stardust Engine"**, here at their alma mater it serves as the culminating third movement of the opening sequence—performed with all 300 members of the **CPI Marching Forgers** still on the stage! Harmonizing 300 collegiate brass instruments and sousaphones with Cassidy's iconic 1986 **"Stardust"** analog synthesizer leitmotif and locking the Kevlar snare battery to Ryan's **"Engine"** guitar and Tyler's gated drums, the performance brings the stadium to a fever pitch before the Marching Forgers march off through the field tunnel—convincing everyone in the building that their guest appearance for the night is over.

### Lyrics

[Live Stadium Intro]
(The 300-piece CPI Marching Forgers remain in formation across the stage and field)
[female]
Keep the Marching Forgers right here with us!
[male]
Thirty years ago in 1986, Evan and Tyler walked into our basement and we wrote our very first song together about the cosmos and the rockets that get you there! Let's hear it, CPI!

(Verse 1)
We were looking for a signal
A light in the endless black
A single voice in the universe
To tell us we're on the right track

(Verse 2)
We found the fire in the wires
We found the heart in the machine
Building a dream from nothing
A sound the world has never seen

(Chorus)
We are the Stardust Engine!
Racing through the night
We're burning on the fuel of a brand new light
We are the Stardust Engine!
Yeah, we're taking flight
Turning our future on, shining so bright!

(Verse 3)
They tried to ground us, keep us small
Tell us that we were gonna fall
But they don't know the things we know
They haven't seen the places we'll go

(Chorus)
We are the Stardust Engine!
Racing through the night
We're burning on the fuel of a brand new light
We are the Stardust Engine!
Yeah, we're taking flight
Turning our future on, shining so bright!

(Guitar Solo)
(A soaring, melodic solo that trades licks with a bright synthesizer hook)

(Bridge)
We are the cosmos, we are the fire
We're taking this one, higher and higher
We are the Stardust!
(We are the Engine!)
We are the Stardust Engine!

(Chorus)
We are the Stardust Engine!
Racing through the night
We're burning on the fuel of a brand new light
We are the Stardust Engine!
Yeah, we're taking flight
Turning our future on, shining so bright!

(Outro)
Shining so bright...
We're taking flight...
(fades out on an epic, sustained guitar chord)

## Main Engine Start (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
As the main body of the Marching Forgers files into the tunnel (leaving their 20-piece Lead Brass & Drumline Honor Guard at the front lip of the turf), the core four-piece band—Cassidy on her wraparound synthesizers, Ryan in his manual chair at center-left on lead guitar, and identical twins Evan (bass) and Tyler (drums) on the back risers—backed on the stage-left riser by their 5-piece "Orbital Crew" stadium touring section (Marcus Vance on rhythm guitar, Darius "Doc" Holloway on second kit/percussion, Claire Lindqvist on auxiliary synths, and CPI Jazz soloists Prof. Julian Vance on trumpet and Elena Rostova on sax so not a single pre-recorded backing track is ever used on stage) slams straight into the 142 BPM countdown thriller from 2015's *Reignition*. Sixty-five thousand fans count down from ten to liftoff in unison.

## Hard Reset (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
Rewinding to the 1997 title track that launched their independent stadium era after buying out their masters in 1996, Ryan and Cassidy trade blistering verses over Evan and Tyler's pounding arena rhythm section.

### Lyrics

[Verse 1]
The G-force fades, the shaking stops
We're floating past the mountain tops
The old computer's dead and gone
We're running on the break of dawn

[Verse 2]
The screens are black, the air is still
We're drifting on a silent hill
Engage the manual override
There's nowhere left for us to hide

[Pre-Chorus]
Thrusters? Ready
Vector? Steady
We have to jump the spark
To light up the dark!

[Chorus]
Hard Reset!
Flip the switch and clear the cache
We’re rising from the static crash
Hard Reset!
The code is new, the core is bright
We’re burning through the edge of night
No ground control!
No gravity!

## Not Your Doll (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
A massive, full-throttle stadium production number where Cassidy O'Connell hands her wraparound synthesizer rig over to touring keyboardist Claire Lindqvist, throws on a black leather jacket, and takes the stage **FRONT AND CENTER** at the lip of the thrust stage. Channeling ferocious 1980s hard-rock frontwoman grit, she tears apart Apex Records' 1980s attempt to package her as their fragile, obedient "Stardust" pop mascot while the 120-foot curved LED wall shatters a neon-pink porcelain doll mask into roaring crimson flames and twin fountains of gold pyrotechnics erupt across The Crucible.
