## Escape Velocity (Ad Astra) (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
**THE LIVE ALBUM PREMIERE — THE ONLY RECORDED LIVE CONCERT PERFORMANCE OF *"ESCAPE VELOCITY (AD ASTRA)"* IN THE BAND'S ENTIRE CATALOG.** Originally written and recorded by **Cassidy O'Connell** in 1995 during the financial struggle of the band's Wilderness Years (*The Warehouse Tapes*) and later featured as the closing magnum opus of 1997's *Hard Reset* (Disc 2: *The Twin Moons*), this monumental four-movement progressive symphonic rock suite (*Part I: The Cage*, *Part II: Ignition [Ad Astra]*, *Part III: The Cosmos*, and *Part IV: Re-entry [The New World]*) is Cassidy's autobiography and what she considers her single most important piece of work. Because it had not been written yet during 1990's *Live in Chicago* and was omitted from the tighter two-hour waterfront amphitheater setlist of 2005's *Live in Norfolk*, **2016's *Live at the Crucible* is the ONLY live album where you can experience *"Escape Velocity (Ad Astra)"* performed live**.

To open **Act III: *Cosmic Tidal Lock*** in astrophysical launch order (achieving the `11.2 km/s` escape velocity required to break free of Earth's gravity before coasting to *Apoapsis* and locking into *Moon 1: Tidal Lock*), all four towering stadium floodlight banks inside The Crucible shut off completely, plunging 65,000 fans into the pitch-black Appalachian starfield while overhead planetarium lasers and the 120-foot curved LED screen transform the bowl into deep space. Across **13 minutes and 3 seconds** of uninterrupted live musicianship with **The Orbital Crew**, Cassidy journeys from the suffocating trauma of the 1992 *Friction* trial (*"The Shark was right, the nuke went off! / We won the battle, but at what cost?!"*), through the roaring rocket liftoff of *Ignition* (`0:00–6:30`) and the stadium-shaking **Main Engine Cutoff** applause at `6:30`, into the weightless zero-gravity sanctuary of *The Cosmos* (`6:30–10:45`, where she sings the line that defines the band's 1986 origin: *"This is the Stardust that is me! / The cosmos and the rocket's fire! / My one, my only true desire!"*), and finally into the triumphant stadium splashdown of *Part IV: Re-entry* (`10:45–13:03`), where all 65,000 Forgers roar ***"HELD BY THE SUN!"*** in unison before Cassidy whispers a breathless, deeply moved ***"Thank you so much..."*** into the microphone—bringing her 26-year journey from 1990's *"If You Walked Away"* full circle under the Blacksburg stars.

### Lyrics

[Live Stadium Crowd Hush]
[Ambient Synth Drone & Haunting Woodwind Intro]

[High-Speed Prog-Rock Launch & Melodic Guitar Solo]

## Apoapsis (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
Having achieved escape velocity (`11.2 km/s`) to break free of Earth's gravity on the preceding 13-minute centerpiece, the vessel coasts upward to the highest point of its elliptical orbit on **"Apoapsis"**—the opening movement of **The Apoapsis Suite** from Disc 2 of 1997's *Hard Reset*. As the stadium bowl remains bathed in deep-space planetarium projections, Cassidy's shimmering analog synthesizers, Evan's floating fretless bassline, and Ryan's volume-pedal guitar swells recreate the weightless silence of deep space inside The Crucible.

### Lyrics

[Instrumental]
*(The track opens with a sprawling, ethereal ambient intro across the darkened stadium. Shimmering, pristine analog synthesizers wash over the 65,000-seat bowl.)*

*(Evan introduces a slow, highly melodic bassline that anchors the floating atmosphere, joined by spacious, echoing percussion from Tyler Wright and Darius "Doc" Holloway.)*

*(Ryan utilizes a volume pedal on his electric guitar, creating swelling, atmospheric waves of sound that mimic the slow, majestic rotation of a planet viewed from high orbit.)*


## Second Apoapsis (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
The second movement of **The Apoapsis Suite** from Disc 2 of 1997's *Hard Reset*. Drawing structural inspiration from the sprawling, multi-part instrumental epics on **Origin**'s self-titled 1983 album, **"Second Apoapsis"** flows gaplessly out of *"Apoapsis"*, introducing cinematic tension and intricate interlocking synthesizer arpeggios between Cassidy O'Connell and touring keyboardist Claire Lindqvist before dissolving directly into the vocal finale of the suite, *"The Spectrum (True Color)"*.

### Lyrics

[Instrumental]
*(Bleeding directly out of "Apoapsis", the spacious stadium percussion slowly begins to drive a more cinematic, deliberate rhythm.)*

*(Cassidy's synthesizers grow brighter and more complex, weaving intricate arpeggios around Evan's melodic bassline.)*

*(Ryan's volume-pedal guitar swells transform into a massive, echoing, cinematic build across the stadium PA, reaching a towering crescendo before suddenly dropping away into a quiet, ethereal outro that sets the stage for the vocals of "The Spectrum [True Color]".)*


## The Spectrum (True Color) (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
The sweeping vocal finale of **The Apoapsis Suite** from Disc 2 of 1997's *Hard Reset*. This progressive space-rock epic reimagines the band's 1987 debut single, *"Electric Color"*, from a sanitized corporate pop track into a sprawling observation of the cosmos (*"We left the atmosphere, the world was gray / The heavy gravity has finally faded away... And we are seeing the true spectrum! / A billion stars burning ultraviolet and blue"*). Performed here in **Act III: *Cosmic Tidal Lock***, it also serves as a brilliant mid-concert foreshadowing of the secret **Act VII True Ending**, when the band and all 300 **CPI Marching Forgers** will reclaim the original 1987 *"Electric Color"* as a blistering 1980s AOR Stadium Rock anthem to close the night.

### Lyrics

[Intro: The Gray]
*(The track opens seamlessly out of the fading echoes of "Second Apoapsis". A slow, pulsing bassline anchors a massive, shimmering synth pad across the darkened stadium before Cassidy's ethereal vocals drift in.)*

[Verse 1]
We left the atmosphere, the world was gray
The heavy gravity has finally faded away
We were living in a room with the curtains drawn tight
But out here in the void, there is only the light

[Pre-Chorus]
The terrestrial sounds are just a buzz in the ear
A static station that we no longer hear
The silence is turning into a song
This is the orbit where we belong

[Chorus]
And we are seeing the true spectrum!
A billion stars burning ultraviolet and blue
Dancing in the dust of a cosmic summer
The nebula is splitting the dark in two
This is the electric color!
The universe waking up for you...

[Instrumental Spacewalk]
*(A sweeping instrumental section. Evan's bassline locks into a slow, hypnotic groove while Cassidy layers intricate, sparkling synthesizer arpeggios that mimic stars passing by the observation deck window.)*

[Verse 2]
They wanted a painted sky, a plastic sun
A little radio hit when the day was done
But you cannot capture the burning of a star
When you keep the engine locked inside a jar
We're charting the coordinates they couldn't see
Out here in the quiet of the zero-G

[Pre-Chorus]
The terrestrial sounds are just a buzz in the ear
A static station that we no longer hear
The silence is turning into a song
This is the orbit where we belong

[Chorus]
And we are seeing the true spectrum!
A billion stars burning ultraviolet and blue

Ultraviolet and blue.
We finally found the color.

## Moon 2: Roche Limit (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
Exploding directly out of the fading starfield of Cassidy's *"Moon 1: Tidal Lock"* to kick off **Act IV: *Roche Limit & Mile Marker 98 (Moon 2 — Ryan's Heavy-Friction Orbit)***, **"Moon 2: Roche Limit"** (from Disc 2 of 1997's *Hard Reset* and Track 1 of Ryan's 2003 solo-led concept album *Mile Marker 98*) shatters the zero-gravity calm of the stadium with crimson strobe lights, pounding double-kit drums, and Ryan O'Connell's blistering, distorted electric guitar. Where Cassidy's *Moon 1* used the astrophysics of a tidal lock to celebrate stable, unbreakable loyalty, Ryan uses the **Roche limit**—the exact gravitational distance at which a satellite is torn to pieces by tidal forces—as a visceral, high-friction thesis statement confronting the trauma of the December 10, 1990 crash.

### Lyrics

[Intro]
*(An aggressive, pounding 1980s drum fill shatters the ambient space. Ryan's heavy, distorted electric guitar riff locks in with Evan's driving synth-bass.)*

[Chorus]
We crossed the Roche Limit!
Tearing me apart, tearing me apart!

Yeah, we crossed the Roche Limit!

I should've listened to the things they said

I feel the friction underneath my skin
I'm caught in the orbit of a dangerous star
I didn't mean to take it this far!

[Pre-Chorus]

## Impact (Mile Marker 98) (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
The visceral title cut of Ryan's 2003 concept album *Mile Marker 98* (*Moon 2*). Translating the night of December 10, 1990 into a cosmic collision in a treacherous debris field, **"Impact (Mile Marker 98)"** recounts how Ryan was cautiously piloting the band's 1988 Holt Freighter van at 40 MPH with hazard beacons active when a speeding vehicle clipped their rear quarter panel near Mile Marker 98, rolling the van twice and leaving Ryan pinned in the wreckage with a complete T10 spinal cord injury (*"We hit a massive fragment in the great unknown / At Mile Marker 98, I lost the throne! / We're barely alive, but we survived the night!"*).

### Lyrics

[Intro]
*(A driving, heavy 1980s electric guitar riff cuts through the stadium darkness, backed by a pounding, relentless hard-rock drumbeat.)*

[Verse 1]
I was holding the controls, trying to beat the storm
Trying to keep the Freighter steady, trying to keep it warm
Running forty through the dark, the hazard beacons bright
We were making steady time across the frozen night
But a shadow came flying through the passing lane
Too fast for the ice, too fast for the rain!

[Pre-Chorus]
A sudden violent strike on the quarter panel!
Ripped us right out of the safety channel!
Caught inside the debris field, spinning out of control
The massive broken planet taking its toll!

[Chorus]
Impact! Spinning off the line!
Impact! Shattering the design!
Rolled twice in the black, thrown into the cold
The heaviest gravity the metal can hold
We hit a massive fragment in the great unknown
At Mile Marker 98, I lost the throne!
We're barely alive, but we survived the night!
Yeah, we're barely alive!

[Verse 2]
I wake up pinned underneath the twisted steel
I'm screaming to the console but I cannot feel
The alarms are blaring, everything is red
But I can hear the crew breathing in the bed
The T-10 circuit is completely blown
I'm looking at a body that is not my own!

[Pre-Chorus]
A sudden violent strike on the quarter panel!
Ripped us right out of the safety channel!
Caught inside the debris field, spinning out of control
The massive broken planet taking its toll!

[Chorus]

The heaviest gravity the metal can hold
We hit a massive fragment in the great unknown
At Mile Marker 98, I lost the throne!
We're barely alive, but we survived the night!
Yeah, we're barely alive!

[Guitar Solo]
*(The rhythm section drops into a heavy, stomping groove as Ryan unleashes a blistering, emotional 1980s electric guitar solo capturing the adrenaline and catastrophic loss of control.)*

The engine is running, but the connection is dead.
Nothing below the damage, just the static in my head.
They pulled me from the wreckage, pulled me from the fire.
But I left half of myself in the twisting wire.

Mile Marker 98.

## Mayday: The Intercept (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
Transitioning directly from *Moon 1* into the heavy, high-friction arena rock of 2003's *Moon 2: Mile Marker 98*, Ryan and Cassidy sing the story of how their family intercepted the darkness after the December 10, 1990 Interstate 81 Mile Marker 98 crash and brought everyone home alive.

## Star City: Orbital Ward (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
Following the rescue of *"Mayday: The Intercept"*, **"Star City: Orbital Ward"** (from 2003's *Mile Marker 98*) slows the tempo into a brooding, heavy 1980s hard-rock power ballad that transposes Ryan's 1990 recovery at Star City General Hospital into an orbital medical bay. Translating the strict daily survival routines of **"Protocol T-10"**—the four-hour hydration and catheterization clock, the 15-minute wheelchair pressure weight shifts, and Evan and Tyler's synchronized "Freight Dog" twin-lifts—into the manual maintenance of a damaged but surviving starship, this performance directly sets the stage for the next Act (*Our Own Frequency*), where Ryan will strip away the sci-fi metaphors on *"Brother's Keeper"* and talk openly about how those daily ADLs led to tonight's decision to end global touring and stay home in Blacksburg.

### Lyrics

[Spoken Intro]
*(Spoken clearly, calmly, and methodically by the Planetarium Narrator over a slow, echoing heartbeat monitor and Ryan's brooding clean electric guitar.)*

Following the fragmentation event, the central engine has been salvaged and transported to the Star City Orbital Ward.
Diagnostics indicate a complete and permanent severing of the T-10 power conduit.
Automated systems below the damage threshold have officially failed.
Strict manual maintenance protocols must now be initiated to ensure system survival.

[Intro]
*(The narrator cuts out. The clean guitar is joined by a heavy, slow, stomping 1980s drum beat and a deep, pulsing bassline.)*

A heavy metal mountain that I still have to climb!
In the Orbital Ward!

Star City Orbital Ward!
Strapped to the table, strapped to the board!

## Twin Moons: Touchdown (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
Closing **Act IV: *Roche Limit & Mile Marker 98 (Moon 2)*** and uniting both halves of the 10-song *Twin Moons* cosmic journey (*Act III: Cosmic Tidal Lock* + *Act IV: Roche Limit & Mile Marker 98*), **"Twin Moons: Touchdown"** is the triumphant sibling duet that concludes 2003's *Mile Marker 98*. Fusing Ryan's heavy, distorted 1980s arena-rock guitar with Cassidy's sweeping analog synthesizers in locked unison (*"The Engine and the Anchor locking into the groove / We finally found the gravity, we're ready to move! / Touchdown!"*), the song brings the vessel out of the freezing vacuum of space and sets it safely down on solid ground in Blacksburg—concluding with the Planetarium Narrator's sign-off (*"Atmospheric entry complete. Gravity is stable... Please exit safely, and welcome home"*) right as Evan and Tyler leave the stage for the intimate living-room acoustic block of **Act V: *Our Own Frequency***.

### Lyrics

*(A massive, thundering 1980s hard-rock drum fill explodes out of the gate. Instantly, Ryan's heavy, distorted electric guitar locks into a triumphant riff, mirrored note-for-note by Cassidy's sweeping analog synthesizer.)*

I'm breaking through the clouds, I'm reading every sign
The atmospheric fire is fading in the rear
I'm dropping down the landing gear!

I held the heavy tether just to pull you back

[Pre-Chorus: Ryan & Cassidy]

We're crossing the terminator side by side!

The center of the universe is coming into view!

[Chorus: Ryan & Cassidy]
Twin Moons! Touching down on the solid ground!
Twin Moons! Listen to the heavy sound!
The metal and the stardust shining in the light
We're walking from the shadow and surviving the night!
The Engine and the Anchor locking into the groove
We finally found the gravity, we're ready to move!
Touchdown!

[Guitar and Synthesizer Solo]
*(Ryan and Cassidy trade solos across the stage—Ryan unleashing a blistering, bluesy hard-rock guitar lead and Cassidy answering immediately with a soaring, ethereal synthesizer run before intertwining in twin harmony.)*

[Bridge]
(Ryan) I thought I lost the wheels in the middle of the crash.
(Cassidy) But we built a sanctuary right out of the ash!
(Both) No more floating in the freezing unknown!
The orbit is over, we are finally home!

[Chorus: Ryan & Cassidy]

Atmospheric entry complete. Gravity is stable.
On behalf of the crew, we would like to thank you for visiting the Engine Room Records Planetarium.
The simulation is now concluded.
Please exit safely, and welcome home.


## Our Own Frequency (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
**Act IV: *Our Own Frequency* — The Intimate Brother & Sister Acoustic Block (Part 1 of 3).** Recorded in complete secrecy in the living room of the Blacksburg house during the middle of the band's decade-long Burnout Hiatus, 2008's *Our Own Frequency* was never toured, promoted, or accompanied by press interviews—Ryan and Cassidy simply uploaded it to the Engine Room Records website on a Tuesday morning with a one-sentence note: *"We're still here, and we're okay."* At the midpoint of *Live at the Crucible*, Evan and Tyler leave the stage completely, and the towering stadium light rigs dim to two solitary warm spotlights at center stage: Ryan in his wheelchair with an acoustic guitar, Cassidy at the piano, and two microphones. For the first time in front of a live audience, brother and sister strip away the stadium walls of sound to share their most private living-room conversation with their CPI family.

### Lyrics

[Audience Applause Softens to a Respectful Hush]
[Single Warm Spotlight on Acoustic Guitar and Piano]

[Spoken Word Intro]
[female]
Evan and Tyler are gonna take five backstage for a little bit. Back in two thousand and eight, right in the middle of our ten-year hiatus, Ryan and I couldn't face the cameras or the stadiums anymore. So we locked the front door of our house right down the road on Progress Street, sat down in the living room with just an acoustic guitar, a piano, and two microphones, and recorded an album we never toured.
[male]
We just uploaded it on a Tuesday morning with one sentence: "We're still here, and we're okay." We never thought we'd play these three songs in a stadium... but tonight, sixty-five thousand of you feel just like our living room.

(A warm, intimate acoustic guitar and piano duet intro in a hushed stadium)

They want to tell us how to burn tonight
But you and me, we know the truth
They can't buy what we built in our youth

Look at the monsters that we have become

And build ourselves a signal fire

Just a brother and a sister and the time we spend
On our own frequency
Just you and me

Just you and me

[male]

On our own frequency

## Brother's Keeper (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
**Act IV Centerpiece: *"Brother's Keeper"* — The Intimate Reality of Ryan's ADLs & The End of Touring / Forger Nation Summer Festival Announcement (Part 2 of 3).** The emotional centerpiece of 2008's *Our Own Frequency* and the most private, vulnerable song Ryan and Cassidy have ever written. *"Brother's Keeper"* is an unflinchingly intimate look at the life-or-death mutual protection between brother and sister and what Cassidy and the family go through every single day taking care of Ryan's **ADLs (Activities of Daily Living)** due to his T10 paralysis from the 1990 crash—performing his daily toileting requirements and morning anti-atrophy leg stretches, pushing his wheelchair when the road is steep, and sharing a bed pushed against the wall at night with Ryan on the inside and Cassidy sleeping on the outside edge to act as a **human bed-rail** so he doesn't roll off, while Ryan serves as Cassidy's emotional anchor against the overwhelming sensory and psychological toll of the world. Before playing the song in the pin-drop silence of The Crucible, Ryan explains that twenty-six years after the 1990 crash, the daily physical toll of those ADLs on the road has gotten too hard on his body and family—revealing that tonight's *Reignition* Tour finale at The Crucible is the **last concert a touring The Stardust Engine will ever play**. When the stadium gasps, Cassidy immediately reassures the crowd that they aren't retiring: to give the Town of Blacksburg and CPI time to plan, starting in **August 2017** (the first weekend of August), The Stardust Engine will stay home in Blacksburg to headline the annual **Forger Nation Summer Festival**—combining a campus-wide CPI Open House, a massive downtown Blacksburg block party for signed and unsigned bands, and The Stardust Engine's permanent annual residency concert + fireworks inside The Crucible.

### Lyrics

[Pin-Drop Stadium Silence]
[Soft Fingerpicked Acoustic Guitar Note]

[Spoken Word Intro]
[male]
This next song is the centerpiece of Our Own Frequency, and it's the most private song Cassidy and I have ever written. People look up at a stage and see two hours of lights and guitars. They don't see what T-ten paralysis actually takes behind closed doors. They don't see Cassidy and Evan and Tyler helping me through my daily care and toileting routines, or stretching my legs every morning so they don't atrophy. They don't see how we push the bed against the wall at night so I can sleep on the inside, and my sister sleeps on the outside edge as a human bed rail so I don't roll off in the dark.
[female]
And they don't see how you keep me anchored when the world gets too loud for my head to handle. We take care of each other. That's what family does.
[male]
Doing all of those daily routines on tour buses and in hotel rooms across the world was one thing when I was twenty-six in nineteen-ninety. Twenty-six years later... I'm gonna be honest with you, Forger Nation. Accommodating this chair on the road has gotten too hard on my body, and on my family. Tonight is the final stop of the Reignition tour, and tonight is the last concert a touring Stardust Engine will ever play.
[Audience Gasps Softly, Then Applauds Warmly in Support]
[female]
Wait—don't be sad! We aren't retiring! If touring the world is too hard on Ryan, then the world can come to Blacksburg! To give the Town of Blacksburg and CPI time to build this right, starting in August twenty-seventeen, we're launching the annual Forger Nation Summer Festival! A massive CPI Open House for incoming and prospective freshmen, the biggest downtown Blacksburg block party of the summer with stages for signed and unsigned bands, and every single year we'll close the festival right here inside The Crucible with fireworks—and sleep in our own beds on Progress Street every night!
[Deafening Cheers and Applause Before Hushing Back to Silence]
[male]
This is "Brother's Keeper."

[Intro]
(A delicate, melancholy grand piano intro)
(Acoustic guitar joins with a steady, comforting fingerpicked rhythm)

## Safe Person (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
**Act IV: *Our Own Frequency* — The Intimate Brother & Sister Acoustic Block (Part 3 of 3).** Completing the three-song *Our Own Frequency* (2008) living-room trilogy, **"Safe Person"** answers the physical devotion of *"Brother's Keeper"* by revealing the other half of their unbreakable bond: how Ryan acts as Cassidy's safe person and emotional anchor when the flashing cameras, press questions, and autistic sensory overload threaten to overwhelm her. Performed strictly by Ryan on acoustic guitar and Cassidy at the piano with zero electric amps or stadium lights, sixty-five thousand Forgers hold up quiet white phone lights across the dark bowl of The Crucible.

[Gentle, Solitary Piano Intro]
(Acoustic guitar softly joins the piano)

I smile and nod, playing the character

I'm terrifyingly alone on these high wire nights
Until I catch your eye across the room
And suddenly I'm safe inside this tomb

I know exactly who you want to be
When the oxygen gets thin up on the stage

Always.

## Still Your Safe Person (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
**Act V Finale: *Our Own Frequency* — The Intimate Brother & Sister Acoustic Block (Part 4 of 4 — Stripped Down to Acoustic Grand Piano & Acoustic Guitar).** Although originally recorded on 2015's *Reignition* as a monumental 78 BPM 1980s arena power ballad, for the May 21, 2016 homecoming concert inside **The Crucible**, **Ryan** and **Cassidy O'Connell** strip **"Still Your Safe Person"** all the way down to **just Cassidy's acoustic grand piano and Ryan's acoustic guitar**—performing it in the hushed, pin-drop quiet stadium directly after 2008's *"Safe Person"* to close **Act V: *Our Own Frequency***. Sitting hip-to-hip at the left edge of the grand-piano bench under two warm golden spotlights while 65,000 Forgers hold up quiet white lights across the stands, brother and sister bridge their 2008 living-room hiatus sanctuary directly into 2015—singing straight to every neurodivergent, overwhelmed, or healing student in the bleachers (*"When the static hits a hundred and the room begins to spin / I am still your safe person—let the quiet in"*).

### Lyrics

[Quiet Stadium Ambience]
[Spoken Intro - Cassidy O'Connell]
(Warm, gentle voice at the grand piano microphone)
[female]
Back in two thousand and eight, when we recorded "Safe Person" in our living room, we didn't know if we would ever stand on a stage again. Seven years later, when we went back into the studio for Reignition, we realized that no matter how much time passes, that promise never changes. So before Evan and Tyler come back out here with the big amplifiers... we wanted to play this one the way we wrote it at home. Just the piano, the acoustic guitar, and all of you.

[Intro]
(Warm, intimate unplugged acoustic grand piano chord progression at 78 BPM)
(Tender fingerpicked acoustic guitar counter-melody enters gently — strictly no drums or electric instruments)

[Verse 1]
[female]
When the fluorescent lights are humming way too loud
And you're spinning in the middle of a noisy crowd
When the rules don't match the script inside your head
And every single wire is flashing crimson red
[male]
You don't have to explain why your hands are shaking now
You don't have to force a smile or take a final bow
Just reach across the console, I'm right beside your chair
Breathing in the cadence of the quiet mountain air

[Pre-Chorus]
[female]
We've known each other since the nursery floor
[male]

[Duet]
No questions asked, no judgment in this room

I am still your safe person—let the quiet in!

I will hold the perimeter throughout the longest night!
You don't have to be unbroken just to be enough
I will be the anchor when the ocean gets too rough
Through every single storm and every place we've been
I am still your safe person—let the quiet in!

[Verse 2]

And I had to learn a brand new way to move down Progress Street
You never looked at me with pity in your eyes
You just lowered every keyboard stand to match my size
[female]

Take this song and keep it in your coat pocket deep

[Chorus]

I am still your safe person—let the quiet in!

## Echoes in the Wire (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
**Act V Opener: Full-Band Electric Return (Permanently Replacing *"If You Walked Away"*).** Following the three-song *Our Own Frequency* brother-and-sister acoustic block, Evan and Tyler walk back onto the stage and strap on their electric bass and drum kit. Because 1989's *"If You Walked Away"* is permanently banned from all post-1990 Stardust Engine concerts to protect Cassidy from autistic sensory and abandonment overload, **"Echoes in the Wire"** (from 1997's *Hard Reset*) holds the emotional power-ballad spotlight—beginning at Cassidy's grand piano before Evan, Tyler, and Ryan's full electric stadium rig roars back to life.

### Lyrics

[Live Stadium Ambience]
[Intro]
(A lone, echoing piano plays a simple, sad melody in the dark stadium)
(A warm, analog synth pad swells underneath)

[Verse 1]
[female]
I found an old cassette inside a box today
The tape was tangled, worn, and faded gray
I pressed "play" and heard the static hiss
A ghost of a melody I didn't know I'd miss
It's the sound of a basement in eighty-five
When the music was the only thing keeping us alive

[Chorus]
(A slow, heavy, gated-reverb drum beat kicks in as the full electric band returns)
(65,000 stadium lights sway in the dark)
There are echoes in the wire!
Shadows of a long-forgotten fire!
We're older now, the scars are deep
We've got promises we couldn't keep
Yeah, the signal's weak, the line is frayed
From all the mistakes that we have made
Just echoes... echoes in the wire.

[Verse 2]
[male]
I look across the console at my sister's face
We're survivors of a brutal, high-speed race
We lost the rhythm, yeah, we lost the beat
On a dark and frozen Midwest street
Now I play my part from a different seat
Trying to make this broken song complete

[Chorus]
(Full stadium power-ballad eruption with distorted electric guitar and soaring synths)
There are echoes in the wire!
Shadows of a long-forgotten fire!
We're older now, the scars are deep
We've got promises we couldn't keep
Yeah, the signal's weak, the line is frayed
From all the mistakes that we have made
Just echoes... echoes in the wire.

(A slow, weeping, deeply emotional electric guitar solo with heavy sustain and delay echoing off the stadium concrete)

[Bridge]
(The music drops back to just the piano and a soft bass pulse)
[female]
Can you hear me on the other end?
My brother, my anchor, my only friend?
[male]
The static's loud, the night is long
[female]
Let's help each other finish this song.

[Outro]
(The piano melody repeats, slowly joined by the warm synth pad)
[male]
Echoes in the wire...
[female]
Still burning...
(The synth pad swells to a warm, unresolved chord and fades into the roar of The Crucible)

## Saturday Night Lights: The Crucible (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
**THE LIVE CONCERT DEBUT.** Although the studio recording of **"Saturday Night Lights: The Crucible"** (from 2004's *Progress Street*) first blasted over the stadium PA between the 3rd and 4th quarters on October 8, 2004, the band vowed they would **ONLY ever perform the song live when playing inside The Crucible** (deliberately omitting it even from 2005's *Live in Norfolk*). As the four massive Ironstone floodlight towers blaze at 100% power to kick off the fourth-quarter sprint of the concert, The Stardust Engine unleashes the live concert debut of *"Saturday Night Lights: The Crucible"* to a 65,000-voice roar of *"GO FORGERS! GO CPI!"*

## Neon on the Huckleberry Trail (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
Performed live in **Act VI: *Homecoming Sprint*** right after *"Progress Street: Where the Fire Began"*, **"Neon on the Huckleberry Trail"** (a deep album cut from 2015's *Reignition*) produces one of the most unexpected, goosebump-inducing moments of the entire May 21, 2016 concert: **the night the audience decided to make the song their own**. To anyone in the stadium who isn't from the Blacksburg or Christiansburg area, a song about the "Huckleberry Trail" is a local mystery—but to **Forgers**, it cuts straight to the heart of home. Every CPI student and young alum knows the six-mile paved rail-trail winding from the **Pendleton Library** lawn past the pastures and Price Mountain down to Christiansburg, where they jog in their CPI hoodies and wave a quiet neighbor's hello as Cassidy rides her bicycle and Ryan pushes his manual wheelchair at dusk. And for the older alumni and lifelong New River Valley locals in the stands—some of whom actually rode the historic **Huckleberry Train** before its final run in 1958, or grew up on their parents' stories of the slow branch-line train between Cambria Depot and campus—hearing *"Six miles of asphalt from the library lawn / Where the old Huckleberry train used to roll on"* triggers an emotional wave of multi-generational nostalgia. By the first chorus, 65,000 Forgers have latched onto the song so fiercely that on the second and final choruses, a visibly moved Cassidy and Ryan step back from their microphones completely—letting three generations of CPI students, townies, and Old Guard alumni roar the chorus across the valley as they claim *"Neon on the Huckleberry Trail"* as their own hometown anthem.

### Lyrics

[Live Stadium Crowd Cheers]
[Spoken Intro - Ryan & Cassidy O'Connell]
(Bright, joyful voices over shimmering 12-string chorus electric guitar arpeggios)
[male]
When we're home in Blacksburg and the sun starts going down behind the mountains, there's one place the four of us always go—six miles of paved rail-trail between here and Christiansburg, with The Crucible tower lights glowing like neon in the evening sky!
[female]
Now, if you're visiting from out of town tonight, you might not know what the Huckleberry is all about—but if you're from Blacksburg or Christiansburg, or if you're a Forger who's walked that path from the library lawn—or if you're old-school enough to remember the actual Huckleberry Train—this one belongs to you!

[Intro]
(Shimmering 1980s chorused 12-string electric guitar arpeggios and warm polyphonic analog synthesizer bells at 124 BPM)
(Breezy eighth-note bassline and crisp gated-reverb stadium drums enter as the local crowd roars in instant recognition)

[Verse 1]
[female]
October five-thirty and the air turns crisp
Woodsmoke curling in a silver wisp
Maples turning crimson by the old rail bed
Canopy of burnt orange overhead
You're pushing on your hand-rims at an easy pace
With the mountain wind cooling off your face
Six miles of asphalt from the library lawn
Where the old Huckleberry train used to roll on
(Massive cheer of nostalgic recognition from the older alumni and local sections across the stands)

[Pre-Chorus]
[female]
No studio clocks and no city stress
Just the valley dressed in its autumn dress
Watch the sun dip low behind Price Mountain's crest
In the quiet corner that we love the best!

[Chorus]
(Uplifting 1980s melodic synth-rock stadium chorus as 65,000 Forgers spontaneously latch onto the melody and sing at the top of their lungs)
Chasing the neon on the Huckleberry Trail!
Where the twilight glows and the shadows sail!
Spinning our wheels through the golden leaves
Underneath a sky that still believes!
See the stadium towers flicker on in the night
Painting the valley in electric light!
Every autumn evening tells the same old tale
We're home together on the Huckleberry Trail!

[Verse 2]
[female]
Passing by the pastures where the cattle graze
Wrapped inside the amber of the college days
Students in their hoodies jogging two by two
Nodding with a smile as we roll on through
No autographs asked, just a neighbor's wave
In the mountain sanctuary that our hometown gave
Thirty years of music and we still belong
To the winding path where we wrote this song!

[Pre-Chorus]
[female]
No studio clocks and no city stress
Just the valley dressed in its autumn dress
Watch the stars come out above the mountain's crest
In the quiet corner that we love the best!

[Chorus - The Audience Takeover]
(Cassidy and Ryan step back from their microphones in stunned, smiling awe as 65,000 Forgers—from 19-year-old students to older alumni who remember the original Huckleberry Train—take over the song and roar every word across the valley)
[female]
(Spoken, overwhelmed) Take it, Forger Nation! It's yours!
(65,000-Voice Stadium Choir Carrying the Chorus)
Chasing the neon on the Huckleberry Trail!
Where the twilight glows and the shadows sail!
Spinning our wheels through the golden leaves
Underneath a sky that still believes!

Painting the valley in electric light!
Every autumn evening tells the same old tale
We're home together on the Huckleberry Trail!

[Bridge]

We've seen Tokyo towers and the London rain
Paris boulevards and the coastal train
[male]
But nothing on earth ever feels as sweet
As the six-mile path leading back to Progress Street!

[Final Chorus]

## Last Bus Home: Lights Out on Progress Street (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
**THE FALSE ENDING.** To close what the audience believes is the end of the concert (and the final stop of their last world tour before beginning their annual CPI Summer Festival residency), the band performs the grand stadium power-ballad finale of 2004's *Progress Street*. As the drums and guitars slowly fade to a lone upright piano chord, Cassidy whispers *"Lights out on Progress Street..."*, Ryan says *"Goodnight, Forger Nation,"* and every stage light and Jumbotron screen inside The Crucible cuts to pitch black—convincing 65,000 fans that the concert is over.

(Gentle, cinematic grand piano chords layered with a warm 1980s analog synthesizer pad)
(A melodic, crying electric guitar lead plays a nostalgic theme)

The road cases are latched and rolled outside the stage door
[female]

Now the highway's calling us to finally take our rest
[male]
Our custom low-floor shuttle bus is kneeling at the curb
[female]
Leading to a quiet place the static can't disturb.

Aluminum ramp unfolds beneath the amber sign
[female]
Five of us together at the end of the line!

(Massive 80s gated-reverb drums and soaring stadium guitars swell in)
We're taking the last bus home! Back to Progress Street!
Turning off the amplifiers and the stadium heat!
Lock the front door tight and let the curtains fall
We don't owe the outside world anything at all!
Through every storm we weathered and every song we played
This family is the greatest music that we ever made!

[male]

[female]

[male]

[female]
We're not fading into nothing, we're just tuning out the noise
[male]

(Ryan: "Goodnight, Forger Nation. We love you.")
(Every stage light, amplifier indicator, and Jumbotron screen cuts to total darkness as 65,000 fans believe the concert has ended)

## Electric Color (Live at the Crucible)
### Lore
The secret 5-minute-26-second Grand Finale Encore of the May 21, 2016 homecoming concert inside **The Crucible**—and the very first time in the band's 30-year history that **"Electric Color"** was ever performed as the blistering **1980s Album-Oriented Rock (Stadium Rock)** anthem Ryan, Cassidy, Evan, and Tyler originally heard in their heads before Apex Records forced them to release it as a glossy 1987 Dance Synth-Pop single.

Protected prior to concert night by Holly O'Connell's **$300,000 collective student escrow ("The Forger Pot")** under Addendum No. 4 to her 1986 Cross-License—and disguised in the **CPI Marching Forgers**' rehearsal folders as *"Exercise 87-B: The Anvil Finale"* with the vocal melody stripped out—the band first staged **"Last Bus Home: Lights Out on Progress Street"** as a false ending, plunging the stadium into darkness so the audience believed the concert was over. Then, in the pitch black, all 300 members of the Marching Forgers marched back onto the stage to join the band (just as they had in the four-song opening act) for the actual ending. For the first **1 minute and 40 seconds**—through Cassidy's spoken greeting over a lone piano chord (*"Blacksburg! We've been all over the world..."*), the Kevlar snare roll-off, and a massive 77-second AOR stadium guitar and 300-horn brass overture (`0:23–1:40`)—not a single fan in the 65,000-seat stadium knew what song the band was playing, until **`1:40`** on the dot when the instruments dropped to a lone heavy kick drum and Cassidy sang the opening words of Verse 1: *"Woke up this morning, the world was gray..."*

### Lyrics

[Live Stadium Crowd Roar]
[Single Echoing Piano Chord]

[Spoken Word Intro]
Blacksburg! We've been all over the world... but there is no better feeling...
Than ending this entire tour... right here at home, in the Crucible!
Are you with us one last time Forger Nation?
Let's go!

[Extended Instrumental Vamp]
[Powerful Synthesizer Entry]
[False Start]
[Confusing Disjointed Instrumentation]
[Tension Building]

[Music Cuts]
[Isolated Heavy Kick Drum]
[Audience Cheering]

[Verse 1 - Vocals driven by Kick Drum only]
Woke up this morning, the world was gray
Didn't know the colors had all faded away
Been living in a room with the curtains drawn tight
Then you flipped a switch and turned on the light

[Pre-Chorus - Instruments Cut Out completely]
[Vocals isolate against Crowd Noise]
The city sounds were just a buzz in my ear
A static station I could never quite hear
But now the noise is turning into a song
And I finally feel like I belong

[Chorus - Full 80s Arena Rock Band slams in]
Oh, I'm seeing in electric color
Finding a rhythm I never knew
Dancing in a brand new summer
My whole world feels bright and new
It's an electric-colored feeling
And it all started with you!

[Bridge - Heavy Bass Groove]

I can't hide it any longer
My heart is playing catch-up
To the music turning way up!

[Breakdown - Music Cuts completely]

That's the sound of a new world.
No more gray.
Just... color.

Finding a rhythm I never knew

My whole world feels bright and new
It's an electric-colored feeling
And it all started with you!

Just you! Let me hear you sing it!
(Crowd Vocals Only)
Oh-oh-oh! Electric Color!
(Spoken)
I can't hear you! For the end of the tour!
(Crowd Vocals Only)
Oh-oh-oh! Electric Color!

[The Crucible Finale - Full Rock Band fuses with Entire Collegiate Marching Band]

COME ON!

[Massive Brass Section Swell and Drumline Explosion]

# The Forger's Call (2016) [CompilationAlbum]
* **Narrative Era:** 2016
* **Real-World DSP Release:** 2026-10-26

## Ignition: The Forger's Call
### Lore
The official **October 1986 Crucible Stadium Master** played over the PA system at **The Crucible** during every CPI Forgers home football game as the football team runs out of the tunnel onto the field—released on an album for the very first time on October 15, 2016 alongside *Live at the Crucible* (both inside the *Live at the Crucible* 30th-Anniversary Box Set at the CPI Campus Bookstore and as a standalone companion compilation). After `Demo Take 3` (Track 16) won the October 1986 CPI Student Government Association anthem contest—and after 16-year-old dual-enrolled **Holly O'Connell** executed the **`$1.00` Perpetual Mutual Cross-License** ("The Progress Street Redline") with CPI's Office of University Counsel—Ryan, Cassidy, Evan, and Tyler tracked this finished **`82 BPM`** stadium master specifically for the press-box audio booth at The Crucible. Replacing the cassette demo's vocalized `(TING... BOOM!)` placeholders with an actual steel foundry anvil strike and Tyler Wright's chest-rattling kick-drum impact, this is the exact recording to which the student section in **The Anvil** organically crafted **The Stomp** on the heavy kick-drum hits over several successive plays.

## This Side of Heaven (1988 Blacksburg Campus Cut)
### Lore
Recorded in **September 1988** in Blacksburg between *Electric Color* (1987) and *Neon Hearts* (1989), ***"This Side of Heaven"*** (`1988 Blacksburg Campus Cut`) was written by Ryan and Cassidy O'Connell during autumn move-in week at **Commonwealth Polytechnic Institute (CPI)**. Watching hundreds of eighteen-year-old freshmen carrying cardboard boxes up the steps of **Bennett Hall** and **Kincaid Hall** into the crisp Blue Ridge sunlight—many of them escaping difficult or suffocating childhood homes for the very first time—Ryan and Cassidy crafted an unapologetically joyful, sunlit **`126 BPM`** late-1980s synth-rock and stadium-guitar anthem about stepping out of the shadows, leaving the dark behind forever, and waking up safe in a brand-new world surrounded by chosen family.

Because the band refused to let **Apex Records** twist a deeply personal song about Blacksburg sanctuary into a disposable corporate single, **Holly O'Connell** kept the master tape out of the label's hands under her 1986 CPI cross-license and distributed it on campus cassettes through the **CPI Campus Bookstore** and student radio. For nearly three decades, *"This Side of Heaven"* has served as the unofficial move-in anthem of CPI—and in **January 2015** (*The Eleventh Child: The Story of Jordan*, Episode 3, Chapter 1), hearing this song while unpacking his maroon-and-orange CPI shirts in **Bennett Hall Suite 214/216** sends **Jordan Fuller** into an overwhelming meltdown of pure joy (`Emotional Overflow: 100`) as it hits him all at once that his mother's house is in the past, he is **SAFE**, and being back at CPI with the people who genuinely love him truly is *This Side of Heaven*.

### Lyrics

[Tempo: 126 BPM - Sunlit, Driving 1988 Melodic Synth-Rock & Stadium Guitar Anthem]
[Intro: Shimmering Sunlit Cascade of 1988 Digital & Analog Polyphonic Synthesizer Chords]
(Bright, crystalline late-1980s synthesizer arpeggios opening like morning sunlight over the Blue Ridge Mountains, joined by a soaring, exuberant electric guitar hook and Tyler Wright's punchy gated snare fill)

## Kerosene & 4-Track Tape (1986 Garage Outtake)
### Lore
Recorded in **late October 1986** inside the unheated two-car garage on **Progress Street** in Blacksburg, Virginia, ***"Kerosene & 4-Track Tape"*** (`1986 Garage Outtake`) was tracked on the B-side of the exact same Maxell XLII-S 90 cassette tape that held `Demo Take 3` of *"Ignition: The Forger's Call"*. With October mountain temperatures dropping into the thirties outside and only a portable cylindrical kerosene heater glowing orange in the corner of the concrete garage, 22-year-old **Ryan O'Connell**, 20-year-old **Cassidy O'Connell**, and 19-year-old identical twins **Evan and Tyler Wright** tore through this raw, high-energy **`138 BPM`** garage rocker to warm up their fingers while 16-year-old **Holly O'Connell** rode the faders on their four-track Tascam Portastudio.

Never submitted to **Apex Records**—who would have balked at the unvarnished tube-amp grit and tape saturation—this 1986 time capsule captures the unfiltered joy of five broke college kids building a life and a sound together on Progress Street before the industry ever knew their names.

### Lyrics

[Intro: Mechanical Cassette Click, Hum of a Garage Kerosene Heater, and Overdriven Tube Guitar Riff]
(Raw 1986 dual-stack tube-amp guitar chord ringing out in a concrete two-car garage, joined by Tyler Wright's four-on-the-snare count-in and Cassidy's gritty analog polyphonic organ-synth stab)

## The Anvil: 126.2 Decibels (1989 Seismograph Instrumental)
### Lore
Recorded in **November 1989** for the press-box audio booth at **The Crucible**, ***"The Anvil: 126.2 Decibels"*** (`1989 Seismograph Instrumental`) commemorates the legendary November 1989 goal-line stand when the North End Zone student section (**The Anvil**) stomped the galvanized steel bleachers in unison to Tyler Wright's kick drum at a verified peak of **`126.2 dBA`**—registering a localized micro-earthquake on the Department of Geosciences seismograph needle inside **Copernicus Hall** half a mile away.

Built around an earth-shaking **`84 BPM`** stomping kick-drum and foundry-anvil groove, Evan Wright's sub-harmonic analog bass synth pedals, Cassidy O'Connell's roaring analog polyphonic brass fanfares, and Ryan O'Connell's pinched-harmonic stadium guitar riffs, this instrumental cue has been blasted over The Crucible PA system before every crucial 3rd-and-short and 4th-and-goal defensive stand since 1989.

### Lyrics

[Instrumental]

[Tempo: 84 BPM - Heavy, Seismic Stadium Stomp & Foundry Brass Instrumental]

[Intro: Seismograph Needle Alarm Pulse, Metallic Foundry Anvil Strike & Crushing 84 BPM Kick-Drum Stomp]
(High-pitched metallic steel anvil strike answered by Tyler Wright's massive, chest-rattling 84 BPM kick drum and Evan Wright's sub-bass analog synth pedal drone)

[Main Riff: Heavy Overdriven 1989 Stadium Guitar & analog polyphonic Analog Brass Fanfare]
(Ryan O'Connell's palm-muted, swaggering E-Minor stadium rock guitar riff locks in with Cassidy O'Connell's towering analog polyphonic brass chords)

[Section A: The North End Zone Bleacher Cadence]
(Rhythmic, synchronized stomping percussion and pounding floor toms driving a relentless 84 BPM defensive-stand groove)

[Guitar & Synthesizer Duel: 126.2 Decibels]
(Soaring, high-gain 1989 electric guitar lead trading aggressive, melodic phrases with screaming analog synthesizer brass and rhythmic anvil chimes)

[Breakdown: The Copernicus Hall Tremor]
(Drums and sub-bass strip down to a seismic half-time anvil-and-kick-drum stomp, building tension octave by octave as analog synth risers climb toward the redline)

[Climax: Full Stadium Roar & Dual-Lead Finale]
(Full band explodes back in at maximum intensity—harmonized electric guitar and analog polyphonic synth leads soaring over thunderous double-kick drums and ringing steel anvil strikes)

[Outro: Final Anvil Strike & Sustaining Sub-Bass Tremor]
(One final deafening steel anvil chime and sustained E-minor power chord echoing into silence)


## Snowfall on Commonwealth Green (1990 Winter Acoustic Session)
### Lore
Recorded in **January 1990** in the living room on **Progress Street** during a quiet winter break in Blacksburg—just eleven months before the December 10, 1990 I-81 tour bus crash—***"Snowfall on Commonwealth Green"*** (`1990 Winter Acoustic Session`) captures The Stardust Engine at their warmest and most reflective. Escaping the relentless promotional grind of *Neon Hearts* for two weeks in the Blue Ridge Mountains, Ryan and Cassidy wrote this **`92 BPM`** acoustic-and-synth winter ballad while watching a heavy Appalachian snowstorm blanket the vast central oval of **Commonwealth Green** (`The Green`), turning the gray-and-pink **Ironstone** battlements of **Preston Hall**, **Pendleton Library**, and **Keystone Hall** into a silent cathedral of white.

Featuring Ryan on a shimmering 12-string acoustic guitar, Cassidy on grand piano and warm analog chorus synthesizer pads, Evan on fretless bass, and Tyler on brushed snare and sleigh-bell tambourine, the track was preserved in Holly O'Connell's Blacksburg vault for twenty-six years before its official release on *The Forger's Call*.

### Lyrics

[Tempo: 92 BPM - Warm 1990 12-String Acoustic Guitar, Grand Piano & Analog Synth Winter Ballad]
[Intro: Shimmering 12-String Acoustic Guitar Arpeggios, Warm Grand Piano & Soft Analog Chorus Synthesizer Pad]
(Gentle, crystalline 12-string acoustic guitar picking and warm grand piano chords evoking quiet snowflakes falling across a mountain campus at twilight)

[Verse 1]
[female]
Six o'clock hush on a January night
Amber streetlamps haloed in white
Across the wide oval where the pathways meet
The snow is erasing every track on the street
[male]
Preston Hall rises through the silver veil
Ironstone towers in a winter's tale

## Under the Keystone Bridge (1991 Blacksburg B-Side)
### Lore
Recorded in **November 1991** at the Progress Street house in Blacksburg, ***"Under the Keystone Bridge"*** (`1991 Blacksburg B-Side`) was the very first song **Ryan O'Connell** wrote and recorded after returning home to Blacksburg as a T10 paraplegic manual wheelchair user following the December 10, 1990 I-81 tour bus crash and his long months of inpatient spinal cord rehabilitation.

For weeks after coming home, Ryan was hesitant to roll across the hilly CPI campus where he had once walked and run as an undergraduate. Then, on a crisp November afternoon, **Cassidy**, **Holly**, **Evan**, and **Tyler** walked beside his custom manual wheelchair up **Commonwealth Mall** and paused beneath the soaring gothic **Ironstone** arches of **The Keystone Bridge**—the iconic campus skybridge spanning the mall between **Commonwealth Green** and the main gate. Looking out through the stone arches at the campus that still welcomed him home without pity or hesitation, Ryan went back to Progress Street that night and tracked this driving **`124 BPM`** heartland synth-rock anthem of resilience, dignity, and belonging.

### Lyrics

[Tempo: 124 BPM - Driving 1991 Heartland Synth-Rock & Melodic Arena Guitar]
[Intro: Crisp Arpeggiated 12-String Electric Guitar, Warm Analog Synth Pad & Driving Drum Beat]
(Bright, propulsive 1991 heartland rock guitar riff locked in with Cassidy's warm analog polyphonic synth chords and Tyler's steady, determined backbeat)

[Verse 1]
[male]
Ten months of fluorescent hospital white
Learning a brand-new way to fight
Two aluminum wheels on the concrete floor
Wondering who I am outside that door
[female]
We parked at the bottom of Commonwealth Mall
Where the autumn oaks grow gold and tall
You gripped the push-rims with your calloused hands
And rolled right back into the promised land!

[Pre-Chorus]
[male]
I thought the hills would lock me out
I had a thousand pounds of doubt
[duet]
Until we reached the top of the rise
And saw the stone arch against the skies!

[Chorus]
[duet]
Under the Keystone Bridge, the wind blows free!
Every stone in the arch still remembers me!
From Commonwealth Mall to the open Green
We're the strongest family this town has seen!
[male]
I don't need to stand to be ten feet tall!
[female]
We're rolling together through it all!
[duet]
Home again under the Keystone Bridge!

[Verse 2]
[male]
Gothic Ironstone spanning the road
Bearing the weight of the heaviest load
Students passing with a nod and a smile
Nobody staring, nobody on trial
[female]
Evan and Tyler on your left and right
Holly ahead in the afternoon light
And I've got my hand on the back of your frame
Different wheels, brother—still the same flame!

[Pre-Chorus]
[male]
I thought the hills would lock me out
I had a thousand pounds of doubt
[duet]

And saw the stone arch against the skies!

[Chorus]
[duet]
Under the Keystone Bridge, the wind blows free!
Every stone in the arch still remembers me!
From Commonwealth Mall to the open Green
We're the strongest family this town has seen!
[male]
I don't need to stand to be ten feet tall!
[female]
We're rolling together through it all!
[duet]
Home again under the Keystone Bridge!

[Guitar Solo]
(Emotional, soaring 1991 electric guitar solo played by Ryan O'Connell from his wheelchair in the Progress Street studio—climbing from warm mid-range bends into a triumphant, sky-piercing stadium melody backed by Cassidy's shimmering synth brass)

[female]

Pressed from both sides when the weather grows cold!
[male]

[Final Chorus]
[duet]

We're the strongest family this town has seen!

We're rolling together through it all!
[duet]
Home again under the Keystone Bridge!

## One Dollar on the Kitchen Table (1992 Founding Jam)
### Lore
Recorded in **July 1992** in the living room on **Progress Street** on the exact evening 22-year-old **Holly O'Connell** filed the articles of incorporation for ***Engine Room Records, LLC*** in preparation for the band's impending war of independence against Apex Records, ***"One Dollar on the Kitchen Table"*** (`1992 Founding Jam`) is Ryan, Cassidy, Evan, and Tyler's joyous **`132 BPM`** melodic arena-rock tribute to their youngest sister.

Six years earlier, in October 1986, sixteen-year-old dual-enrolled CPI freshman Holly O'Connell had sat at the Progress Street kitchen table with a red ballpoint pen, crossed out the university's boilerplate *"Work Made for Hire"* clause on the student stadium anthem contest entry form, and negotiated the historic **`$1.00` Perpetual Mutual Cross-License** ("The Progress Street Redline") with CPI's Office of University Counsel. That crisp one-dollar bill—still framed beside the kitchen table—kept 100% of *"Ignition: The Forger's Call"* out of Apex Records' catalog and became the legal cornerstone upon which Engine Room Records was built.

### Lyrics

[Tempo: 132 BPM - Upbeat, Joyous 1992 Melodic Arena Rock & Synth-Rock Founding Anthem]
[Intro: Punchy Arena Rock Guitar Riff, Bright analog polyphonic Brass Hook & Driving 132 BPM Drum Beat]
(Energetic, triumphant 1980s-style melodic rock riff with Ryan's crunching rhythm guitar, Cassidy's celebratory synth brass fanfare, and a four-person gang shout)

[Verse 1]
[male]
October nineteen eighty-six, a Tuesday after class
Four kids in a cold garage trying to make the music last
The student contest form came down in twenty lines of gray:
"Sign your name on the dotted line and give your song away!"
[female]
Then sixteen-year-old Holly walked in through the kitchen door
Dropped her calculus textbook down and read the boilerplate score
She uncapped a red ballpoint pen beneath the ceiling light
And drew a crimson battle line across the black and white!

[Pre-Chorus]
[male]
She marched up into Preston Hall at nine AM the next day!
[female]
Looked the university counsel in the eye and had her say!
[duet]
"The Forgers get the stadium roar for every autumn game—
And my family keeps the master tape and everything we claim!"

[Chorus]
[duet]

That's how Holly built our label!
A sixteen-year-old kid with a red-ink pen
Protected our future before it even began!
[male]
George Washington smiling in a wooden frame!
[female]
No corporate suit can ever buy our name!
[duet]
Yeah, it all started with one dollar on the kitchen table!

[Verse 2]

Looking for the Crucible tape to see what they could do
Holly pulled the redline contract out of the fireproof safe
And watched the lawyers pack their bags and vanish without a trace!

To the newest company in town that's built to stand and last
Engine Room is open and the masters are our own—
Because our little sister stood like Blacksburg Ironstone!

"The Forgers get the stadium roar for every autumn game—

[Chorus]

[male]

No corporate suit can ever buy our name!
[duet]
Yeah, it all started with one dollar on the kitchen table!

[Guitar & Synth Solo]
(Exuberant, high-flying 1992 melodic arena rock guitar solo by Ryan O'Connell trading triumphant harmonies with Cassidy's bright analog synthesizer lead while Evan and Tyler lock into a joyous double-time groove)

## Copernicus Hall: Roof of the World (1994 Observatory Instrumental)
### Lore
Recorded in **April 1994** immediately after **Holly O'Connell** won the landmark legal settlement emancipating 100% of The Stardust Engine's master tapes from Apex Records, ***"Copernicus Hall: Roof of the World"*** (`1994 Observatory Instrumental`) documents the night the five family members returned to the CPI campus to celebrate their freedom under the stars.

Climbing up to the rooftop astronomy observation deck of **Copernicus Hall**—home to CPI's Department of Physics, Astrophysics, and Geosciences on the north rim of **Commonwealth Green**—Cassidy and Ryan looked up at the clear Appalachian night sky where they had first dreamed up the band's name in 1986. Returning to the studio at dawn, Cassidy (on sweeping analog analog polyphonic, Minimoog, and zero-gravity arpeggiators) and Ryan (on soaring, delay-drenched electric guitar) tracked this **`108 BPM`** cosmic space-rock instrumental—serving as the direct sonic bridge between 1986's *"The Stardust Engine"* and Cassidy's 1996 magnum opus, *"Escape Velocity (Ad Astra)"*.

### Lyrics

[Instrumental]

[Tempo: 108 BPM - Soaring 1994 Cosmic Synthesizer & Melodic Electric Guitar Space-Rock Instrumental]

[Intro: Zero-Gravity Analog Synthesizer Arpeggios & Night-Sky Pad]
(Crystalline eight-voice analog polyphonic synthesizer arpeggiators sparkling like constellations above the Blue Ridge Mountains, underpinned by Evan Wright's deep sub-bass synthesizer pedal sustain)

[Theme A: The Copernicus Hall Observatory Dome Opens]
(Cassidy O'Connell introduces a majestic, wide-interval analog brass and poly-synth melody at 108 BPM as Tyler Wright's crisp, reverberant acoustic drums enter on a steady orbital pulse)

[Theme B: Ryan's Guitar Ascent]
(Ryan O'Connell's singing, sustain-rich electric guitar enters in harmony with Cassidy's lead synthesizer, soaring over Commonwealth Green toward the stars)

[Bridge: Perigee to Apogee]
(Rhythm section drops into a weightless, shimmering half-time suspension—fretless bass harmonics and cascading piano-and-synth bells echoing through deep space)

[Dual Solo: Stardust & Engine Reclaimed]
(Triumphant, emotionally charged twin-lead dialogue between Ryan's weeping melodic guitar bends and Cassidy's blazing Minimoog lead, celebrating total artistic freedom after the 1994 emancipation)

[Finale: Roof of the World]
(Full band locks into the main observatory theme a final octave higher—towering analog synth brass, soaring guitar sustain, and rolling timpani-and-tom fills fading into starlight)


## Foundry Maroon & Molten Orange (1996 Homecoming Single)
### Lore
Recorded in **October 1996** to celebrate the **10th Anniversary** of *"Ignition: The Forger's Call"* debuting inside **The Crucible**, ***"Foundry Maroon & Molten Orange"*** (`1996 Homecoming Single`) was pressed by Engine Room Records as a limited-edition Homecoming cassette distributed free to CPI students outside **The Foundry** (CPI's bustling two-story campus dining hall rotunda) and **Kincaid Hall**.

Written at **`130 BPM`** in the band's signature 1980s AOR stadium-rock style, this high-voltage vocal duet between Ryan and Cassidy celebrates CPI's official collegiate colors—**Foundry Maroon** (`#861F41`, representing the structural iron and seasoned brick of the forge) and **Molten Orange** (`#E5751F`, representing liquid steel pouring from the crucible at $2,800^\circ\text{F}$)—and the lifelong bond of coming home to Blacksburg every October.

### Lyrics

[Tempo: 130 BPM - High-Energy 1980s-Style Stadium Rock & Melodic AOR Duet]
[Intro: Blazing Twin-Guitar & Analog Synth Brass Fanfare over Driving 130 BPM Stadium Drums]
(Triumphant, sunlit stadium rock riff with Cassidy's punchy analog polyphonic brass chords, Ryan's soaring electric guitar hook, and Tyler's booming arena drums)

## The Old Lady on the Hill (1998 Bennett Hall Acoustic Cut)
### Lore
Recorded in **September 1998** in Blacksburg during a brief rest between legs of the *Hard Reset* World Tour, ***"The Old Lady on the Hill"*** (`1998 Bennett Hall Acoustic Cut`) is a warm **`112 BPM`** heartland acoustic-rock tribute to **Bennett Hall**—Commonwealth Polytechnic Institute's oldest surviving red-brick academic building (constructed in **1882** on the highest knoll overlooking the upper quad) and affectionately known to every generation of CPI students and alumni as ***"The Old Lady on the Hill"***.

Before the campus expanded into the gothic **Ironstone** quadrangles of **Commonwealth Green**, Bennett Hall housed CPI's original classrooms, laboratories, and dormitories. It was inside Bennett Hall's creaking heart-pine lecture rooms that Ryan and Cassidy O'Connell took their freshman humanities seminars in 1985—and twenty-nine years later, in 2014–2015, its renovated lower residential wing (`Bennett Hall Suite 214/216`) would become the beloved freshman sanctuary of **Jordan Fuller**, **Aubrie Jean Sinclair**, **Mark**, **Madison**, **Zoë**, **Lauren**, **Hannah**, **Sarah**, **Jessica**, and **RA Isabel**.

### Lyrics

[Tempo: 112 BPM - Warm 1998 Heartland Acoustic Rock with 12-String Guitar, Tonewheel Organ & Sibling Harmonies]
[Intro: Strumming Acoustic 6-String & 12-String Guitars, Warm Tonewheel Organ Swell & Gentle Rimshot Beat]
(Warm, nostalgic acoustic guitar chord progression joined by a soulful tonewheel organ melody and Evan Wright's woody acoustic bass)

## Cadence in The Furnace (2000 Drumline & Guitar Studio Cut)
### Lore
Recorded in **February 2000** for CPI Athletics to blast inside **The Furnace**—Commonwealth Polytechnic Institute's 10,000-seat parabolic-arch basketball and convocation coliseum located right across the plaza from **The Crucible**—***"Cadence in The Furnace"*** (`2000 Drumline & Guitar Studio Cut`) is a high-voltage **`120 BPM`** percussion and electric-guitar instrumental showcase led by **Tyler Wright** and **Evan Wright**.

Because **The Furnace** was engineered with a steep, windowless poured-concrete parabolic ceiling that reflects crowd and percussion transients directly back onto the hardwood court, Tyler invited eight student snare and tenors players from **The Engine Room** (the championship drumline of the **CPI Marching Forgers**) into the studio to trade blistering Kevlar marching-snare cadences with his acoustic drum kit, Evan's syncopated slap-and-fingerstyle bass, Ryan's razor-sharp rhythm/lead guitar, and Cassidy's clavinet-and-brass synth stabs.

### Lyrics

[Instrumental]

[Tempo: 120 BPM - High-Voltage Collegiate Drumline, Slap Bass & Arena Rock Guitar Instrumental]

[Intro: Solo Kevlar Marching Snare Rimshot Cadence in a Concrete Arena]
(Crisp, rapid-fire collegiate Kevlar marching snare rudiments and multi-tenor drum rolls echoing beneath a parabolic concrete dome, answered by Tyler Wright's booming acoustic drum kit)

[Main Groove: 120 BPM Drumline & Twin-Cousin Rhythm Lock]
(Evan Wright's punchy, syncopated bassline locks into Tyler's thunderous drumkit-and-drumline cadence as Ryan O'Connell unleashes a tight, rhythmic arena-rock guitar riff and Cassidy punches in with funky analog clavinet and brass stabs)

[Section A: Full-Court Press]
(Driving 120 BPM arena rock theme with soaring electric guitar melody and synchronized drumline rimshots on the backbeat)

[Percussion Duel: Tyler Wright & "The Engine Room" Drumline]
(All guitars drop out for a 16-bar percussion showdown—Kevlar marching snares, quad tenors, and tonal bass drums trading lightning-fast herta and paradiddle grooves with Tyler's studio drum kit)

[Guitar & Synth Solo: Inside The Furnace]
(Ryan's screaming, tapped electric guitar solo and Cassidy's blazing analog synth lead trade bars over the relentless marching percussion battery)

[Finale: Buzzer-Beater Climax]
(Full band and drumline unite at maximum volume on the main riff, ending on a blazing drumline roll and a massive synchronized final hit)


## Ironstone & Starlight (2004 Progress Street Outtake)
### Lore
Recorded in **August 2004** at the **Progress Street** house studio in Blacksburg during the sessions for *Progress Street* (2004), ***"Ironstone & Starlight"*** (`2004 Progress Street Outtake`) was mixed and mastered alongside the rest of the album, but held back in the vault only because the band felt *Progress Street* needed to end directly at Track 13 with *"Last Bus Home: Lights Out on Progress Street"*.

Written at a majestic **`118 BPM`** in the band's classic 1980s AOR and melodic synth-rock style, the duet brings together the two halves of The Stardust Engine's identity: the terrestrial permanence of **Ironstone**—the rough-hewn gray-and-pink dolomitic limestone quarried from the campus quarry to build **Preston Hall**, **Pendleton Library**, **The Keystone Bridge**, and **The Crucible**—and the weightless **Starlight** of the cosmos above the New River Valley.

### Lyrics

[Tempo: 118 BPM - Soaring 2004 Melodic 1980s-Style AOR & Cosmic Synth-Rock Duet]
[Intro: Shimmering Analog Synth Arpeggios, Warm Electric Guitar Lead & Big Gated Snare]
(Lush, wide-screen 1980s AOR synthesizer pads and crystalline arpeggios joined by Ryan's singing sustain guitar melody and Tyler's booming mid-tempo arena drum beat)

## Walk-On: Tunnel to the Crucible (2006 Athletics Vault Cut)
### Lore
Recorded in **August 2006** in the Progress Street studio—two years into the band's ten-year Burnout Hiatus (2004–2014)—***"Walk-On: Tunnel to the Crucible"*** (`2006 Athletics Vault Cut`) was recorded quietly by Ryan, Cassidy, Evan, and Tyler as a 20th-Anniversary gift to the **CPI Athletic Department** and the football coaching staff.

Never released commercially during the hiatus—and played exclusively inside the CPI team locker room and over the tunnel speakers thirty minutes before kickoff—this blistering **`142 BPM`** hard-rock charger honors the long tradition of unrecruited, zero-star CPI walk-on players who came to Blacksburg with no scholarship and no hype, earned their jersey through sheer polytechnic grit, and charged down the concrete tunnel into the roar of 65,000 fans inside **The Crucible**.

## Living Room Reignition (2012 Progress Street Tape)
### Lore
Captured live on a single stereo pair of condenser microphones in the **Progress Street** living room in **November 2012**—eight years into the band's ten-year Burnout Hiatus—***"Living Room Reignition"*** (`2012 Progress Street Tape`) documents the exact night **Ryan O'Connell**, **Cassidy O'Connell**, **Evan Wright**, and **Tyler Wright** plugged in all four electric instruments together as a full rock band for the first time since 2005's *Live in Norfolk*.

After Thanksgiving dinner at the Progress Street house, Tyler sat down behind the living-room drum kit, Evan picked up his four-string bass, Cassidy switched on her vintage analog polyphonic, and Ryan rolled his wheelchair over to his tube amplifier and hit an open A-major chord. What followed was this spontaneous, joyful **`116 BPM`** rehearsal tape—the exact creative spark that led to the writing of 2015's *Reignition* and their May 21, 2016 homecoming inside **The Crucible**.

### Lyrics

[Tempo: 116 BPM - Warm, Raw, Joyful 2012 Four-Piece Living-Room Rock Rehearsal Tape]
[Intro: Living-Room Ambience, Guitar Amp Switch Click, Warm Tube Hum & Spontaneous Drum Count-In]
(Warm living-room acoustics: click of a guitar cable plugging into a tube amp, Cassidy laughing softly as her analog polyphonic synth warms up, and Tyler counting "One, two, three, four!" into a punchy 116 BPM four-piece rock groove)

## Ad Astra, Per Aspera: Hymn for the Crucible (2016 Brass & Band Studio Master)
### Lore
Recorded in **May 2016** inside **Callahan Hall** (CPI's School of Performing Arts concert hall and home of the **CPI Marching Forgers**) during the week leading up to the May 21, 2016 *Live at the Crucible* concert, ***"Ad Astra, Per Aspera: Hymn for the Crucible"*** (`2016 Brass & Band Studio Master`) is a majestic **`78 BPM`** rock-and-brass alma mater benediction written by Cassidy and Ryan O'Connell to honor the 30th Anniversary of their 1986 CPI origins.

Translating the Latin motto (`"Through Hardship to the Stars"`) into a sweeping cathedral of sound combining Cassidy's grand piano and analog synthesizers, Ryan's weeping sustain electric guitar, Evan and Tyler's stately rhythm section, and the 60-horn symphonic brass choir of the **CPI Marching Forgers**, Track 15 brings the thirty-year arc of *The Forger's Call* to a deeply moving close—right before Track 16 (`"Ignition: The Forger's Call [Demo Take 3]"`) rewinds the tape thirty years back to the October 1986 Progress Street garage where the five of them started it all.

[Intro: Warm Concert Grand Piano & Noble 60-Piece Collegiate Brass Chorale in Callahan Hall]

## Ignition: The Forger's Call (Demo Take 3)
### Lore
The legendary **October 1986 4-track Tascam Portastudio cassette demo (`Demo Take 3`)** recorded inside the unheated garage on Progress Street in Blacksburg, Virginia—submitted by five college kids (Ryan O'Connell, Cassidy O'Connell, Evan Wright, Tyler Wright, and 16-year-old dual-enrolled Holly O'Connell) to the CPI Student Government Association contest to replace the football team's entrance march. Before submitting the Maxell XLII-S 90 cassette tape, 16-year-old Holly redlined the university's standard *"Work Made for Hire"* entry form at the Progress Street kitchen table and negotiated the historic **`$1.00` Perpetual Mutual Cross-License** ("The Progress Street Redline"), retaining 100% of the band's master and publishing ownership while granting CPI Athletics and the Marching Forgers a perpetual, royalty-free stadium license.

Recorded before the student section in **The Anvil** ever organically invented **The Stomp** over several successive plays in the stadium—and twenty-eight years before the VDU *Anchor* rivalry inspired Ryan's 2016 live ad-lib—this raw 1986 cassette cut is the very first of the three canonical versions of *"Ignition: The Forger's Call"* (followed by the 1986 Crucible Stadium Version played at CPI football games and the 2016 *Live at the Crucible* concert opener). Because the five college kids were tracking inside an unheated garage without a real foundry anvil yet, the demo features the mechanical click of Holly's 4-track tape deck, the band members vocalizing `(TING... BOOM!)` to test the idea of where the anvil strike and heavy kick-drum impact would go, Cassidy's raw analog polyphonic analog synth brass, Ryan's overdriven garage-rock guitar riff, and Cassidy, Evan, and Tyler shouting the gang-vocal `(IGNITION!)` response into a single shared Shure SM57 microphone.
